## 1. SubDocs: subcontractor and supplier document tracking

**Construction / facility services** · €49–199 / month · score **55/70** · competition: low

> "I only find out a subcontractor's insurance has expired when the client's auditor asks for it."

AI reads every insurance certificate, tax registration and safety card your subcontractors send, tracks the expiry dates and chases renewals for you. Flat price for the contractor, free for the subcontractors.

**Why now:** There is no single new deadline, but the penalties are real and ongoing. In Sweden a missing or wrong construction site staff register costs SEK 12,500 per occasion plus SEK 2,500 per undocumented person (SEK 25,000 for a repeat within a year), and the EU's NIS2 law pushes security and document requirements from large regulated companies down to their direct suppliers through contracts.

### The problem

**Anna is operations manager at a Swedish facility-services firm with 60 staff.** She uses about **80 subcontractors** for cleaning, security and property maintenance. Each one must have valid insurance, F-skatt tax status, ID06 cards and safety training. She tracks all of it in one Excel sheet and her inbox, and every month she spends hours emailing people for new certificates.

Then a large property client asks for **proof that every subcontractor on its sites is insured and registered** before it renews the contract. Anna loses **two full days** building the audit pack by hand, and finds three expired insurance certificates and one missing safety card. With SubDocs, the subcontractors would have got automatic reminders 30, 14 and 0 days before expiry, and the audit pack would be **one click**.

### Evidence

- Swedish site staff register (personalliggare) fines: SEK 12,500 per occasion plus SEK 2,500 per undocumented person, and SEK 25,000 for a repeat within a year [FAR Online / Skatteverket]
- ISNetworld costs a small subcontractor about $875 a year, including a $125 setup fee [Capterra]
- Avetta costs suppliers about $799+ a year plus $249 setup (figure published by a competitor) [Billy]
- Avetta reviews mention "significant supplier pushback… mainly due to membership fees, the administrative burden on suppliers… very slow customer support" [G2]
- US certificate tools are cheap but built around the US ACORD 25 form: CertCove $99/month Starter, SubDoc from $14/month, SmartCompliance $1,000/year [Capterra / GetApp / Software Advice]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** General contractors and facility-management or facility-services firms (cleaning, security, property maintenance) with 10–100 staff that use 10–200 subcontractors.
- **Buyer:** The owner, operations manager or health-and-safety manager: the person who chases documents and answers to auditors.
- **Start market:** Sweden and the UK first (document types differ by country), then other Nordic and EU markets.

### How it works

1. **Import the subcontractor list:** the firm uploads a CSV of its subcontractors and picks which documents each one needs.
2. **Subcontractors upload for free:** each subcontractor gets a magic-link upload page (no login) or forwards documents to an email address.
3. **AI reads every document:** OCR + LLM pull out the document type, company, policy number, expiry date and amounts; a person clicks "confirm".
4. **Green / amber / red dashboard:** one view per subcontractor shows what is valid, expiring or missing.
5. **Automatic chasing:** reminders go out at 30, 14 and 0 days, in the subcontractor's language.
6. **One-click audit pack:** a PDF per project or client proves every document, with a full audit log.

### MVP (first version)

- Magic-link upload page for subcontractors + email-in address
- OCR + LLM extraction (type, company, policy number, expiry, amounts) with a confirm step
- Green/amber/red dashboard per subcontractor
- Automatic reminders at 30, 14 and 0 days in the subcontractor's language
- CSV import of the subcontractor list
- One-click PDF audit pack per project or client
- Audit log, EU hosting and encryption

*Later:* Client audit portal (part of the €199 plan) · More countries' document types (Norway, Denmark, Germany) · Integrations with site-register and ID06 providers · Construction ERP integrations for larger firms · Swedish interface

### Pricing

- **Starter: €49/mo:** Up to 25 subcontractors; free for subcontractors who upload
- **Team: €99/mo:** Up to 100 subcontractors
- **Unlimited: €199/mo:** Unlimited subcontractors plus a client audit portal

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | Extraction costs a few cents per document (about $0.002 per document with a small model) | €5–20 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €20–60 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report: €200–300 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| ISNetworld, Avetta | Enterprise prequalification platforms | Charge each small subcontractor about $800–875+ a year; supplier pushback over fees and admin |
| CertCove, SubDoc, SmartCompliance | US certificate-of-insurance tracking | Built around the US ACORD 25 form; not localised for EU documents |
| Bokio Byggliggaren | Swedish site staff register | Only handles the site register, not insurance, tax or training documents |
| Excel + email | What most small firms do (an inference, not measured) | Manual chasing, no reminders, audit packs built by hand |
| **Us** | AI reads any document, tracks expiry and chases subcontractors automatically | Flat €49–199/mo, free for subcontractors, EU documents (F-skatt, ID06, A1, safety training) |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Days 1–3: Jonatan calls 20 Swedish and UK facility and construction firms; a Carrd page offers "send us your subcontractor list and we return a free compliance status report". Days 4–10: five concierge audits by hand (ChatGPT + spreadsheet), returning an expiry report and draft reminders. Days 11–14: ask for a €49/month pre-order or a letter of intent. Success = 3+ paid pre-orders.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the upload page, email-in, AI extraction with confirm step and the dashboard.
- **Week 6–8:** Reminders, CSV import, PDF audit pack, audit log and Stripe billing.
- **Week 9–10:** Move the concierge pilots onto the product; ask each for a referral to another firm.

### Risks

- **Document types differ by country:** Start with Sweden and the UK only and add countries one by one.
- **Some firms are happy with Excel:** Sell the audit pack and the time saved; target firms with 20+ subcontractors or a recent audit problem.
- **ID documents fall under GDPR:** EU hosting, encryption, a data processing agreement and clear deletion rules from day 1: a selling point that matches Victor's security skills.
- **Swedish customers may expect a Swedish interface:** Keep reminders multilingual from the start; add a Swedish UI once pre-orders prove demand.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many subcontractors or suppliers did you use last year, and how do you track their insurance and certificates today?
2. When did an expired document last cause a problem (fine, client audit, claim), and what did it cost?
3. Who spends time chasing documents, and how many hours a month?
4. What do your clients or main contractors ask you to prove, and how often?
5. What have you tried (Excel, Avetta, ID06 tools), and why did you stop or keep it?

### Scores

pain: 8 · willingnessToPay: 7 · lowCompetition: 7 · easyToBuild: 8 · easyToSell: 9 · skillFit: 9 · globalPotential: 7 · **total 55/70**

## 2. ExtraWork: change-order capture for small subcontractors

**Construction** · €39–199 / month · score **53/70** · competition: low

> "The site manager said 'just do it'. Three months later the main contractor called it original scope and refused to pay."

Foremen record extra work by voice note or photo; AI turns it into a priced change order that the client's site manager signs on their phone before anyone forgets.

**Why now:** A 2026 Clearstory and Dodge Construction Network report says 92% of subcontractors are not always paid in full for change orders and 66% wrote off change-order revenue in the last 12 months. Cheap speech-to-text and LLMs make a voice-first tool possible for small firms for the first time. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Mikael owns a 20-person electrical subcontracting firm in the UK.** On a typical job the main contractor's site manager asks his foreman to move six sockets and add a cable run. It is agreed by voice or on WhatsApp. The foreman means to write it down, but by Friday nobody remembers the hours or materials.

When Mikael invoices at the end of the project, the main contractor disputes the extras: there is **no signed paper**. He either writes the money off or accepts a big discount. Industry surveys say this is normal: **66% of subcontractors wrote off change-order revenue in the last year**. With ExtraWork, the foreman speaks a 20-second voice note, AI prices it from the rate card, and the site manager signs a link on his phone **the same day**.

### Evidence

- 92% of subcontractors are not always paid in full for change orders; only 6% say all are paid in full (vendor survey) [Clearstory]
- 66% of subcontractors wrote off change-order revenue in the last 12 months; change orders are discounted by an average of 22% (vendor survey) [Clearstory]
- "Up to 36% of T&M tags never make it from the field to the office" (vendor claim) [Clearstory blog]
- An older 2023 vendor survey estimated 32% of project revenue lost to unbilled or unpaid change orders (probably inflated) [Procore]
- Procore starts at roughly $4.5k–10k a year with a one-year minimum; Fieldwire is free for 5 users, then $39/$64/$89 per user per month [Projul / BuilderSoftwareGuide]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Specialist subcontractors with 5–50 staff: electrical, HVAC, drywall, painting, steel, scaffolding.
- **Buyer:** The owner or project manager. The daily users are the foremen.
- **Start market:** UK and Sweden (Jonatan's calls), then US and Australia, where the pain is higher than in Nordic markets with strong standard contracts.

### How it works

1. **Foreman records the extra:** a voice note or typed line plus photos in a phone web app: "moved 6 sockets per site manager, 2 men 4 hours + materials".
2. **AI structures it:** speech-to-text + LLM pull out labour hours, materials and the reason for the extra work.
3. **Priced from the rate card:** the company's own rates turn the tag into a priced change order.
4. **Client signs on the phone:** the site manager gets a signing link; the signature is stored with timestamp, location and a PDF.
5. **Status board and reminders:** draft, sent, signed, invoiced, disputed; unsigned tags trigger reminder emails.
6. **Monthly export:** a CSV for invoicing so nothing signed is forgotten.

### MVP (first version)

- Progressive web app with voice-to-text and photo capture
- AI extraction of labour hours, materials and reason
- Company rate card that prices each tag
- Client signing link with timestamp, location and PDF
- Status board: draft, sent, signed, invoiced, disputed
- Reminder emails for unsigned tags
- Monthly CSV export for invoicing

*Later:* Rates library (part of the €199 plan) · Accounting integrations (Fortnox, Xero, QuickBooks) · WhatsApp capture · Swedish and German interface · Dispute pack: all tags and photos for a project in one PDF

### Pricing

- **Crew: €39/mo:** Up to 3 field users
- **Team: €99/mo:** Up to 15 field users
- **Company: €199/mo:** Unlimited users plus a rates library

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | Speech-to-text + LLM per tag are cheap | €5–20 / month |
| SMS / email notifications | Signing links and reminders | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €20–50 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report: €200–300 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Clearstory | Change-order management for enterprise contractors | No public price; built for large firms |
| Procore | Full construction management platform | About $4.5k–10k a year with a one-year minimum |
| Fieldwire | Field management app | Free for 5 users, then $39–89 per user per month; not focused on signed extras |
| eSUB | Trade-contractor project management | A bigger system than a small subcontractor needs |
| Paper, WhatsApp, memory | What most small subcontractors use | No signature, no price, extras forgotten or disputed |
| **Us** | Voice note → priced change order → client signs on phone | WhatsApp-simple, under €100/mo for most small subcontractors |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Post in r/Construction, r/electricians and on LinkedIn; Jonatan calls 15 Swedish and UK subcontractors (same calls as SubDocs). Give them a free "digital T&M tag" made with Typeform and a PDF template, and check whether foremen use it on real jobs within a week. Ask 5 firms for a €39/month pre-order.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the phone app with voice and photo capture, AI extraction and the rate card.
- **Week 6–8:** Signing link, status board, reminders, CSV export and Stripe billing.
- **Week 9–10:** Pilot firms use it on live jobs; measure how many tags get signed and invoiced.

### Risks

- **The client's site manager may refuse to sign:** Ask this in every interview; the signed link is optional, and a timestamped, photo-backed record still helps in disputes.
- **Foremen may not use it:** Voice-first, no login, under 30 seconds per tag; test real usage with the Typeform version first.
- **Lower pain in the Nordics:** Strong standard contracts (AB04/ABT06) reduce disputes; push UK, US and Australia harder.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Roughly how much extra work did you do last year that you were never paid for?
2. How does a foreman record extra work today, and how does it reach the office?
3. How often does the main contractor dispute a variation, and on what grounds?
4. How long is it from doing the extra work to invoicing it?
5. Would your clients' site managers sign on a phone link? Why or why not?

### Scores

## 3. ChaseAI: client document collection and checking for small accounting firms

**Accounting** · €29–149 / month · score **52/70** · competition: high

> "I send three reminders, the client finally uploads something, and it's last year's bank statement."

Collects clients' tax and bookkeeping documents by email or WhatsApp, with no portal password, and checks with AI that each file is the right one for the right period.

**Why now:** Document collection became the number-one workflow challenge for accounting firms in 2025, named by 55% of about 816 firm owners. Firms are ready for AI: 98% of accounting firms now use AI, most of them daily.

### The problem

**Emma runs a 4-person bookkeeping firm in Manchester with 120 clients.** Every tax season she sends checklists by email. Clients forget their portal passwords, send blurry photos, or upload the wrong year. On average clients take **5 days** to send what she asks for, and many need several reminders.

In peak season her team spends hours a week just asking for and checking documents. A missing page is often found only when the return is half done. With ChaseAI, each client gets a **magic link or replies by email or WhatsApp**, and the AI immediately says "this is the 2024 statement, we need 2025" before Emma even opens the file.

### Evidence

- 55% of about 816 accounting firm owners named document collection their biggest workflow challenge in 2025 (secondary coverage) [CPA Practice Advisor]
- 98% of accounting firms now use AI, most of them daily [Karbon]
- Clients take on average 5 days to send requested documents [Financial Cents]
- Mango's 2025 survey of about 300 professionals ranks document collection as the top client-communication challenge [Mango]
- Automated chasing saves 3.2–3.5 hours per employee per week (vendor figure) [Karbon]
- About 10,000 document checks a month cost roughly $20 in AI fees at current small-model prices [AnotherWrapper]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Accounting and bookkeeping firms with 1–15 people.
- **Buyer:** The owner or practice manager.
- **Start market:** UK, Ireland, the Nordics and the EU; later US tax preparers.

### How it works

1. **Import clients and checklists:** the firm uploads its client list and picks a checklist template (UK Self Assessment, Swedish K10/INK1, Irish Form 11).
2. **No-login collection:** each client gets a magic-link upload page and their own inbound email address; WhatsApp in version 1.1.
3. **AI checks every file:** the LLM classifies each upload and validates document type, period and name match.
4. **Missing / incorrect list:** the client sees exactly what is still missing or wrong, in plain words.
5. **Escalating reminders:** email first, then WhatsApp, until the checklist is complete.
6. **Export and digest:** files go to Google Drive, Dropbox or OneDrive; the firm gets a weekly status digest.

### MVP (first version)

- Firm dashboard and client list (CSV import)
- Checklist builder with country templates
- Magic-link upload page and inbound email address per client
- LLM classification and validation of uploads (type, period, name match)
- Missing/incorrect list per client
- Escalating automatic email reminders
- Export to Google Drive, Dropbox or OneDrive + weekly digest

*Later:* WhatsApp reminders and replies (version 1.1) · White-label client pages (€149 plan) · More country checklists · Integrations with practice-management tools · US tax-preparer templates

### Pricing

- **Solo: €29/mo:** Up to 50 active clients
- **Practice: €79/mo:** Up to 200 active clients
- **Unlimited: €149/mo:** Unlimited clients plus white-label (or about €1 per active client per month)

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | About $20 per 10,000 document checks with a small model | €5–25 / month |
| WhatsApp business number | For WhatsApp reminders (included in the report's 3-month cash) | €5–20 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €30–100 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €550–800 (report: €250–350 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Content Snare | Client document request tool | About $29–99/month; chases but does not check what arrives |
| TaxDome | Full practice-management platform | From $695/year; a big system to adopt |
| Financial Cents | Practice management with client requests | From $19/user/month; no AI check of uploads |
| Dext | Receipt and invoice capture | About $31.50/user/month; receipts only |
| Indie AI clones (e.g. NudgeDocs) | AI document chasers | Many exist already; the market is crowded |
| **Us** | No-login collection + AI check of every file + country checklists | Priced per active client so tiny firms pay little (€29–149/mo) |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Jonatan contacts 100 UK and Swedish bookkeepers: "send us your client list and checklist; we will chase your clients and tell you what is missing, free for 2 weeks". Run it by hand with a shared inbox, Google Forms upload and ChatGPT to check files. Success = 3+ firms using it and at least one agreeing to pay €29+.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor reuses the document engine: client list, checklists, magic links, inbound email, AI validation.
- **Week 6–8:** Reminders, exports, weekly digest, Stripe billing; move pilot firms onto the product.
- **Week 9–10:** Add WhatsApp; push before the next tax deadline, when the pain peaks.

### Risks

- **Crowded market; Karbon or TaxDome can add AI checks:** Win on the narrow wedge: validation of each upload, no-login replies and UK/Nordic/Irish checklists.
- **Financial documents bring GDPR and security duties:** EU hosting, encryption and a data processing agreement; Victor's security skills are a selling point.
- **Demand is seasonal around tax deadlines:** Sell bookkeeping (monthly) as well as year-end; time outreach before deadlines.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Walk me through the last client whose documents arrived late: what happened, and how many reminders did it take?
2. How many hours per week do you or your staff spend asking for or checking documents in peak season?
3. What do you use today (portal, email, Content Snare, TaxDome), and what do clients hate about it?
4. How often do clients send the wrong or an incomplete document, and how do you notice?
5. If a tool cut chasing time in half, what would you pay per month, and who decides?

## 4. InsureReady: cyber-insurance evidence from Microsoft 365, sold through MSPs

**IT services / MSP** · $4 / tenant / month (min $49) · score **51/70** · competition: medium

> "The insurer doesn't want a yes anymore. They want proof that MFA is on for every admin, every mailbox and the backups."

Connects read-only to a company's Microsoft 365 and produces the evidence pack that cyber insurers now ask for. Sold mainly through small IT providers (MSPs), who resell it to their clients.

**Why now:** Insurers have moved from yes/no questions to asking where MFA is enforced, and partial MFA can count as no MFA. Business email compromise and payment fraud made up 58% of incidents in Coalition's 2026 claims report, and the UK's Cyber Essentials version 3.3 tightened MFA rules from 27 April 2026.

### The problem

**Tom runs a 6-person MSP in Leeds that looks after 35 small businesses' Microsoft 365.** Every few weeks a client's cyber-insurance renewal arrives with a long questionnaire: is MFA enforced for remote access, admin accounts and email? Are legacy logins blocked? Are backups tested? Tom's engineer clicks through each tenant's admin pages and takes screenshots for **hours per client**.

Insurers now check these answers, and claims can be refused if reality does not match the questionnaire. With InsureReady, Tom connects each tenant **read-only once**, and gets a **white-label evidence PDF** mapped to the insurance questions, plus a weekly "what changed" email. He resells it to every client as a renewal service.

### Evidence

- BEC and payment fraud made up 58% of incidents in Coalition's 2026 claims report, with an average payment-fraud loss of $112k [Coalition]
- BEC claims rose about 30% in the second half of 2025 [Risk & Insurance]
- UK Cyber Essentials v3.3 tightened MFA rules from 27 April 2026 [Computing]
- 19% of MSPs name "inability to quickly demonstrate value to clients" as a growth barrier (from a search summary) [MSP Success]
- Inforcer needs a 20-tenant minimum and 12-month terms; CoreView runs about $24.7k–49.5k a year (reseller listing) [Inforcer / ChannelOnline]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small MSPs (1–15 staff, 5–60 client Microsoft tenants); second, small businesses with 10–250 staff renewing cyber insurance.
- **Buyer:** The MSP owner or technical lead; for direct sales, the office manager or finance lead. Small insurance brokers act as referral partners.
- **Start market:** UK, Nordics, US, Australia and New Zealand, in English.

### How it works

1. **Connect read-only:** the MSP gives admin consent to a multi-tenant Microsoft Entra app with least-privilege, read-only permissions.
2. **Check the tenant:** MFA registration and enforcement per user and role, Conditional Access, legacy logins, number of global admins, mailbox forwarding rules, stale accounts, Secure Score.
3. **Check from the outside:** SPF/DKIM/DMARC, TLS certificates and exposed remote-desktop ports per domain.
4. **Add backup proof:** upload a backup restore-test record with a timestamp.
5. **Fill the questionnaire:** a standard 30-question insurance questionnaire is answered automatically, each answer linked to evidence.
6. **White-label PDF + weekly changes:** the MSP's logo on the pack, and a weekly "what changed" email.

### MVP (first version)

- Multi-tenant Microsoft Entra app with read-only consent
- MFA, Conditional Access, legacy auth, admin count, forwarding rules, stale accounts, Secure Score checks
- External checks: SPF/DKIM/DMARC, TLS, exposed RDP
- Backup restore-test upload with timestamp
- Auto-filled 30-question insurance questionnaire with evidence
- White-label PDF evidence pack
- Weekly "what changed" email

*Later:* Google Workspace connector · Insurer-specific form mappings · Pax8 marketplace listing · Broker referral portal · Modules shared with QBR Autopilot, Leaver and AI Use Guard

### Pricing

- **MSP per tenant: $4/tenant/mo:** Minimum $49/month; plus $29 one-off renewal report per tenant
- **MSP flat: $149/mo:** Up to 40 tenants
- **Direct business: $39–79/mo:** Or a $299 one-off renewal pack

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | Light use: questionnaire wording and summaries only | €5–15 / month |
| Microsoft test tenant | May need a paid trial (unverified); Graph API itself is free | €0–25 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €20–60 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €550–850 (report: €250–400 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Inforcer | Multi-tenant M365 management for MSPs | Priced per tenant with a 20-tenant minimum and 12-month terms |
| CoreView | Microsoft 365 management | About $24.7k–49.5k a year (reseller listing) |
| CIPP | Free open-source M365 multi-tenant tool | The MSP must host and maintain it; no insurance-mapped report |
| Intruder, UpGuard | External attack-surface scanning | Intruder from about $119/month, UpGuard Starter $1,599/month |
| Insurers' own scans | Outside-in scans during underwriting | Do not show internal M365 controls like MFA coverage |
| **Us** | Hosted, insurance-questionnaire-mapped evidence PDF per client | No tenant minimum, $4/tenant, white-label, plus outside-in checks per domain |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Publish a free "M365 cyber-insurance readiness checklist" on r/msp and LinkedIn and count downloads. Jonatan books 15 calls with MSP owners. Victor hand-produces 5 evidence packs for 2–3 MSPs' real client renewals with free tools (Microsoft admin exports, MXToolbox, Shodan). Ask for a $99 pre-order or $10 per client per month. Success = 3 MSPs commit with named clients.
- **Week 3–6:** Victor builds the Entra app, Graph checks and external checks.
- **Week 7–9:** Questionnaire mapping, white-label PDF, weekly change email, published security documentation.
- **Week 10:** Pilot MSPs run real renewals; collect testimonials for r/msp and MSPGeek.

### Risks

- **MSPs may not trust a young vendor with admin consent:** Least-privilege, read-only permissions; publish security documentation and a clear data policy.
- **Microsoft API changes; CIPP adds a similar free report:** Focus on the insurance mapping and the client-ready PDF, not raw data.
- **Each insurer uses a different form:** Start with one standard 30-question set and add insurer mappings as customers ask.
- **Liability if an answer in the pack is wrong:** Every answer links to evidence; the MSP reviews and signs off before sending.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Walk me through the last time a client renewed cyber insurance: who filled in the questionnaire and how long did it take?
2. How do you prove MFA coverage today?
3. Have you or a client had a claim questioned, a premium raised or cover refused because of a control gap?
4. What do you use for multi-tenant Microsoft 365 management, and what do you pay?
5. Who would pay for a client-ready evidence PDF (you, the client or the broker), and at what price per tenant would it be a no-brainer?

## 5. TenderPilot: public-tender search and bid drafting for small suppliers

**Public sector** · €79–199 / month · score **51/70** · competition: medium

> "We skip half the tenders we could win, because nobody has three days to read a 120-page PDF."

Finds public tenders that fit a small supplier, turns the long tender PDF into a requirements checklist, and drafts first answers from the company's past bids.

**Why now:** Tender data is public and free (EU TED, UK Find a Tender, Nordic portals; API terms still need checking), and AI that drafts answers with citations to the source is now good enough to save real hours. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Lena runs a 15-person cleaning and facility-services company in Ireland.** Public contracts for schools and offices would be steady work, but each tender is a long PDF with dozens of requirements, and each bid takes days of copying old answers, CVs and case studies from folders. So she only bids a few times a year.

AI tender tools exist, but they cost **$500–900 a month** and are built for big proposal teams. With TenderPilot, Lena gets alerts for cleaning tenders in her region, a **one-page requirements checklist** for each one, and **first-draft answers with links to her own past bids**. She bids twice as often in the same time.

### Evidence

- AI tender-response tools cost about $500–800/month for small teams [SiftHub]
- AutoRFP is listed at $899/month billed yearly (competitor figure) [Inventive]
- DeepRFP now explicitly targets small businesses and consultants, showing the small-bidder segment is real [Capterra]
- Proposify starts at $29/month but is a proposal tool, not a tender tool [Inventive]
- No survey of hours spent per bid was found; the pain evidence is indirect [Report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Companies with 2–50 staff that sell to the public sector: IT services, translation agencies, cleaning and facility services, training providers, consultancies, design and marketing agencies.
- **Buyer:** The founder, managing director or bid manager.
- **Start market:** English-language UK, Irish and Nordic tenders plus English notices on EU TED.

### How it works

1. **Tender alerts:** by CPV code (the EU's product/service code) and keyword from TED plus one national feed.
2. **Upload the tender:** the PDF becomes a compliance matrix (every requirement) and a deadline list.
3. **Build the answer library:** upload past bids, CVs and case studies once.
4. **AI first drafts:** each question gets a draft answer with citations to the library, so nothing is made up.
5. **Human review and export:** edit, approve and export to Word.

### MVP (first version)

- Tender alerts by CPV code and keyword (TED + one national feed)
- Tender document upload → compliance matrix and deadline list
- Answer library from uploaded past bids
- AI first draft per question with citations
- Word export
- Stripe billing

*Later:* More national portals · Bid/no-bid scoring · Partner workspace for freelance bid writers · Swedish, German and other languages · Award-notice analytics (who wins what, at what price)

### Pricing

- **Alerts: €79/mo:** Tender alerts and requirement extraction, 5 tenders a month
- **Bid: €199/mo:** Unlimited tenders, AI drafting and an answer library
- **Annual:** Discount for paying yearly (e.g. €500 for a year as a pre-order offer)

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | Long tender PDFs and drafting; still cents per tender | €10–30 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €20–80 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report: €200–300 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| AutoRFP, Inventive | AI RFP/tender response | About $500–900/month; built for large proposal teams |
| DeepRFP | AI RFP tool aimed at small businesses | Moving down-market; a real competitor to watch |
| Loopio | Enterprise RFP response | Enterprise pricing (price not found) |
| Tendium (Sweden) and national alert services | Tender alerts | Price not found; mainly search, not drafting |
| Proposify | Proposal software from $29/month | Proposals, not public-tender requirements |
| **Us** | Find tenders + requirements checklist + cited first drafts | €79–199/mo for firms that bid a few times a month |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Pull 200 recent award notices in 3 CPV codes. Email 100 winners and runners-up a free "tender digest + requirement checklist" for one live tender, made by hand with ChatGPT. Target 10 calls and 3 pre-orders (€79 for the first month or €500 for a year).
- **Week 3–6:** Victor builds alerts, tender upload and the compliance matrix.
- **Week 7–9:** Answer library, AI drafting with citations, Word export, billing.
- **Week 10:** Pre-order customers use it on live tenders; collect win/loss feedback.

### Risks

- **National portals differ:** Start with TED and one English-language national feed; add portals by demand.
- **Many languages:** Start with English-language UK, Irish and Nordic tenders plus English notices on TED.
- **Competitors like DeepRFP move down-market:** Win on price, alerts + checklist in one, and Jonatan's direct outreach to past bidders.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many tenders did you bid on last year, and how many did you skip for lack of time?
2. Walk me through the last bid: how many hours, and spent on what?
3. How do you find tenders today, and what do you pay for that?
4. Where do you store past answers, CVs and case studies?
5. What would a tool need to do for you to pay €100+ a month?

## 6. Questionnaire Autopilot: security and NIS2/DORA supplier answers plus a trust page

**Cybersecurity** · $49–299 / month · score **50/70** · competition: high

> "At fifty security questionnaires, it's a second job nobody signed up for."

Answers the security and NIS2/DORA questionnaires that big customers send to small suppliers, from one reusable answer library, and hosts a simple public trust page.

**Why now:** NIS2 had a national law in force in 22 of 27 EU member states by July 2026, and the Commission referred four countries to the EU Court of Justice on 8 July 2026. Article 21(2)(d) makes regulated firms manage their direct suppliers' security through contracts, and under DORA banks and insurers had to file registers of all their IT suppliers around March 2026.

### The problem

**Jakob is CTO of a 25-person software agency in the Netherlands.** Since NIS2 came into force, his bank and logistics customers send supplier security questionnaires with new clauses in their contracts. Each one is a different Excel file with 100+ questions, and he has no SOC 2 report to point to.

He spends days on each one, copying old answers and guessing. Full compliance platforms cost **$10k–30k a year**, far too much for his agency. With Questionnaire Autopilot, he builds a **supplier security profile once**, drops each new questionnaire in, gets drafted answers with sources and confidence scores, and sends customers a link to his **trust page**.

### Evidence

- NIS2 national law in force in 22 of 27 EU member states by July 2026; Ireland, Spain, France and the Netherlands referred to the EU Court of Justice on 8 July 2026 [Cullen International / Fieldfisher]
- The Netherlands reportedly gave about 8,000 organisations five weeks to prepare for NIS2 [ManageEngine]
- Third-party involvement in breaches doubled from 15% to 30% in the 2025 Verizon report [Security Today]
- Conveyor paid plans from about $9.6k/year; SafeBase about $5k–15k+/year; full platforms like Vanta about $10k–30k/year [G2 / Orbiq / UnderDefense]
- Sprinto is free for companies with 20 or fewer employees; ComplyJet starts at $5k/year [Sacra / ComplyJet]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small B2B suppliers with 5–100 staff: SaaS companies, agencies, IT service firms, MSPs, and logistics or facility-services suppliers to NIS2-regulated companies.
- **Buyer:** The founder, CTO, head of sales or managing director.
- **Start market:** Netherlands, Belgium, Germany and the Nordics in English; then worldwide for general security questionnaires.

### How it works

1. **Onboarding wizard:** which tools, policies and controls the company has.
2. **NIS2 self-assessment:** 40–60 questions mapped to the EU's implementing rules and ISO 27001, with starter policy templates for gaps.
3. **Answer library:** upload past questionnaires and policies once.
4. **Drop in a new questionnaire:** Excel or Word; AI drafts each answer with a confidence score and source citation.
5. **Review and export:** export back in the original format.
6. **Public trust page:** a simple page with NDA-protected documents to send to every customer.

### MVP (first version)

- Onboarding wizard (tools, policies, controls)
- 40–60 question NIS2 self-assessment mapped to implementing rules and ISO 27001
- Starter policy templates
- Answer library from past questionnaires and policies
- Excel/Word questionnaire import with AI drafts, confidence scores and citations
- Export in the original format
- Public trust page with NDA-protected documents

*Later:* DORA mappings and custom domain (€149 plan) · Agency/MSP workspace for 10 clients · German and Dutch language · External security scan on the trust page · CRA mappings (shared with CRA Reporting Desk)

### Pricing

- **Starter: $49/mo:** One trust page plus 5 questionnaires a month
- **Pro: $149/mo:** Unlimited questionnaires, NIS2/DORA mappings, custom domain
- **Agency/MSP: $299/mo:** 10 clients; plus a €299 one-off "NIS2 supplier readiness report"

| **Us** | Reusable supplier security profile + NIS2/DORA-mapped answers + trust page | Under $150/mo for suppliers without SOC 2 |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Jonatan offers "we fill in your next security questionnaire in 48 hours for €99" to 50 small SaaS firms and agencies, done by hand with an LLM and Victor's review. In parallel, a Typeform "NIS2 supplier readiness score" with a hand-made PDF report. Success = 3+ paid jobs, repeat requests, and 5 people willing to pay €299 for the report.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the answer library, questionnaire import and AI drafting.
- **Week 6–8:** NIS2 self-assessment, policy templates, export, trust page, billing.
- **Week 9–10:** Convert paid hand-done jobs into subscriptions; recruit SOC 2/ISO consultants and MSPs as partners.

### Risks

- **Heavy competition including free tiers:** Stay narrow: small suppliers without SOC 2, NIS2/DORA mapping, low monthly price.
- **A wrong AI answer can become a contract problem:** Mandatory human review; every answer shows its source and confidence.
- **Big compliance platforms bundle this:** Sell to firms that will never buy a $10k platform.
- **NIS2 rules vary by country; German markets may need German:** Start with English-speaking buyers in NL, BE and the Nordics; add languages later.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many security or vendor questionnaires did you receive in the last 12 months, and from whom?
2. Who answers them, and how many hours does each take?
3. Have you lost or delayed a deal because of a security review or missing SOC 2/ISO?
4. Has any customer cited NIS2, DORA or the CRA in a contract or questionnaire to you?
5. Would a reusable supplier security profile save you time, and what would it be worth per month?

## 7. CredTrack: staff certificate and right-to-work tracking for care and staffing agencies

**Healthcare / staffing** · $49–199 / month · score **50/70** · competition: medium

> "We had to stand a nurse down on the morning of a shift because her training certificate had expired and nobody noticed."

Staff upload certificates and right-to-work documents from their phone; AI reads the expiry dates and chases renewals, so the agency, clinic or care home is always ready for an inspection.

**Why now:** In 2026 several funded US companies added AI credential checks for staffing firms (KarmaCheck inside Bullhorn, Asurint with Bullhorn in July 2026, Firstwork). This shows the workflow has value, but they focus on US healthcare staffing on Bullhorn, leaving small UK and EU agencies. No regulatory deadline was found.

### The problem

**Priya is compliance manager at a UK care staffing agency with 120 workers.** Every worker needs valid right-to-work documents, DBS checks, training certificates and sometimes a driving licence. She keeps them in shared folders and a spreadsheet, and she chases workers by text message when something is about to expire.

Before an inspection she spends days checking every file by hand. One expired certificate can mean a worker cannot take a shift, or a client loses trust. With CredTrack, workers **upload a photo from their phone**, AI reads the **issuer and expiry date**, reminders go out automatically, and Priya can export an **inspection-ready pack per worker** in one click.

### Evidence

- KarmaCheck expanded its Bullhorn integration with AI credentialing automation in 2026 [HR Tech Edge]
- Asurint and Bullhorn launched a healthcare-staffing credential solution in July 2026 [Asurint]
- Firstwork offers credential and onboarding automation for staffing firms [BERI]
- Credential and expiry tracking is named a "boring but profitable" niche, with estimated (not reported) revenue potential of $7k–18k a month [Redwerk / MicroGaps]
- Evidence is weak: no UK small-agency survey was found, so this idea must be tested first [Report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Care homes, staffing agencies (care, nursing, education, drivers, warehouse temps), private clinics, nurseries and sports clubs with 5–200 workers.
- **Buyer:** The owner, compliance manager or office manager.
- **Start market:** UK, Ireland and the Nordics.

### How it works

1. **Requirement profiles:** the agency sets which documents each role needs (nurse, carer, driver).
2. **Phone upload:** each worker gets a magic link and uploads photos of documents; no app or login.
3. **AI reads the document:** OCR + AI extract name, issuer, issue date and expiry.
4. **Compliance matrix:** a green/amber/red grid of workers against required documents.
5. **Automatic reminders:** to workers and managers before anything expires.
6. **Inspection-ready export:** PDF and CSV pack per worker.

### MVP (first version)

- Requirement profiles per role
- Magic-link upload page for phone photos
- OCR + AI extraction of name, issuer, issue date and expiry
- Green/amber/red compliance matrix
- Reminders to workers and managers
- Inspection-ready PDF and CSV pack per worker

*Later:* Price per active worker (£2–4/worker/month) · Integrations with ATS / rostering tools · Country-specific credential templates (Ireland, Nordics) · Sports-club welfare officer edition · Client portal for agencies' customers

### Pricing

- **Small: $49/mo:** Up to 25 staff
- **Agency: $99/mo:** Up to 100 staff
- **Large: $199/mo:** Up to 250 staff (or £2–4 per active worker per month)

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | A few cents per document extracted | €5–20 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €30–80 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report: €200–300 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| KarmaCheck | AI credentialing inside Bullhorn | US healthcare staffing on Bullhorn |
| Asurint | Background and credential checks with Bullhorn | US focus; built for Bullhorn users |
| Firstwork, NextCrew | Staffing onboarding and credential automation | US agencies; heavier platforms |
| Generic HR tools and spreadsheets | What small UK/EU agencies use | No AI expiry reading, manual chasing (UK competitors not yet researched) |
| **Us** | Phone upload → AI reads expiry → automatic reminders → inspection pack | Dead simple for agencies without Bullhorn, $49–199/mo |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Send 100 cold emails to UK care homes and agencies and 50 to sports clubs. Do a concierge audit of one agency's worker files with a spreadsheet and ChatGPT extraction, and count expired or missing documents. Also check UK competitors. Target 3 paying pilots.
- **Week 3–5:** Re-skin the SubDocs engine: role profiles, phone upload, extraction.
- **Week 6–8:** Compliance matrix, reminders, inspection pack, billing.
- **Week 9–10:** Pilots go live; measure expired documents caught and hours saved.

### Risks

- **Right-to-work and criminal-record checks have legal rules:** The tool helps track documents; it does not certify. Say so clearly.
- **Sensitive personal data under GDPR:** UK/EU hosting, encryption, a data processing agreement and strict access control.
- **HR suites or ATS vendors may add the feature:** Target agencies too small for a big ATS; move fast with the shared engine.
- **Credential types differ by country:** Start with UK only; add Ireland and the Nordics later.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How do you know today whose certificates expire next month?
2. Have you ever failed an inspection or had to stand someone down because of an expired document?
3. Who chases workers, and how many hours a week does it take?
4. Which tool or ATS do you use now, and what does it cost?
5. What would losing a contract over compliance cost you, and who signs off on a €50–100 monthly purchase?

## 8. PriceGuard: supplier price-list sync with margin protection

**E-commerce** · $29–119 / month · score **50/70** · competition: low

> "A supplier raised prices in a PDF I didn't open for two weeks. We sold 40 units at a loss."

Reads messy supplier price lists (Excel, CSV, even PDF), updates the online store's prices automatically and warns before you sell anything below cost.

**Why now:** Several new tools launched in 2026 (Feedstead, FeedForge, Newcraft Feed Sync), a sign of live demand, while WooCommerce options remain limited.

### The problem

**Daniel runs a WooCommerce store for electrical supplies with 3,000 products and 12 suppliers.** Every supplier sends price changes in a different format: one Excel with changing column names, one CSV, one PDF. Updating the store takes him a full day each time, so he often falls behind.

When a supplier raises prices and he misses it, the store keeps selling at the old price, **sometimes below cost**. Existing plugins only handle clean files or one feed. With PriceGuard, he forwards the supplier email, AI maps the columns once, he sees a preview ("**12 products now sell below cost**"), and he clicks apply.

### Evidence

- Feedstead launched a Shopify supplier price review tool in 2026, alongside other new feed-sync tools [CFOtech]
- Store owners repeatedly ask how to update prices from supplier CSV or XML files [Shopify Community]
- FeedForge starts at $9/month but the entry plan is manual only, 1 supplier feed and 500 rows [Shopify App Store]
- EmberPrice is free but has no scheduling or stock updates; Sourcelyx is in beta and CSV-only [WordPress.org]
- Shopify takes 0% of the first $1M of app revenue [Shopify]
- No study measures the money lost to stale prices; that is a gap in the evidence [Report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** WooCommerce stores (later Shopify and Shopware) with 3–30 suppliers sending price lists: electronics, auto parts, building supplies, B2B distributors, dropshippers.
- **Buyer:** The owner or operations manager.
- **Start market:** Worldwide in English via WordPress.org and search; Jonatan's outreach to distributors and WooCommerce agencies.

### How it works

1. **Bring in the price file:** upload, forward by email, or fetch from a URL or FTP.
2. **AI maps the columns:** AI-assisted mapping of columns and product codes, saved as a template for next time.
3. **Apply your rules:** markup rules per supplier or category.
4. **Preview the changes:** prices up, down, below margin and missing products, before anything changes.
5. **Apply or roll back:** one click, with a full undo.
6. **Schedule and digest:** daily automatic runs and an email digest.

### MVP (first version)

- WordPress plugin + cloud backend
- Upload, email-in or URL fetch of price files
- AI-assisted column and product-code mapping saved as a template
- Markup rules
- Preview: up, down, below margin, missing products
- Apply and roll back
- Daily schedule + email digest

*Later:* PDF price lists · FTP pulls and stock updates · Shopify app (0% revenue share on the first $1M) · Shopware support · Agency plan for 10 stores

### Pricing

- **Basic: $29/mo:** Few suppliers and products
- **Growth: $59/mo:** More suppliers, daily schedule
- **Pro: $119/mo:** Many suppliers and products; agency plan $199 for 10 stores

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |
| AI API | Only for new file layouts; mapping is then saved | €0–10 / month |
| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €10–30 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report: €200–300 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| FeedForge | Shopify supplier feed app from $9/month | Entry plan is manual only, 1 feed, 500 rows |
| EmberPrice | Free WordPress plugin | No scheduling or stock updates |
| Sourcelyx | WordPress price import plugin | Beta and CSV-only |
| WP All Import | Do-it-yourself import plugin | No AI mapping for messy files, no margin alerts |
| Feedstead, Newcraft Feed Sync | New 2026 Shopify tools | Shopify-focused; WooCommerce left out |
| **Us** | AI mapping of messy files + "12 products now sell below cost" alerts + scheduled pulls | $29–119/mo, WooCommerce first |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Collect 10 real supplier files from 5 stores (WooCommerce Facebook groups, agency contacts); run them through an LLM mapping by hand; offer a done-for-you sync at $49/month. Target 3 pre-commitments.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the cloud backend, file intake and AI mapping templates.
- **Week 6–8:** WordPress plugin, preview, apply/roll back, schedule and digest; free plugin listing on WordPress.org.
- **Week 9–10:** SEO pages ("update WooCommerce prices from supplier Excel") and agency outreach.

### Risks

- **WooCommerce users are used to free plugins:** Free plugin as a lead magnet; charge for the cloud service, scheduling and margin alerts.
- **Messy-file edge cases eat development time:** Start with Excel/CSV; add PDF later; keep a human review step.
- **WP All Import already serves DIY users:** Target owners who want it automatic and checked for margin, not a DIY tool.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many suppliers send you price changes, and how often?
2. Walk me through the last price update: how much time, which tools?
3. Have you ever sold below cost because a supplier price changed, and what did it cost?
4. What have you tried (WP All Import, Zapier, a virtual assistant), and why did it fall short?
5. What would you pay monthly if updates were automatic and checked for margin?

## 9. QuoteChaser: instant replies and follow-up for B2B quotes and leads

**Sales / B2B e-commerce** · $39–149 / month · score **50/70** · competition: medium

> "We sent the quote, the buyer went quiet, and nobody followed up. We found out a month later they bought elsewhere."

Replies to new B2B leads within a minute and follows up open quotes with AI-written messages until the buyer says yes or no.

**Why now:** AI can now write a context-aware reply (reading the quote lines and the customer's history) for a fraction of a cent, while most small sales teams still answer slowly. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Sara runs a B2B Shopify store selling packaging to cafés and shops.** Her "request a quote" form brings in dozens of requests a month. She or her one sales rep replies when they have time, often the next day, and follow-ups happen only when someone remembers.

When she exports the last 90 days of quotes, a big share never converted and most got **no second message**. With QuoteChaser, each new request gets an AI-drafted reply **within 60 seconds** that reads the quote lines, and open quotes get polite follow-ups until the buyer answers. A weekly report shows the **money left on the table**.

### Evidence

- A 2025 study of 1,333 law firms found a 13-minute median response time, only 25% replying within 5 minutes, and 26% never replying [Focus Digital]
- Only about 3% of teams respond to new leads within 5 minutes [Neil Patel]
- The "100 times more likely to connect within 5 minutes" figure comes from an old MIT/InsideSales study (around 2007): a direction, not a fact [Verse.ai]
- B2B QuoteAutomator leads quote intake on Shopify with 4.9 stars from 658 reviews [Shopify App Store]
- SetuBridge's AI quote app starts at $7.99/month [eCommerce Guide]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** B2B and wholesale online stores with "request a quote" forms, and small sales teams (2–10 reps) on HubSpot free/Starter or Pipedrive.
- **Buyer:** The owner or head of sales.
- **Start market:** English-speaking B2B stores worldwide via the Shopify App Store and HubSpot App Marketplace.

### How it works

1. **Connect the source:** Shopify draft orders, a WooCommerce quote plugin or a HubSpot form webhook.
2. **Instant AI reply:** a reply that reads the quote lines and customer history, within 60 seconds; approve-first or automatic mode.
3. **Follow-up cadence:** polite follow-ups that stop as soon as the buyer replies.
4. **Reply sorting:** interested, price objection, not now.
5. **Weekly report:** "money left on the table": value of open and lost quotes.

### MVP (first version)

- Shopify draft orders connection
- WooCommerce quote plugin or HubSpot form webhook
- AI-drafted reply within 60 seconds (approve-first or automatic)
- Follow-up cadence that stops on reply
- Reply sorting (interested, price objection, not now)
- Weekly "money left on the table" report

*Later:* Pipedrive integration · HubSpot App Marketplace listing · SMS / WhatsApp follow-up · Partnerships with quote-form apps · Multi-language replies

### Pricing

- **Starter: $39/mo:** Small number of quotes and leads
- **Growth: $79/mo:** More quotes and leads
- **Pro: $149/mo:** High volume (or $99/month per sales team)

### Costs

## 10. AgencyPulse: white-label website health, security and accessibility reports for web agencies

**Web agencies** · $29–199 / month · score **50/70** · competition: medium

> "A client's SSL certificate expired on a Saturday and they found out from their customers, not from us."

One monthly report with the agency's logo on every client website (uptime, security, expiry dates, speed and EU accessibility) that web agencies resell to clients as a "care plan".

**Why now:** The European Accessibility Act has been enforceable since 28 June 2025, and its second year of enforcement began on 28 June 2026. Sweden's regulator PTS opened its first e-commerce cases, German shops received warning letters, and a July 2026 article says most businesses are "still catching up".

### The problem

**Marco runs a 5-person web agency in Berlin that maintains 60 client sites** on WordPress, Webflow, Shopify and a few Next.js builds. He sells "care plans", but reporting to clients means logging into different tools for each stack and pasting screenshots into a monthly email.

Since the **European Accessibility Act** became enforceable, shop clients ask whether their checkout is compliant; German shops have already received warning letters. With AgencyPulse, every site gets one **outside-in report with Marco's logo**: uptime, SSL and domain expiry, email security, exposed files, speed, and honest accessibility checks on the checkout, plus AI fix suggestions.

### Evidence

- The EAA has been enforceable since 28 June 2025; Sweden's PTS opened its first e-commerce cases and German shops received warning letters [Disability World / TestParty]
- Most businesses are "still catching up" one year after the EAA (July 2026) [KEYT/Stacker]
- WP Umbrella charges €1.99 per site per month for white-label WordPress maintenance reports [WP Umbrella]
- ManageWP has a free core with paid per-site add-ons such as +$2 for backups and +$1 for uptime [Gautam Khorana]
- 62% of second-year EAA sanctions targeted checkout flows (single editorial source, unverified) [Disability World]
- Very small firms (under 10 staff and up to €2M turnover) are exempt for services, which shrinks the EU market [fudge.ai]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Web agencies and freelancers that maintain 10–300 client sites on WordPress, Webflow, Shopify, Next.js or static hosting.
- **Buyer:** The agency owner.
- **Start market:** EU agencies (Sweden and Germany for the EAA hook), then worldwide in English.

### How it works

1. **Add sites:** paste a list or upload a CSV of client sites.
2. **Scheduled outside-in checks:** uptime, SSL/domain/DNS expiry, SPF/DKIM/DMARC, security headers, exposed files (.git, .env), outdated JavaScript libraries and CMS versions, Core Web Vitals, broken links.
3. **Accessibility tests:** axe-core with Playwright on key pages, including a scripted checkout.
4. **AI fix suggestions:** plain-English fixes, alt-text drafts and an accessibility statement generator.
5. **Alerts:** instant alerts for downtime and expiries.
6. **White-label report:** monthly PDF and web report with the agency's logo.

### MVP (first version)

- Add sites by CSV
- Uptime, SSL/domain/DNS expiry and email-security checks
- Security headers, exposed files, outdated libraries and CMS versions
- Core Web Vitals (PageSpeed API) and broken links
- axe-core + Playwright accessibility tests incl. scripted checkout
- AI fix suggestions and alt-text drafts
- White-label PDF and web report + alerts

*Later:* Accessibility statement generator · Free "site health grade" lead-magnet page · Client-facing dashboard · Plugin-based deeper checks for WordPress · Shopify Partners listing

### Pricing

- **Per site: $1.50/site/mo:** Minimum $29/month
- **Bundles: $49 / $99 / $199 per month:** 25 / 75 / 250 sites
- **Accessibility module:** $29 per store or $149 for 10 stores

| **Us** | One outside-in report for any stack: health, security and honest accessibility checks | White-label, $1.50/site, no "overlay" promises |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Produce reports by hand with free tools (PageSpeed Insights, SSL Labs, MXToolbox, the axe browser extension) for the checkouts of 30 Swedish and German shops and for 3 client sites each of 50 agencies. Target 5 agencies asking for a bulk version and 2 pre-paying.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the scanner: uptime, expiry, email security, headers, exposed files, speed.
- **Week 6–8:** Accessibility tests, AI fix suggestions, white-label PDF, alerts, billing.
- **Week 9–10:** Free "site health grade" page; Jonatan emails agencies a free report on three of their client sites.

### Risks

- **Low barrier to entry; many cheap monitors:** Bundle security + accessibility + white-label in one report; that is the difference.
- **Price-sensitive agencies:** Per-site pricing that the agency marks up in its care plan.
- **Outside-in scanning is shallower than plugin tools:** Be clear about scope; add plugins later for WordPress.
- **Automated accessibility tests find only part of the problems:** Never promise full compliance; position as monitoring and fix guidance.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many client sites do you maintain, and on which platforms?
2. Do you sell care or maintenance plans, and what do you include and charge?
3. How do you report to clients today?
4. Has a client asked about the European Accessibility Act, or had a site hacked or a domain or SSL certificate expire?
5. What would you pay per site for automated white-label reports?

## 11. OrderDesk: email, PDF and WhatsApp orders turned into draft orders for small wholesalers

**Wholesale / distribution** · €99–249 / month · score **49/70** · competition: medium

> "Two people in my office do nothing all morning but type orders from emails and WhatsApp into Fortnox."

Turns order and quote-request emails, PDFs and WhatsApp messages into draft orders matched to the wholesaler's own product list, ready to approve.

**Why now:** AI order entry is moving into distribution, but for big companies: Proton.ai launched AI order and quote entry in July 2026, and trade press wrote in September 2026 that "AI moves into the distributor order desk". Small distributors on small accounting systems are left out.

### The problem

**Erik owns a building-materials wholesaler in Sweden with 12 staff.** Customers order by email, PDF purchase orders and WhatsApp messages like "same as last time but 20 more of the grey ones". Two office staff type every order into the accounting system by hand, matching vague product names to codes.

Mistakes cost money: wrong products shipped, wrong prices invoiced. AI order-entry tools exist, but they are built for big distributors on Epicor, NetSuite or SAP. With OrderDesk, every order goes to one inbound address, AI pulls out **customer, lines, quantities and dates**, matches them to Erik's **product list**, and his staff just **review and approve** before it goes to Fortnox.

### Evidence

- More than 30% of wholesale orders arrive through unstructured channels and manual entry takes about 6 minutes per order (vendor figures) [Workd]
- A distributor with 200 orders a day spends about $130k a year on manual order entry (vendor figure) [Workd]
- Proton.ai launched AI order and quote entry for distributors in July 2026 [Distribution Strategy]
- "AI moves into the distributor order desk as new tools target manual work" (September 2026) [Distribution Strategy]
- Nexcade's reported $6M seed round for AI freight-forwarder agents confirms quote-request emails are a real pain [The Loadstar]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Wholesalers and distributors with 3–50 staff (food, building materials, industrial supplies, farm inputs) that receive 20–200 orders a day by email, PDF or WhatsApp. Later: small road forwarders and CNC job shops with quote requests.
- **Buyer:** The owner or sales-operations lead.
- **Start market:** Sweden (Fortnox first) and Romania/Moldova, then the UK and other Nordic/CEE countries.

### How it works

1. **One inbound address:** customers keep ordering the way they do; orders are forwarded to a dedicated email address.
2. **AI extracts the order:** customer, order lines, quantities and dates from emails, PDFs and messages.
3. **Match to the product list:** fuzzy matching of product names to the wholesaler's uploaded product CSV.
4. **Review screen:** staff check and fix the draft order in seconds.
5. **Export:** to CSV or one accounting API (Fortnox first).
6. **Confirm to the customer:** an automatic confirmation email goes back.

### MVP (first version)

- Dedicated inbound email address
- AI extraction of customer, lines, quantities and dates
- Fuzzy matching of product names to an uploaded product CSV
- Review screen
- Export to CSV or the Fortnox API
- Confirmation email back to the customer

*Later:* WhatsApp intake · Visma, Odoo and SmartBill integrations · Learning from corrections (customer-specific product aliases) · Quote-request mode for road forwarders and job shops · Accounting-system app marketplace listings

### Pricing

- **Standard: €99/mo:** Up to 500 orders a month
- **Pro: €249/mo:** Up to 2,000 orders a month

|---|---|---|

| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €550–800 (report: €250–350 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Proton.ai | AI order and quote entry for distributors (July 2026) | Built for larger distributors |
| Y Meadows, WizCommerce, Moblico, Advantive, Tungsten | AI order entry | Target mid-market and enterprise ERPs (Epicor, P21, NetSuite, SAP) |
| Paperless Parts | Quoting for job shops | Quote-priced and costly for small shops |
| QuotedPart | Smaller job-shop quoting option | Job shops only, not wholesale orders |
| Customer portals / EDI | Structured ordering | Small customers don't adopt them |
| **Us** | Emails, PDFs and WhatsApps → matched draft orders → approve → Fortnox/CSV | Self-serve, €99–249/mo, for small-business accounting systems |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Find 10 Swedish or Romanian wholesalers; ask for 50 real past order emails; run them through an LLM by hand; show the accuracy and time saved. Target 2 letters of intent.
- **Week 3–6:** Victor builds inbound email, AI extraction, product matching and the review screen.
- **Week 7–10:** CSV and Fortnox export, confirmation emails, billing; tune accuracy on real orders.
- **Week 11–12:** Pilots run live order volume; add WhatsApp if customers ask.

### Risks

- **Integrations with accounting and ERP systems eat development time:** One integration only (Fortnox) plus CSV export at first.
- **Customers expect very high accuracy:** Always a review screen; measure accuracy on their own past orders before selling.
- **Mid-market vendors may move down-market:** Win on self-serve setup, price and small local systems.
- **The freight segment is heavily funded:** Keep freight and job-shop quotes as a later segment.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many orders arrive per day, and through which channels?
2. Who types them in, and how long does each take?
3. How often are there mistakes, and what does one cost?
4. Which accounting or ERP system do you use?
5. Have you tried a customer portal or EDI, and why did customers not adopt it?

## 12. QuoteDesk: request-to-quote assistant for small translation agencies

**Translation services** · €39–199 / month · score **49/70** · competition: low

> "By the time I've opened the PDFs, counted the words and priced it, the client has already accepted someone else's quote."

Turns a translation request email and its attachments into a word count, a price from the agency's rate card and a draft reply in minutes, working next to Gmail or Outlook.

**Why now:** The professional systems for translation agencies are priced for large firms: XTRF Professional costs $9,000/year and Ultimate $34,000/year, and Plunet uses custom quotes that small shops "rarely qualify for". On G2, Plunet scores only 6.7 for AI text generation. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Ioana runs a 6-person translation agency in Bucharest.** Every day requests arrive by email: a scanned contract to translate into German, a website in three languages, a certified birth certificate. For each one she opens the attachments, runs a word count (OCR for scans), checks the language pair and deadline, prices it from her rate sheet and writes a reply.

Big agency systems like XTRF cost **$9,000+ a year**, far too much for her. With QuoteDesk, she forwards the email, and within minutes gets the **word count, a price from her rate card and a draft reply**, plus suggested freelancers for the language pair. She stays in Gmail.

### Evidence

- XTRF Professional costs $9,000/year and Ultimate $34,000/year (vendor page, May 2026) [XTM]
- Plunet uses custom quotes that small shops "rarely qualify for" [FitGap]
- Plunet scores only 6.7 for AI text generation on G2 [G2]
- Projetex costs €2,900 one-time; Protemos is a cheap alternative [Report / MakerStack]
- Evidence is indirect only: no time study of quoting at small agencies was found [Report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Translation and localisation agencies with 2–20 people.
- **Buyer:** The owner or a project manager.
- **Start market:** Small agencies in Eastern Europe (Romania, Moldova) as a beachhead, then the EU, UK and US.

### How it works

1. **Connect the inbox:** Gmail or Outlook connection, or a forwarding address.
2. **Read the attachments:** DOCX and PDF (with OCR) are read and word-counted.
3. **Extract the job:** AI pulls out language pairs, subject, certified or not, and deadline.
4. **Price it:** a rate-card engine prices the job and makes a quote PDF.
5. **Draft the reply:** one click to a ready reply in the agency's tone.
6. **Plan the work:** a simple job board, freelancer roster with language pairs and rates, and a deadline calendar.

### MVP (first version)

- Gmail/Outlook connection or forwarding address
- DOCX and PDF reading with OCR and word counts
- AI extraction of job details (languages, subject, certified, deadline)
- Rate-card engine and quote PDF
- One-click draft reply
- Simple job board, freelancer roster and deadline calendar

*Later:* Freelancer matching (€199 plan) · Win/loss tracking by turnaround time · Invoicing export · CAT-tool analysis import (fuzzy-match discounts) · Client portal for repeat customers

## 13. CRA Reporting Desk: Cyber Resilience Act reporting and SBOM for small product makers

**Cybersecurity** · €79–199 / month · score **48/70** · competition: low

> "If a vulnerability in our device were exploited tomorrow, I honestly don't know who would file the report, or where."

Helps small device and software makers meet the EU Cyber Resilience Act's 24-hour vulnerability-reporting clock, with ready report drafts and an up-to-date list of the software parts in each product (SBOM).

**Why now:** This is the strongest legal deadline in the report. Since 11 September 2026, every maker of a product with digital elements sold in the EU must report actively exploited vulnerabilities and severe incidents through ENISA's Single Reporting Platform: a 24-hour early warning, a 72-hour notification, then a final report. Full obligations, including a machine-readable SBOM, follow on 11 December 2027.

### The problem

**Lukas is CTO of a 30-person smart-home device maker in Austria.** Since 11 September 2026, the Cyber Resilience Act says that if a vulnerability in one of his products is actively exploited, he must send an **early warning within 24 hours** and a full notification within **72 hours** through ENISA's reporting platform. He has no security response team and no list of all the open-source parts in his firmware.

Enterprise tools like Black Duck are far beyond his budget, and consultants are slow. With CRA Reporting Desk, his team imports an **SBOM for each product**, gets alerts when a component is **actively exploited**, and when it happens a **countdown clock** starts with AI-drafted report text in ENISA's field structure.

### Evidence

- Since 11 September 2026: 24-hour early warning, 72-hour notification and a final report for actively exploited vulnerabilities, via ENISA's Single Reporting Platform [Noerr / usd.de]
- CRA fines reach €15M or 2.5% of global turnover (secondary source) [advisori]
- Full CRA obligations, including a machine-readable SBOM, apply from 11 December 2027 [SafeDep]
- ENISA's 2026 SME survey (194 organisations, 31 countries): 66% had heard of the CRA, but incident-response and lifecycle maturity was only 2.6 out of 5 [ENISA]
- 73% of surveyed SMEs asked for documentation templates and 71% for secure-development templates [ENISA]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small and medium companies (5–250 staff) that sell connected or software products in the EU: IoT and embedded device makers, smart-home and industrial gadget firms, desktop and mobile software vendors, including non-EU makers selling into the EU.
- **Buyer:** The CTO, head of engineering or founder. CRA consultants could resell it.
- **Start market:** EU product makers plus non-EU makers selling into the EU, in English.

### How it works

1. **Inventory products:** list products and their components.
2. **SBOM import or generation:** wrapping the open-source Syft/CycloneDX tools, plus a GitHub Action.
3. **Vulnerability matching:** through OSV, with an "actively exploited" flag from the US CISA KEV list.
4. **Intake and disclosure:** a vulnerability intake form plus a security.txt and disclosure-policy generator.
5. **Countdown and drafts:** a 24h / 72h / final-report clock with AI-drafted text in ENISA's field structure.
6. **Audit log and notifications:** every step logged; customer-notification templates ready.

### MVP (first version)

- Product and component inventory
- SBOM import or generation (Syft/CycloneDX) + GitHub Action
- Vulnerability matching via OSV with CISA KEV "actively exploited" flag
- Vulnerability intake form, security.txt and disclosure-policy generator
- 24h / 72h / final-report countdown with AI drafts in ENISA's field structure
- Audit log and customer-notification templates

*Later:* €990 CRA readiness pack (documentation skeleton) · SBOM monitoring for up to 10 products · Consultant/reseller workspace · Firmware binary SBOM analysis · Mappings shared with Questionnaire Autopilot (NIS2)

### Pricing

- **Product: €79/mo:** One product family
- **Portfolio: €199/mo:** Up to 10 products with SBOM monitoring
- **CRA readiness pack: €990 one-off:** Documentation skeleton and vulnerability-disclosure policy

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server, database and email (basic stack) | Small EU cloud server or Vercel/Supabase free tiers + transactional email (report estimate) | €25–45 / month |

| Landing page and validation tools | Carrd / Typeform / Google Forms for the no-code test | €0–20 / month |
| Running cost with 20–50 customers | Report estimate, not a price quote | €30–80 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report: €200–300 product costs + €300–450 AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Black Duck, Revenera | Enterprise software-composition analysis | Enterprise pricing and setup |
| CRA consultancies | Readiness projects | Expensive and slow; not a daily workflow tool |
| OpenChain CRA checklist | Free checklist | A checklist, not a reporting workflow or clock |
| **Us** | Self-serve reporting runbook + countdown clock + AI drafts + SBOM | €79–199/mo for teams with no security response team |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Publish a landing page ("Meet the CRA's 24-hour reporting deadline without a security team") with a free downloadable "CRA reporting runbook" PDF. Post in embedded/IoT communities and on LinkedIn, and cold-email 100 small IoT makers. Success = 20+ email sign-ups and 5+ booked calls.
- **Week 3–6:** Victor builds the inventory, SBOM import/generation and OSV/KEV matching.
- **Week 7–9:** Intake form, security.txt generator, countdown clock with AI drafts, audit log.
- **Week 10:** First paying product makers; approach CRA consultants as resellers.

### Risks

- **Technical buyers are hard for Jonatan to sell to:** Jonatan books the calls; Victor joins them. Use CRA consultants as resellers.
- **Legal liability if a draft is wrong:** Position it as a workflow tool, not legal advice; a human always submits.
- **Big security vendors could add the same features:** Stay SMB-priced and self-serve; move fast while the deadline is new.
- **ENISA's form may change; market limited to product makers:** Keep field mappings configurable; target the many small IoT and software makers.

### Questions to ask customers

1. If a vulnerability in your product were exploited tomorrow, who would file the CRA report, and how?
2. Do you have an SBOM for each product today, and how do you make it?
3. How do you find out today that a component you ship is vulnerable or actively exploited?
4. What have you spent or budgeted on CRA readiness (consultants, tools, staff time)?
5. If a tool handled the deadline clock, report drafts and SBOM, what would it need to do for you to pay €100–200 a month?
