## 14. VisaKit: document kit for immigration advisers outside the US

**Legal / immigration services** · €49–149 / month · score **48/70** · competition: low

> "Every client sends me photos of passports and bank statements on WhatsApp, and half of them are expired or not translated."

Case checklists, client document collection in the client's own language, and passport-data extraction for small immigration consultancies in the UK, Canada and the EU. Funded immigration software is built for the US system, so advisers elsewhere use generic tools.

**Why now:** Funded immigration software (LegistAI, eazyPetition, ParaLeagle) is built for the US USCIS system, so advisers in the UK, Canada and the EU are left with generic tools. AI can now read passports and ID documents, including the machine-readable zone, cheaply. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Elena runs a three-person agency in Chișinău** that helps Moldovans get Romanian citizenship. At any time she has **dozens of active files**. Each one needs birth certificates, old Soviet-era documents, translations and apostilles. Clients send everything by **WhatsApp photo**, often blurry, sometimes the wrong document.

Her assistant spends whole afternoons checking which papers are missing, which translation is not certified, and which passport will expire before the appointment. Then she **types the same passport data by hand** into government forms. When one file is sent back because a document was missing, the client blames the agency, and the case loses months.

### Evidence

- eazyPetition offers US petitions at $100 flat per case, showing funded tools focus on the US system [Sherbrooke Record]
- LegistAI tracks USCIS (US) cases, not UK, Canadian or EU case types [Software Advice]
- Prolexis Essentials costs $79/month for 1 user and 25 active cases [Capterra UK]
- Visalaw.ai Pro costs $160–180/month (2025 pricing) [Visalaw.ai]
- Pain evidence is indirect: document collection is the top pain in similar professional services; no survey of immigration consultants was found [Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small immigration consultancies and solo advisers: UK advisers regulated by the OISC/IAA, Canadian RCICs, and EU work-permit, relocation and citizenship agencies.
- **Buyer:** The owner of the consultancy.
- **Start market:** Romanian-citizenship agencies serving Moldovans (English and Romanian), then UK and Canadian advisers found through public registers.

### How it works

1. **Pick a case type:** the adviser chooses a template, for example UK Skilled Worker, Canada Express Entry or Romanian citizenship under Article 11, and edits the checklist.
2. **Send the client a link:** the client gets an upload link in English, Romanian, Russian or Ukrainian. No login, works on a phone.
3. **AI reads the documents:** it recognises each document, pulls passport and ID data from the machine-readable zone and checks expiry dates.
4. **Warnings:** expired, missing or untranslated documents are flagged, so the adviser sees at a glance what is still needed.
5. **Timeline and reminders:** each case has deadlines and automatic reminders to the client.
6. **Export:** a JSON, CSV or PDF summary the adviser can copy into government portals instead of re-typing.

### MVP (first version)

- Case types with editable requirement checklists
- Client upload links in English, Romanian, Russian and Ukrainian
- AI extraction of passport and ID data, including the machine-readable zone
- Expiry checks and warnings for expired or untranslated documents
- Case timeline and deadline reminders
- Data export (JSON, CSV or PDF summary) for government portals

*Later:* WhatsApp intake of documents · Translation and apostille tracking with translator hand-off · More case-type templates per country · Team members and client status page · Invoicing per case

### Pricing

- **Solo: €49/mo:** Up to about 10 active cases, 1 user
- **Agency: €149/mo:** More active cases, team members, all case templates
- **Per case: €5–10 / active case / month:** Alternative for agencies with changing volume

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| AI + OCR API | Document and passport reading | €10–40 / month |
| Encrypted file storage | Very sensitive personal data | €5–15 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark free or low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | No-code builder or built by us | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report estimate €200–300 for hosting, AI and validation, plus €300–450 for AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Prolexis | Immigration case management, Essentials $79/month (1 user, 25 active cases) | Not focused on multilingual client intake or non-US document checks |
| Visalaw.ai | AI for immigration lawyers, Pro $160–180/month (2025) | Built around the US system |
| Docketwise, LegistAI, ParaLeagle | US immigration case software | USCIS-centric; price per user (Docketwise figures not found) |
| Google Drive + WhatsApp | What most small advisers use today | No checklists, no expiry checks, lots of re-typing |
| **Us** | Non-US case checklists, multilingual upload links, passport extraction | €49–149/month, built for UK, Canada and EU files |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Interview 10 consultancies. Run 2 live cases by hand with Google Drive and a ChatGPT checklist. Measure hours saved and willingness to pay.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds case types, checklists, multilingual upload links and passport/ID extraction (reusing the document engine from SubDocs/ChaseAI if built).
- **Week 6–7:** Warnings, reminders, timeline and export; security basics (encryption, EU hosting, data processing agreement).
- **Week 8:** Move the 2 hand-run pilot firms onto the product and ask for payment.

### Risks

- **Seen as giving immigration advice:** The tool only organises documents for licensed advisers. It never tells a client what to apply for.
- **Requirements change often:** Checklists are editable by the adviser; we provide templates, not legal truth.
- **Extremely sensitive personal data:** EU hosting, encryption, short retention, a data processing agreement and Victor's security skills shown on a public security page.
- **Fragmented market, harder lead lists:** Use public adviser registers (OISC, RCIC), Google Maps searches and Victor's local contacts.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many active cases do you handle, and what share of your team's time goes to collecting and checking documents?
2. What are the most common reasons a file is delayed or refused because of paperwork?
3. How do clients send you documents today (WhatsApp, email, Drive)?
4. Which government portals or forms do you re-type data into, and how long does that take per case?
5. What software have you tried, and why did you stop, or why have you never bought any?

### Scores

pain: 8 · willingnessToPay: 7 · lowCompetition: 7 · easyToBuild: 7 · easyToSell: 5 · skillFit: 8 · globalPotential: 6 · **total 48/70**

## 15. CleanProof: quality inspections and client reports for cleaning companies

**Facility services** · €59–149 / month · score **48/70** · competition: medium

> "The client says the office is dirty. I know my team was there every night, but I have nothing to show them."

Supervisors inspect cleaning with phone photos and scores, and AI writes the monthly quality report the client actually reads. Built for cleaning firms with 5–60 sites that find bigger systems too heavy.

**Why now:** Bigger systems are too heavy for mid-sized cleaners: Aspire is aimed at mid-size to enterprise firms with a claimed 6–12 month setup for 100+ sites (a competitor's claim). AI can now turn photos and scores into a readable report automatically. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Anders owns a cleaning company in Gothenburg with 40 staff and 25 office sites.** His area manager walks through sites with a paper checklist and takes photos on her own phone. The photos stay on her phone.

When a facility manager complains, Anders has **no clear record** of what was checked and when. At contract renewal, a cheaper competitor shows up with a nice-looking report, and Anders has only a spreadsheet. He does not want a big system that takes months to set up; he wants **proof of quality, sent to the client every month**, without extra office work.

### Evidence

- Swept starts at about $77/month by number of locations (unverified), with 4.3 stars from 79 reviews [Capterra]
- SafetyCulture Premium costs $24 per user per month for general inspections [SafetyCulture]
- Aspire is aimed at mid-size to enterprise firms with a claimed 6–12 month setup for 100+ sites (claim from competitor SweepOps) [SweepOps]
- Pain evidence is weak: lost contracts from missing proof of quality is an inference from Jonatan's industry, not a measured fact [Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Commercial cleaning and facility-services companies with 10–100 staff and 5–60 sites.
- **Buyer:** The owner or area manager.
- **Start market:** The Nordics through Jonatan's network, then other English-speaking markets.

### How it works

1. **Build a checklist:** pick a template (office, restroom, kitchen) and adjust it per site.
2. **Inspect on the phone:** the supervisor walks the site, gives scores and takes photos in a mobile web app.
3. **QR feedback:** each site gets a "rate this restroom" QR code so building users can report problems.
4. **Issue tickets:** low scores and complaints become tasks assigned to a cleaner.
5. **AI monthly report:** AI turns scores, photos and fixed issues into a short PDF/email report per client.
6. **Client portal:** the client can see inspections and reports in a read-only view.

### MVP (first version)

- Mobile inspections with photos and scores
- QR feedback page per site
- Issue tickets assigned to cleaners
- AI-written monthly PDF/email report per client
- Read-only client portal

## 16. AI Use Guard: shadow-AI visibility and AI-policy records for small firms

**Cybersecurity** · $2–4 / user / month · score **47/70** · competition: medium

> "I have no idea which AI tools my staff use, or what client data they paste into them."

Shows which AI tools a company's staff use, warns before someone pastes client data into them, and records AI-policy and training sign-offs. Sold per user, mainly through MSPs.

**Why now:** The EU AI Act's Article 4 "AI literacy" duty has applied since 2 February 2025. The Digital Omnibus, in force since 27 July 2026, softened it to "take measures to support AI literacy", so it is a duty of effort: a conversation opener, not a hard mandate. Meanwhile staff adopt AI faster than firms can control it.

### The problem

**Maria is managing partner of a 35-person accounting firm in Stockholm.** Her staff use ChatGPT, Copilot and a few AI note-takers. Nobody approved them, and nobody knows what client data goes in. Last month a large client asked in a supplier questionnaire: **"How do you control the use of AI tools with our data?"** Maria had no answer.

Her IT provider (an MSP) cannot help either: it has **no list of AI tools** in her company. Big-company AI security products are quote-only and made for enterprises. Maria wants a simple view of what is used, a warning before client data is pasted, and a record that everyone signed the AI policy.

### Evidence

- A Pax8 survey of 1,150 SMB leaders (US, UK, Germany, Sweden) found shadow AI in 35% of AI-using firms, and nearly half got a bigger AI bill than expected [The Next Web]
- 81% of US SMBs use AI but only 69% have a policy [tech.co]
- In large firms, 57% of employees entered confidential information into AI tools and only 24% had mandatory training [Dataconomy]
- Only 51.9% of MSPs keep a full inventory of AI tools in client environments [Channel Insider]
- Caveat: many shadow-AI percentages cannot be traced to an original study [ainora.lt]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small and mid-sized firms (20–250 staff) in professional services: accounting, law, agencies, recruiting.
- **Buyer:** The owner or COO, or the MSP that resells it to its clients.
- **Start market:** MSPs in the EU and English-speaking markets, plus direct sales to EU accounting and law firms.

### How it works

1. **Deploy the extension:** a Chrome/Edge extension is pushed to all staff through Intune or Google admin.
2. **See AI use:** it detects visits to 200+ AI websites and shows a dashboard per client company.
3. **Warn before leaks:** local pattern-matching spots personal data, IBANs, card numbers and API keys and shows a warning banner (warn, not block, at first).
4. **Check connected AI apps:** a scan of which AI apps users connected to Microsoft 365.
5. **Policy and training records:** an AI-policy generator with employee sign-off, plus 5 short training modules with a completion log.
6. **Monthly PDF:** a report the MSP can use in client reviews.

### MVP (first version)

- Chrome/Edge extension deployable through Intune or Google admin
- Detection of 200+ AI websites
- Local pattern-matching for personal data, IBANs, card numbers and API keys, with a warning banner
- Dashboard per client company
- Scan of AI apps connected to Microsoft 365
- AI-policy generator with employee acknowledgement and 5 training modules with completion log
- Monthly PDF report for MSP client reviews

*Later:* Blocking mode and allow-lists per company · Google Workspace connected-apps scan · Bundle with InsureReady, Leaver and QBR Autopilot as one MSP product · Firefox and Safari support · PSA integration for MSP billing

### Pricing

- **MSP wholesale: $2–3 / user / month:** 10-user minimum per client, multi-client dashboard
- **Direct: $3–4 / user / month:** Minimum $49/month
- **Founding plan: $490 / year:** Early adopters during validation

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting; the extension does most work on the user's computer | €10–25 / month |
| AI API | Policy generator and report text | €5–15 / month |
| Chrome Web Store developer fee | One-time | ≈ €5 one-off |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark free or low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | No-code builder or built by us | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report estimate €200–300 for hosting, AI and validation, plus €300–450 for AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Prompt Security (now SentinelOne), Lakera (now Check Point) | Enterprise AI security | Bought by large vendors; enterprise sales |
| Harmonic, LayerX, Nightfall | AI data-loss prevention | Quote-based, not MSP-friendly |
| Microsoft / Google native controls | Built-in data-loss features | Need premium licences and expert setup |
| **Us** | Browser extension + AI policy + training log, multi-client | Cheap, per user, built for MSPs and small firms |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Publish a free "AI usage policy + shadow-AI checklist" lead magnet. Jonatan offers 20 MSPs a free 14-day hand-run pilot on one client. Pre-sell a $490/year founding plan to 5 firms. Target: 3 pilots and 1 paid commitment.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the extension (AI site detection, data-pattern warnings) and the per-client dashboard.
- **Week 6–7:** Microsoft 365 connected-apps scan, policy generator, training log, monthly PDF.
- **Week 8:** Deploy at pilot MSPs' clients and convert pilots to paid.

### Risks

- **Microsoft and Google add native AI data-loss controls:** Stay cross-platform and focus on the policy/training records and MSP multi-client view.
- **Employee-monitoring rules and works councils in the EU:** Warn, do not record content; process data locally; give a clear privacy notice.
- **Extension deployment friction:** Sell through MSPs who already push software via Intune or Google admin.
- **Article 4 was softened:** Lead with client questionnaires and data-leak risk, not with the law.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Which AI tools do your (or your clients') staff use for work, and how do you know?
2. Do you have a written AI usage policy, and is it followed?
3. Has a client asked how you handle their data in AI tools?
4. Have you sold any AI-related service yet, and at what price?
5. Would you deploy a browser extension to all staff, and what would you pay per user?

## 17. DisputeKit: flat-fee chargeback evidence for WooCommerce and Stripe stores

**E-commerce** · $29–149 / month · score **46/70** · competition: high

> "Every week I lose money to chargebacks, and writing each response takes me an hour."

Gathers chargeback evidence automatically for WooCommerce and Stripe stores and writes the rebuttal, for a flat monthly fee instead of a cut of what the store wins back.

**Why now:** Card disputes keep rising: about 261 million in 2025, projected at about 324 million by 2028 (sources disagree on the 2028 number). Most tools charge a percentage of recovered money and focus on Shopify. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Dan sells digital design templates from a WooCommerce store using Stripe.** Each month he gets **a handful of disputes**, often from customers who downloaded the files and then told their bank they never got them. Each response means digging out the order, the IP address, the download log and the emails.

He looked at Chargeflow, but it takes **25% of every recovered chargeback** and is built mainly for Shopify. Dan wants a tool that collects the evidence automatically when a dispute arrives, drafts the answer for the right reason code, and submits it to Stripe, for a **flat monthly fee**.

### Evidence

- Card disputes: about 261 million in 2025, projected at about 324 million by 2028 (vendor source; sources disagree on 2028) [chargeback.io]
- Average internal cost of about $82 per chargeback; fraud behind about 45% of chargeback volume; "friendly fraud" in about 1 in 5 disputes [Mastercard]
- Chargeflow takes 25% of recovered chargebacks; ChargePay charges $19.99–99.99/month flat on Shopify [Chargeflow]
- Disputifier takes 20% of won disputes, capped at $250 [Chargeflow comparison page]
- chargeback.io sells alerts at $15–29 each [chargeback.io]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** WooCommerce and custom-cart stores on Stripe or PayPal with 5–100 disputes a month: digital goods, clothing, supplements.
- **Buyer:** The store owner or finance person.
- **Start market:** Global, in English, starting with digital-product sellers on WooCommerce + Stripe.

### How it works

1. **Connect:** Stripe Connect plus a WooCommerce plugin, set up in minutes.
2. **Collect evidence:** when a dispute arrives, gather order details, IP and location match, delivery tracking, login or download logs and customer emails.
3. **AI writes the response:** a rebuttal tailored to each dispute reason code.
4. **One-click submit:** send the evidence through Stripe's disputes API.
5. **Learn and warn:** a win/loss dashboard and fraud warnings on new orders.

### MVP (first version)

- Stripe Connect integration
- WooCommerce plugin for order, IP, delivery and download data
- Automatic evidence collection when a dispute arrives
- AI-written response per dispute reason code
- One-click submission via Stripe disputes API
- Win/loss dashboard
- Fraud warnings on new orders

*Later:* PayPal disputes · Shopify and other carts · Card-network alerts (pre-dispute) · Customer-email templates to prevent disputes · Agency plan for multiple stores

### Pricing

- **Starter: $29/mo:** Small stores, flat fee, no success fee
- **Growth: $79/mo:** More disputes per month, fraud warnings
- **Pro: $149/mo:** High-volume stores, priority support

## 18. GrantDesk: multi-client workspace for freelance grant writers

**Nonprofit / grant consulting** · $39–199 / month · score **46/70** · competition: high

> "I have twelve clients, forty deadlines and my boilerplate is spread across a hundred Word files."

One workspace for freelance grant writers to manage all their clients: deadlines, reusable text per client, report reminders and AI first drafts. Existing tools are built for one organisation, not a consultant with many clients.

**Why now:** Grant software is a proven paid category: Instrumentl starts at $179/month and Grantable at $50/month, and Grantable claims 30,000+ grant professionals as users. AI now writes good first drafts from a client's own past text. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Rachel is a freelance grant writer with 12 nonprofit clients.** Each client has its own mission text, budgets, past applications and funder reports due on different dates. She tracks deadlines in a spreadsheet and keeps text in **folders full of Word files**.

Twice last year she nearly missed a **funder report deadline** because it was buried in an email. Tools like Instrumentl are great for finding funders but are built for one organisation, not a consultant juggling many. Rachel wants one place with client folders, a text library per client, a deadline calendar and a status link she can send to each client.

### Evidence

- Instrumentl plans start at $179/month; Grantable paid plans start at $50/month (pricing as of May 2026) [Grantable]
- Grantable claims 30,000+ grant professionals as users (vendor claim) [Grantable]
- A third-party review lists Grantable at free / $50 / $150 per month (unverified) [aitoolsbakery]
- Many small new entrants on Capterra, e.g. Imagagrant and ScriptaGrant [Capterra]
- Pain evidence is indirect: no survey of grant-writer hours or missed deadlines was found [Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Freelance grant writers and consultants managing 5–20 clients, and small nonprofits with budgets under $2M.
- **Buyer:** The consultant, or the nonprofit's director.
- **Start market:** English-speaking freelance grant writers (US, UK, Canada, Australia), found on LinkedIn and Upwork.

### How it works

1. **Pipeline board:** each application moves through prospect, drafting, submitted, awarded and reporting.
2. **Deadline reminders:** email reminders for application and report deadlines across all clients.
3. **Library per client:** documents and boilerplate stored per client, searchable with AI.
4. **AI first drafts:** draft sections from the client's library and the pasted funder guidelines.
5. **Client status link:** a shareable page so each client sees progress without emails.

### MVP (first version)

- Grant pipeline board (prospect, drafting, submitted, awarded, reporting)
- Email reminders for deadlines and reports
- Document and boilerplate library per client with AI search
- AI drafts of sections from the library and pasted funder guidelines
- Shareable client status link
- Stripe billing

*Later:* Export drafts to Word with formatting · Budget templates per client · Time tracking and invoicing for consultants · Agency plan with team members · Calendar sync (Google / Outlook)

### Pricing

- **Nonprofit: $39/mo:** A single nonprofit organisation
- **Consultant: $99/mo:** Up to 10 clients
- **Agency: $199/mo:** More clients and team members

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Vercel + Supabase free or low tiers | €0–25 / month |
| AI API | Drafting and search | €10–30 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark free or low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | No-code builder or built by us | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–700 (report estimate €150–250 for hosting, AI and validation, plus €300–450 for AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Instrumentl | Funder discovery and tracking, from $179/month | Strong on finding funders; not built for multi-client consultants |
| Grantable | AI grant writing, $50–150/month | Single-organisation focus |
| Granted AI, Imagagrant, ScriptaGrant | AI grant writing tools | Writing only; no multi-client workspace |
| **Us** | Client folders, text library per client, deadline calendar, client status page | Built for the consultant with many clients |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Message 150 freelance grant writers on LinkedIn and Upwork; hold 10 interviews; show a clickable prototype (Figma or similar). Target: 5 consultants agree to a $99 founding-member pre-sale or paid pilot.
- **Week 3–4:** Victor builds client workspaces, pipeline board and deadline reminders.
- **Week 5–6:** Library with AI search, AI drafts, client status link, Stripe.
- **Week 7–8:** Onboard founding members and iterate on their feedback.

### Risks

- **Crowded with AI writing tools:** Sell the multi-client workspace and deadlines, not the AI writing.
- **Nonprofits are price-sensitive:** Target consultants first: they bill clients and feel the pain directly.
- **Funder data is US-centric:** Do not build a funder database; users bring their own funders.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many clients and applications do you juggle per month?
2. How do you track deadlines and reporting duties today?
3. Where does your reusable text live, and how do you adapt it?
4. Which tools do you pay for now, and what do you dislike about them?
5. What has a missed deadline or late report cost you?

### Scores

pain: 7 · willingnessToPay: 6 · lowCompetition: 4 · easyToBuild: 7 · easyToSell: 7 · skillFit: 8 · globalPotential: 7 · **total 46/70**

## 19. SafeListing: AI fills EU product-safety and packaging data from supplier documents

**E-commerce** · $19–199 / month · score **45/70** · competition: high

> "I have 2,000 products and the law says each one needs manufacturer details and warnings in the buyer's language. The data is buried in supplier PDFs."

AI pulls EU product-safety (GPSR) and packaging data out of supplier PDFs, translates the warnings and fills the store and marketplace fields sellers are legally required to show. Existing plugins only add empty fields.

**Why now:** The EU General Product Safety Regulation (GPSR) has applied since 13 December 2024 and requires manufacturer details, an EU responsible person and warnings in the buyer's language on listings. The Packaging Regulation (PPWR) has applied since 12 August 2026, so online sellers must register and pay packaging fees in each country they ship to; a vendor cites German deadlines of 12 September and 12 November 2026.

### The problem

**Jan runs a WooCommerce shop in the Netherlands selling toys and home goods** from a dozen suppliers. Since December 2024 the GPSR rules say every listing must show **manufacturer details, an EU responsible person and safety warnings** in the buyer's language. He installed a free plugin, but it only added **empty fields** to 2,000 products.

The real data is in supplier PDFs and safety sheets, in English, German and Chinese. On top of that, from August 2026 he must **register for packaging fees** in each country he ships to. Jan needs a tool that reads the supplier documents, fills the fields, translates the warnings and tracks his registrations.

### Evidence

- GPSR has applied since 13 December 2024 and requires manufacturer details, an EU responsible person and buyer-language warnings on listings [PriceProof]
- Shopify does not provide a responsible person, so liability stays with the merchant [Meetanshi]
- PPWR has applied since 12 August 2026: online sellers must register and pay packaging fees in each country they ship to [Taxually]
- Amazon published a paper in March 2026 on how fragmented EPR packaging registrations are [Amazon]
- Enforcement intensity is uncertain: marketplaces reportedly not yet required to check packaging registration numbers, no mass delisting [expan.do]
- About 20 Shopify GPSR apps (most with no reviews) and at least 10 WooCommerce GPSR plugins, many free [Shopify App Store / WordPress.org]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** EU and non-EU merchants selling into the EU on WooCommerce, Shopware or PrestaShop with hundreds of products or more, and the agencies that run their stores.
- **Buyer:** The owner or e-commerce manager.
- **Start market:** WooCommerce and Shopware stores in Germany, the Netherlands and Sweden, plus their agencies.

### How it works

1. **Scan the catalogue:** a WooCommerce plugin finds every product with missing GPSR fields.
2. **Upload supplier documents:** AI extracts manufacturer, responsible person and warnings, with a confidence score.
3. **Human review:** a review queue lets the merchant approve or fix each extraction.
4. **Translate and publish:** warnings are translated and shown in a product-page safety block.
5. **Packaging tracker:** a simple per-country packaging-registration checklist.
6. **Export:** CSV export, later feeds for Google, eMAG and Allegro.

### MVP (first version)

- WooCommerce plugin plus cloud service
- Bulk scan of the catalogue for missing GPSR fields
- Supplier-document upload with AI extraction and confidence score
- Human review queue
- Translation of warnings
- Product-page safety block and CSV export
- Simple packaging-registration checklist per country

*Later:* Shopware and PrestaShop apps · Marketplace feeds (Google, eMAG, Allegro) · Translation into all EU languages · Bundle with PriceGuard · Agency plan for many stores

### Pricing

- **Small: $19/mo:** Small catalogues
- **Growth: $49/mo:** Hundreds of products, translations
- **Pro: $99/mo, Agency: $199/mo:** Large catalogues; many stores for agencies

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Small VPS or free tiers | €5–20 / month |
| AI API (document + image) | Main running cost; bill extra credits above plan limits | €15–30 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark free or low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | No-code builder or built by us | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–750 (report estimate €200–300 for hosting, AI and validation, plus €300–450 for AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Shopify GPSR apps (about 20, e.g. Safix, PriceProof) | Add GPSR fields to listings | Mostly empty fields; most have no reviews |
| WooCommerce GPSR plugins (10+, many free) | Add fields and display blocks | Do not gather the data |
| Compliance consultants / EPR services | Registration services | Not linked to the product catalogue |
| **Us** | AI extraction from supplier documents, translation, packaging tracker, feeds | Gathers the data, not just the fields |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Post in WooCommerce and Shopware groups offering a free "GPSR gap scan" of up to 1,000 products; measure sign-ups. Run AI extraction by hand on three merchants' supplier PDFs. Ask for $49/month to keep it updated.
- **Week 3–5:** Victor builds the plugin, catalogue scan and supplier-document extraction.
- **Week 6–7:** Review queue, translation, safety block, CSV export, packaging checklist.
- **Week 8:** Publish a free plugin on WordPress.org with paid AI credits; convert gap-scan users.

### Risks

- **Saturated, often free competition:** Free plugin as the entry, charge for AI extraction credits.
- **Buyers fear legal liability:** State clearly it is not legal advice; human review before publishing.
- **Urgency fades if enforcement stays weak:** Combine with PriceGuard so value does not depend only on enforcement.
- **Packaging rules still changing:** Keep the packaging part a simple, editable checklist.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How did you handle GPSR in December 2024, and what is still missing?
2. Where does your manufacturer and safety data come from today?
3. Have you been delisted or warned by a marketplace?
4. Are you registered for packaging fees in every country you ship to?
5. What would you pay never to fill these fields by hand again?

### Scores

pain: 7 · willingnessToPay: 6 · lowCompetition: 4 · easyToBuild: 6 · easyToSell: 6 · skillFit: 9 · globalPotential: 7 · **total 45/70**

### Sources

- [PriceProof GPSR (Shopify App Store)](https://apps.shopify.com/priceproof-gpsr)
- [Meetanshi: EU GPSR for Shopify](https://meetanshi.com/blog/eu-gpsr-for-shopify/)
- [Taxually: PPWR for online sellers](https://www.taxually.com/blog/ppwr-eu-packaging-regulation-online-sellers)
- [Printify: Germany EPR registration](https://printify.com/blog/germany-epr-registration/)
- [Amazon: fragmentation in EPR packaging registration (March 2026)](https://assets.aboutamazon.com/4a/4f/533903e94ec3befeb991ab514a03/fragmentation-in-epr-packaging-registration-and-opportunities-to-harmonize-march-2026.pdf)
- [expan.do: PPWR marketplace enforcement](https://www.expan.do/post/ppwr-marketplaces-authorised-representative-enforcement)
- [Shopify App Store: Safix](https://apps.shopify.com/safix)

## 20. ReportPilot: AI-written client report commentary for marketing agencies

**Marketing agencies** · $49–199 / month · score **45/70** · competition: high

> "The dashboard builds itself. Writing why the numbers changed for 25 clients takes me two days every month."

Pulls each client's numbers from Google Analytics, Search Console and Google Ads and writes the monthly "what happened and what we do next" commentary in the agency's own voice, for a flat price.

**Why now:** The market leader AgencyAnalytics moved to per-client pricing of about $20 per client per month (billed annually), and reviewers say per-client pricing "adds up fast". Sources disagree on the exact current plans. AI can now explain metric changes in plain language.

### The problem

**Tom runs a five-person SEO and Google Ads agency with 25 clients.** At the start of every month, his team exports dashboards and then writes a short explanation for each client: why traffic dropped, why cost per lead rose, what they will do next.

Tom pays for a reporting tool, but it moved to **per-client pricing**, so his bill grows with every new client. And it still does not write the part clients actually read: **the explanation**. Tom wants the commentary drafted in his agency's voice, ready to approve, at a flat price.

### Evidence

- AgencyAnalytics moved to per-client pricing of about $20 per client per month, billed annually [Coupler.io]
- Reviewers say per-client pricing "adds up fast"; sources disagree on exact plans [Swydo]
- Older AgencyAnalytics tiered plans are still cited at $79 (Freelancer) and $400–548/month for mid-size agencies [Reporting Ninja]
- No survey of hours spent writing reports was found; commentary as the main time sink is an inference to test [Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** SEO, pay-per-click and social-media agencies with 1–15 people and 5–40 clients.
- **Buyer:** The agency owner.
- **Start market:** English-speaking agencies worldwide, found on LinkedIn and agency communities.

### How it works

1. **Connect Google:** Google login connects GA4, Search Console and Google Ads per client.
2. **Templated metrics:** standard metrics per client, no dashboard building.
3. **AI narrative:** AI explains unusual changes ("why did cost per lead rise?") in the agency's own voice.
4. **Approve:** the agency comments and approves before anything is sent.
5. **Send:** a white-label PDF and scheduled email to each client.

### MVP (first version)

- Google login connections for GA4, Search Console and Google Ads
- Templated metrics per client
- AI narrative explaining unusual changes
- Agency voice settings (tone, examples)
- Comment-and-approve step before sending
- White-label PDF and scheduled email

*Later:* Meta Ads and LinkedIn Ads · Bulk approve for many clients · Client portal · Integration as an add-on to existing dashboards

### Pricing

- **Starter: $49/mo:** Up to 10 clients
- **Growth: $99/mo:** Up to 30 clients
- **Unlimited: $199/mo:** Unlimited clients

### Costs

|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Vercel + Supabase low tiers | €0–20 / month |
| AI API | Monthly narratives | €10–20 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark free or low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | No-code builder or built by us | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–700 (report estimate €150–250 for hosting, AI and validation, plus €300–450 for AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| AgencyAnalytics | Market-leading agency dashboards, about $20/client/month | Per-client pricing adds up; commentary still written by hand |
| Swydo, Reporting Ninja, Databox, Whatagraph | Reporting dashboards | Data first, explanation second |
| Porter Metrics, Coupler, Looker Studio (free) | Data connectors and free dashboards | No narrative, more setup |
| **Us** | AI-written commentary in the agency's voice + white-label PDF | Flat price; the part clients actually read |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Offer 10 agencies a free AI-written report for one client using exported data; ask for $49/month to continue. Goal: 3 yes. Apply for a Google Ads developer token early.
- **Week 3–4:** Victor builds GA4 and Search Console connections and templated metrics.

## 21. QBR Autopilot: AI-drafted quarterly client reviews for small MSPs

**IT services / MSP** · $79–149 / month · score **45/70** · competition: medium

> "I know I should show every client what we did this quarter, but preparing one review takes me half a day."

Turns an MSP's ticket history and Microsoft 365 data into a ready-to-edit quarterly business review (QBR) report for each client. Small MSPs often skip QBRs because preparing them takes hours.

**Why now:** In Kaseya's 2026 survey, staffing replaced tool sprawl as MSPs' biggest operational challenge, so tools that save technician hours sell better. 19% of MSPs say they cannot quickly show clients their value (search summary).

### The problem

**Mark owns a 6-person MSP with 30 small business clients.** He knows quarterly reviews keep clients loyal and open up upsells. But each one means exporting tickets from his PSA, sorting them by category, checking Microsoft 365 licences and writing slides.

So he does them for **only his biggest clients**. The others never see the value he delivers, and one of them recently left for a cheaper provider, saying "we never hear from you". Mark wants a **drafted QBR per client** that he can edit in ten minutes.

### Evidence

- Staffing replaced tool sprawl as MSPs' biggest operational challenge in 2026 [Kaseya]
- 19% of MSPs say they cannot quickly show clients their value (search summary) [MSP Success]
- ScalePad Lifecycle Insights listed from about $149/month; ScalePad bundles from $250/month [Software Advice / ScalePad]
- An aggregator lists Lifecycle Manager at $99/month for 10 clients (low confidence) [checkthat.ai]
- No survey of hours per QBR was found [Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small MSPs whose owners or virtual CIOs skip QBRs because preparing them takes hours.
- **Buyer:** The MSP owner.
- **Start market:** English-speaking MSPs not on ScalePad, reached through MSP communities and as an InsureReady add-on.

### How it works

1. **Connect the PSA:** connect HaloPSA, Syncro or Autotask, or upload a CSV export as a fallback.
2. **Group tickets:** tickets are sorted into categories and trends per client.
3. **AI draft:** AI writes editable sections: what we did, risks, recommendations, budget.
4. **Edit and brand:** the MSP edits in 10 minutes and exports a branded PDF or slides.

### MVP (first version)

- Connection to one or two PSA systems (HaloPSA, Syncro or Autotask)
- CSV upload as a fallback
- Ticket categories and trends per client
- AI summary with editable sections (what we did, risks, recommendations, budget)
- Branded PDF or slide output

*Later:* Microsoft 365 licence and security data · Combined InsureReady evidence pack + QBR · More PSA integrations (ConnectWise) · Client feedback form after each QBR

- **Growth: $149/mo:** Up to 30 clients

## 22. Leaver: offboarding and access clean-up for small companies

**Cybersecurity** · $2 / user / month (min $39) · score **45/70** · competition: medium

> "Three months after she left, we found out our old sales manager could still log into the CRM."

When someone leaves a small company, one click removes their access to Google Workspace or Microsoft 365 and connected apps, and keeps proof that it was done.

**Why now:** Insurers, auditors and customers increasingly ask for offboarding evidence as part of security checks (an inference from the insurance and questionnaire trends). There is no specific deadline.

### The problem

**Sofia is office manager at a 60-person design firm with no IT department.** When someone leaves, she disables their email, but nobody knows which other apps they used: Dropbox, a CRM, design tools, apps connected with "Sign in with Google".

Last quarter a client's security questionnaire asked: **"Can you show evidence that leavers lose access within 24 hours?"** Sofia could not. Later she found a former employee **still had access to a shared drive**. She wants a checklist that runs itself and a log that proves it.

### Evidence

- 48% of former employees still had app access months after leaving (CloudEagle 2026 report, 1,000 enterprise CIOs and CISOs; vendor source) [CloudEagle]
- A Sagiss survey, cited second-hand, found 82% of workers at firms under 250 staff had lingering access [Efficiently Connected / Infonaligy]
- Torii, Zluri, BetterCloud and CloudEagle are enterprise-oriented; their prices were not found [Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Companies with 20–250 staff and no dedicated IT person, plus MSPs.
- **Buyer:** The office manager, HR/operations lead or MSP.
- **Start market:** Global in English, through MSPs and office-manager/HR communities.

### How it works

1. **Connect:** a read-first connection to Google Workspace or Microsoft 365.
2. **See connected apps:** a list of third-party apps each user has connected through login grants.
3. **One-click leaver flow:** suspend the account, transfer Drive and mail, revoke tokens, remove from groups.
4. **Manual checklist:** for apps without an API, a checklist with owners and due dates.
5. **Proof and savings:** an audit-log export as evidence, plus a report of wasted licences.

### MVP (first version)

- Google Workspace and Microsoft 365 connector
- List of third-party apps each user has connected
- One-click leaver flow (suspend, transfer Drive and mail, revoke tokens, remove from groups)
- Manual checklist for other apps
- Wasted-licence report
- Audit-log export

*Later:* Onboarding flow (joiner checklist) · Direct integrations with common SaaS apps · MSP multi-tenant dashboard · HR system trigger (leaver date starts the flow) · Bundle with InsureReady and AI Use Guard

### Pricing

- **Per user: $2 / user / month:** Minimum $39/month per company
- **MSP: volume pricing:** Multi-client dashboard, bundled with other MSP modules

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| Microsoft test tenant | For development and demos | €0–20 / month |
| Google security review for sensitive permissions | May take time and money; not researched | Unknown |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark free or low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | No-code builder or built by us | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–850 (report estimate €200–400 for hosting, AI and validation, plus €300–450 for AI coding tools)** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Torii, Zluri, BetterCloud, CloudEagle | SaaS management and identity governance | Enterprise-oriented; prices not public |
| MSP tools | Many competing tools in the MSP world | Often part of larger, pricier suites |
| Manual checklists | What most small firms do | No visibility of connected apps, no proof |
| **Us** | Connected-app discovery, leaver checklist, evidence log | $2/user, set up in minutes |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Offer a free "zombie access audit" to 10 small companies using manual admin exports; count lingering accounts and app grants; ask whether owners would pay to fix and monitor them.
- **Week 3–4:** Victor builds the Google and Microsoft connectors and the connected-apps list. Start the Google security review early.

> "One customer used our AI feature so much that he cost us more than he paid, and we only found out from the bill."

### The problem

**Priya is CTO of a 6-person SaaS startup** that added an AI writing feature. The OpenAI bill grows every month, but she cannot see **which customers or plans** cause it. Last month one free-trial user ran thousands of requests overnight.

She tried an open-source logging tool, but it shows tokens and traces, not business answers like **"which plan loses money on AI?"**. She wants per-customer costs next to Stripe revenue, and hard caps that switch heavy users to a cheaper model instead of failing.

### Evidence

### How it works

2. **Live prices:** a live price table per model turns tokens into money.
3. **Margin per customer:** a dashboard combines AI cost with Stripe revenue per customer.
4. **Caps:** budget caps return a graceful error or switch to a cheaper model.
5. **Alerts:** Slack and email alerts, plus CSV export.

## 24. AfterHours: AI phone answering in local languages for trades and cleaning firms

**Trades / home services** · €79–199 / month · score **42/70** · competition: high

> "When I'm under a sink I can't answer the phone, and the customer just calls the next plumber."

An AI phone assistant that answers missed calls for plumbers, electricians and cleaning firms in Swedish, Romanian or German, takes the job details and books a slot.

**Why now:** No-code voice-AI platforms (such as Vapi or Retell) make a first version possible without telecom expertise. The English-speaking market is full, but local languages are less served. There is no regulatory deadline.

### The problem

**Erik is a plumber in Uppsala with 4 employees.** During the day he and his team are on jobs with dirty hands; in the evening he is with his kids. Calls go to voicemail, and **most people do not leave a message**. They call the next plumber on Google.

US products answer calls in English, but his customers speak **Swedish**. He wants something that picks up when he cannot, asks for name, address, problem and urgency, sends him a text summary and books a time.

### Evidence

- 27% of calls to home-services businesses go unanswered (vendor source) [Invoca]
- 14% unanswered according to a CallRail benchmark [StealthAgents]
- A missed call is worth $200–500 in lost jobs [Signpost]
- The common "62% unanswered" figure comes from a 2016 study of 85 businesses and should not be used [SchedulingKit]
- US AI receptionists: Rosie about $49/month, Sameday about $449/month [Solvea / Sameday]

### How it works

1. **Forward missed calls:** the firm forwards unanswered calls to a local number.
2. **AI answers:** a voice agent in Swedish (or Romanian/German) takes name, address, problem and urgency.
3. **SMS summary:** the owner gets a text with the job details right away.
4. **Booking:** the caller gets a calendar booking link.
5. **Dashboard:** transcripts of all calls in one place.

- Voice agent that takes name, address, problem and urgency

## 25. MCP Shield: security scanner for AI-agent tool connections

**Cybersecurity** · Free CLI; $8 / dev / month · score **42/70** · competition: high

> "Our developers installed a dozen MCP servers into their AI agents last month. I have no idea what those tools can do."

A free command-line scanner, with a paid team dashboard, that checks the MCP servers developers plug into AI coding agents for poisoned tool descriptions, too-broad permissions and leaked secrets.

**Why now:** Investors are pouring money into the space: Runlayer launched with an $11M seed in November 2025 and Obot raised a $35M seed. The OWASP Top 10 for LLM applications lists prompt injection and "excessive agency" among the main risks. MCP (the Model Context Protocol) is now the standard way to connect tools to AI assistants such as Claude Code or Cursor.

### The problem

**Lukas is head of engineering at an 80-person software company.** His developers use Claude Code and Cursor, and each one has plugged in several MCP servers: database tools, GitHub, Slack, random open-source tools found online.

The security team asks: **which tools have access to what?** Could a tool description contain hidden instructions? Are API keys sitting in plain-text config files? The enterprise MCP gateways on the market are heavy to deploy. Lukas wants a **simple scanner** his developers can run, and one dashboard that shows the risks across the team.

### Evidence

- Runlayer launched with an $11M seed in November 2025 [TechCrunch]
- Obot AI raised a $35M seed for an enterprise MCP gateway [VCA Online]
- Archestra raised $3.3M (Aug 2025) to secure enterprise use of MCP servers [SiliconANGLE]

- **Buyer:** The engineering lead or CTO.
- **Start market:** Global, through GitHub, Hacker News and security communities.

### How it works

1. **Run the free CLI:** it reads MCP configs from Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code and Windsurf.
2. **Find risks:** static rules plus an AI review of tool descriptions flag poisoning, broad permissions and leaked secrets.
3. **Fit into CI:** SARIF output for GitHub code scanning.
4. **Team dashboard:** reports per developer combined in one view (paid).
5. **Policy:** an allowlist policy file of approved MCP servers.

### MVP (first version)

- CLI that reads MCP configs from Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code and Windsurf
- Static rules for secrets and broad permissions
- AI review of tool descriptions for poisoning
- SARIF output for GitHub code scanning
- Small team dashboard combining reports per developer
- Allowlist policy file

*Later:* Public registry of scanned MCP servers with risk scores · Runtime monitoring of tool calls · SSO and audit logs for the dashboard · IDE extension

- **Team: $8 / developer / month:** Dashboard, policy file, combined reports
- **Company: $299/mo:** Up to 50 developers

## 26. PayGuard: fake-invoice and payment-fraud protection for small firms

**Cybersecurity** · €49–199 / month · score **47/70** · competition: medium

> "The email came from our regular supplier's address, the invoice looked normal, and we paid €38,000 to a new account. The supplier never sent it."

Small firms lose money when criminals send fake invoices or ask them to "update our bank details". PayGuard locks down the firm's email domain, watches for lookalike domains of the firm and its main suppliers, and warns when an incoming invoice has new bank details.

**Why now:** Business email compromise is still one of the most expensive cybercrimes, and AI makes fake emails better written in every language. Banks in the euro area must check payee names since 9 October 2025; non-euro EU countries such as Sweden and Romania follow on 9 July 2027, so the product must be positioned as the extra layer that bank checks miss (lookalike domains, own-domain spoofing, changed details on the invoice itself).

### The problem

**Anna is finance manager at a 45-person installation company in Gothenburg.** She pays about **120 supplier invoices a month**. One Friday an email arrives from what looks like the steel supplier: "Our bank has changed, please use the new account on the attached invoice." The domain is **one letter different** from the real one. Nobody notices, and **€38,000** goes to the criminals.

After the loss, the company's accountant tells Anna three things: their own domain has **no DMARC protection**, so criminals can also send emails that look like they come from her firm; nobody checks if supplier bank details have changed; and lookalike domains are registered every day. Big-company tools for this are sold with a sales call and custom prices. Anna wants something that costs less than one month of coffee and just warns her.

### Evidence

- Business email compromise losses were about $2.77 billion in 2024 across 21,442 complaints, the second-biggest loss category [FBI IC3 2024 via CyberScoop]
- UK invoice and mandate scam losses fell to £41.3m in 2025, the lowest since 2020, after banks introduced name checks; business APP fraud losses were £75.6m [UK Finance Annual Fraud Report 2026 via Today's Conveyancer / Payment Expert]
- DMARC adoption reached 52.1% of domains, but more than half of those sit at p=none, which does not block spoofing [dmarcreport summary of EasyDMARC 2026 report]
- Eftsure, a leading supplier-payment verification tool, has only custom pricing; a small-business reviewer called it "somewhat steep and difficult for our small company to handle" [G2]
- Euro-area banks must verify payee names from 9 October 2025, non-euro EU countries from 9 July 2027 [ECB / Taylor Wessing]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Firms with 10–150 staff that pay many supplier invoices by bank transfer: installers, construction and facility-service firms, wholesalers, agencies.
- **Buyer:** The finance manager, CFO or owner. Through partners: the accounting firm or MSP that serves them.
- **Start market:** Sweden and the Nordics (Jonatan's network, bank name checks only from July 2027), then UK and English-speaking EU firms.

### How it works

1. **Domain check in 2 minutes:** enter the company domain; PayGuard reads SPF, DKIM and DMARC records and shows in plain words whether criminals can send email as you.
2. **Guided DMARC to "reject":** PayGuard receives DMARC reports, lists every service that sends email for you (Fortnox, Microsoft 365, newsletter tool) and gives the exact DNS record to fix each one, step by step until spoofing is blocked.
3. **Lookalike watch:** the firm adds its own domain and its 20–50 most important suppliers. PayGuard watches new domain registrations and certificate logs for lookalikes (one letter changed, other ending) and alerts the same day.
4. **Invoice bank-detail check:** invoices are forwarded to a PayGuard address or read from the accounts-payable mailbox. AI pulls out the IBAN, Bankgiro or sort code and compares it with what that supplier used before.
5. **Stop-and-call alert:** if bank details changed or the sender domain is a lookalike, finance gets a red alert with a call-back checklist ("call the supplier on the number you already have"), and the result is logged.
6. **Monthly proof report:** a one-page PDF for the owner, insurer or accountant: domain status, lookalikes found, invoices checked, alerts handled.

### MVP (first version)

- Free domain email-security check (lead magnet)
- DMARC report inbox and sender list with fix instructions
- Lookalike-domain watch for own domain + 20 suppliers
- Invoice forwarding address with AI extraction of bank details
- Supplier bank-detail history and change alerts
- Email alerts and a simple dashboard
- Monthly PDF report

*Later:* Direct Microsoft 365 / Google mailbox connection for accounts payable · Fortnox / Visma / Xero supplier-register sync · White-label partner portal for accounting firms and MSPs · Swedish and Romanian interface · Short staff training on fake-invoice tricks

### Pricing

- **Starter: €49/mo:** 1 domain, 20 suppliers watched, 150 invoices checked per month
- **Business: €129/mo:** 3 domains, 100 suppliers, unlimited invoices, mailbox connection
- **Partner: €199/mo:** For accounting firms and MSPs: 10 client firms, white-label reports

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | ~€0.002–0.01 per invoice | €10–30 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Domain and threat data | Free certificate-transparency feeds first; paid WHOIS/new-domain feed later | €0–30 / month |
| Lead list / cold email tool | Short trial for Jonatan's outreach | €30–50 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–800** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Eftsure, Trustpair | Supplier bank-account verification and payment controls | Custom, sales-led pricing aimed at mid-size and large finance teams |
| EasyDMARC, PowerDMARC, Valimail | DMARC monitoring and enforcement | Only protects your own domain; does not check invoices or supplier lookalikes |
| Bank name checks (Verification/Confirmation of Payee) | Bank warns if the account name does not match | Free and growing, but does not catch lookalike domains, spoofing of your own domain, or accounts opened in a matching name |
| **Us** | Domain protection + supplier lookalike watch + invoice bank-detail check | One cheap tool for firms paying 50–500 invoices a month, sold through accountants |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Sell first.** Victor builds the free domain checker in a weekend. Jonatan runs it on 50 Swedish installers and facility firms and calls the owners with the result. Goal: 3 paid pilots at €49.
- **Week 3–5:** DMARC report processing, sender list with fix guides, lookalike-domain watch.
- **Week 6–8:** Invoice forwarding, bank-detail extraction and change alerts, monthly report, Stripe.
- **Week 9–12:** First accounting-firm partner with 5–10 client firms; measure alerts and false alarms.

### Risks

- **Bank name checks make the problem smaller:** Be honest: sell PayGuard as the layer before payment (domain spoofing, lookalikes, changed details), and validate in Sweden before July 2027 and in the UK, where banks already check names.
- **False alarms annoy finance teams:** Only alert on real changes compared with the supplier's history; let users mark known changes; measure false-alarm rate in pilots.
- **Trust: a young vendor reading invoices:** Start with forwarding (no mailbox access), EU hosting, delete invoice files after extraction, public security page.
- **Accounting software adds this feature:** Lookalike-domain and DMARC parts are outside accounting tools; partner with accountants instead of competing with their software.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Has your firm, or a client, ever received a fake invoice or a "new bank details" email? What happened?
2. How do you check today that a supplier's bank account is really theirs?
3. How many supplier invoices do you pay per month, and who approves them?
4. Do you know if your own email domain is protected against spoofing? Who set it up?
5. Would you pay €49–129 a month for warnings before a fake invoice is paid? Who would decide?

## 27. FlowWatch: monitoring and client reports for automation agencies (n8n, Make, Zapier)

**IT services / automation agencies** · €49–249 / month · score **46/70** · competition: medium

> "Our client's lead-routing flow had been dead for 11 days. No error, it just stopped firing. They found out before we did."

Agencies that build n8n, Make and Zapier automations for clients often learn about broken workflows from an angry client. FlowWatch watches every client's workflows in one place, catches errors and workflows that silently stopped running, and sends a monthly white-label report that justifies the maintenance fee.

**Why now:** Workflow automation is booming: n8n raised $180M at a $2.5B valuation in October 2025, and a whole new type of small "AI automation agency" sells builds plus monthly retainers. These agencies need proof of their maintenance work, and built-in error alerts miss workflows that stop running.

### The problem

**Mihai runs a 4-person automation agency in Bucharest** that builds n8n and Make workflows for **23 clients** in the UK and Germany: lead routing, invoice syncing, AI email sorting. Each client pays a **€300–800 monthly maintenance retainer**. Every client has its own n8n instance or Make account, so there are 23 dashboards to check.

Last month a client's API token expired and a workflow simply **stopped triggering**. n8n's error workflow never fired, because nothing ran. The client lost **11 days of leads** and asked what the retainer was for. Mihai wants one screen for all clients, an alert when something fails *or goes quiet*, and a monthly report that shows the client "1,840 runs, 3 failures, all fixed within 2 hours".

### Evidence

- n8n raised a $180M Series C at a $2.5B valuation in October 2025, bringing total funding to about $240M [n8n blog / Crowdfund Insider]
- Error-trigger alerts miss workflows that stop running, so agencies combine error alerts with missed-execution checks [Osher Digital on Cronly]
- Small monitoring tools for n8n agencies are appearing (n8ops, Cronly, FlowGuard, Flowmetr, community dashboards like Pulse) but are early-stage [Product Hunt / n8n community / G2]
- A boring monitoring product can work for a tiny team: Healthchecks.io, run by one part-time developer, reached $14,043 MRR with 652 customers [MicroGaps via Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Automation and AI agencies and freelancers with 5–100 clients on n8n, Make or Zapier.
- **Buyer:** The agency owner or lead automation engineer.
- **Start market:** Global in English, starting with n8n agencies (community forum, YouTube creators, agency directories on n8n and Make partner pages).

### How it works

1. **Connect client accounts:** the agency adds each client's n8n instance (API key) or Make/Zapier account; FlowWatch lists all active workflows.
2. **Learn the normal rhythm:** FlowWatch learns how often each workflow usually runs (every 5 minutes, daily at 08:00, about 40 times a day).
3. **Alert on errors and silence:** it alerts in Slack, email or Telegram when a run fails, when a workflow has not run for longer than usual, or when run counts drop suddenly.
4. **AI explains the error:** the error message is translated into a short likely cause ("Google token expired", "rate limit") and a suggested fix.
5. **Incident log:** every alert becomes an incident with who fixed it and how long it took.
6. **Monthly client report:** a white-label PDF or link per client: runs, uptime, incidents and fix times, hours saved.

### MVP (first version)

- Agency workspace with client folders
- n8n API connection (self-hosted and cloud)
- Error alerts and missed-run detection
- Slack / email / Telegram notifications
- Incident log with fix notes
- White-label monthly PDF report

*Later:* Make and Zapier connections · AI error explanation and fix suggestions · Workflow backup and version history · Client-facing status link · Billing hours to retainers

### Pricing

- **Solo: €49/mo:** Up to 10 client instances, alerts, incident log
- **Agency: €129/mo:** Up to 40 clients, white-label reports, team members
- **Scale: €249/mo:** Unlimited clients, Make + Zapier, backups

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | only for error explanations | €5–20 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €400–650** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| n8n / Make built-in error alerts | Error workflow or email when a run fails | Per instance, misses workflows that stop running, no client report |
| Healthchecks.io, Cronitor, Better Stack | General cron and uptime monitoring | Need manual pings in every workflow; not built for agencies or client reporting |
| n8ops, Cronly, FlowGuard, Flowmetr | New small n8n monitoring tools | Early-stage; mostly n8n only; competition is real and must be watched |
| **Us** | One view for all clients across n8n and Make, silence detection, white-label reports | Built around the agency's retainer, not just alerts |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Talk to 15 n8n agencies (n8n forum, LinkedIn). Offer a free "workflow health audit" of one client instance using a script.
- **Week 3–5:** n8n connector, run history, missed-run detection, alerts.
- **Week 6–8:** Incident log, white-label report, Stripe; 5 design-partner agencies.
- **Week 9–12:** Make connector, AI error explanations, launch in n8n community.

### Risks

- **n8n or Make build this in:** Platforms serve single accounts; the cross-client agency view and reports are the product. Support several platforms to reduce dependence.
- **Several small competitors already exist:** Win on Make + n8n together, silence detection quality and the client report; talk to agencies before building to find what they miss.
- **Agencies are small and churn:** Price per agency, push annual plans, and make the client report part of their retainer so cancelling hurts them.
- **API keys to client systems are sensitive:** Read-only keys where possible, encrypted storage, EU hosting, clear security page.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many client automations do you maintain, and on which platforms?
2. How did you find out about the last broken workflow: alert, or the client?
3. Do you charge a monthly maintenance fee? How do you show clients what you did?
4. What do you use today to monitor runs across clients?
5. Would €49–129 a month for one dashboard plus client reports be worth it? What must it do?

### Scores

pain: 6 · willingnessToPay: 6 · lowCompetition: 4 · easyToBuild: 7 · easyToSell: 5 · skillFit: 9 · globalPotential: 9 · **total 46/70**

## 28. RiskFile: AML risk assessments and audit-ready files for small UK law firms

**Legal** · £79–199 / month · score **44/70** · competition: medium

> "We do ID checks on every client. But when the SRA asked for our firm-wide risk assessment, we sent a template we downloaded in 2018."

UK regulators are fining small law firms for missing or weak anti-money-laundering risk assessments. RiskFile drafts the firm-wide risk assessment, guides a short client and matter risk assessment for every new matter, and keeps everything in a file ready for an SRA review.

**Why now:** The SRA has said it will "ratchet up" AML enforcement after a wave of fines in 2025, many of them for missing or inadequate firm-wide risk assessments. ID-check tools solve identity, not the written risk assessments the regulator now asks to see.

### The problem

**David is managing partner of a 6-solicitor firm in Leeds** doing conveyancing, wills and small business work. The firm uses an online ID-check tool for every client, so he thought AML was handled. Then the regulator sent a **desk-based review** letter asking for the firm-wide risk assessment, three recent client and matter risk assessments, and evidence of staff training.

The risk assessment was a **2018 template** never updated, and the matter assessments were ticks in a case-management field with no reasons. Firms like his have been fined **from about £2,000 up to the £25,000 maximum** for exactly this. David spends two weekends rewriting documents with a consultant at £150 an hour, and still is not sure every new matter will be done properly from now on.

### Evidence

- The SRA issued about £575,000 of AML fines in six months and said it would "ratchet up" its enforcement [Legal Futures]
- Small firms were fined roughly £2,000–7,000 for missing or inadequate firm-wide risk assessments, and several firms got the £25,000 maximum for traditional firms [Legal Futures / Solicitor News]
- UK law firms were hit with record AML fines in 2025 [FinTech Global]
- ID checks are already sold per check: Thirdfort lists about £15 per biometric ID check; Amiqus reportedly has a £500/month minimum (competitor-published figure) [Thirdfort / FigsFlow]
- 71–75% of solo and small-firm lawyers use AI, mostly generic tools [Clio 2026 Solo and Small Legal Trends Report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small law firms (1–25 fee earners) in England and Wales, especially conveyancing, private client and small commercial practices.
- **Buyer:** The managing partner or the Money Laundering Compliance Officer (MLCO).
- **Start market:** UK (SRA-regulated firms), later Irish solicitors and UK accountants supervised for AML.

### How it works

1. **Firm profile interview:** a 20-minute guided questionnaire about practice areas, client types, countries, payment methods and delivery channels.
2. **Draft firm-wide risk assessment:** AI drafts the assessment from the answers, mapped to the regulator's guidance and the national risk assessment, with a clear list of controls. The MLCO edits and signs.
3. **Matter risk check in 3 minutes:** for each new matter, a short form produces a risk score with written reasons and the checks required (simplified, standard or enhanced due diligence).
4. **Attach evidence:** upload or forward the ID-check report, source-of-funds documents and notes; everything is stored per matter.
5. **Yearly review and training log:** reminders to review the firm assessment, track staff AML training and keep a dated history.
6. **Audit pack:** one click exports the firm assessment, a sample of matter files and the training log for an SRA review.

### MVP (first version)

- Firm profile questionnaire
- AI-drafted firm-wide risk assessment (DOCX export)
- Client and matter risk assessment form with written reasons
- Evidence upload per matter
- Review and training reminders
- Audit pack PDF export

*Later:* Clio / LEAP / Actionstep integration · Import of Thirdfort or Amiqus reports · Policy, controls and procedures templates · Irish and accountant versions · Partner channel through compliance consultants

### Pricing

- **Solo: £79/mo:** Up to 5 fee earners, firm assessment, unlimited matter checks
- **Firm: £149/mo:** Up to 25 fee earners, training log, audit pack, integrations
- **Fix-it-now: £300 one-off:** We draft and review the firm-wide risk assessment with you

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | drafting assessments, a few cents each | €10–30 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Compliance consultant review | One-off check of templates by a UK AML specialist | €150–300 one-off |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €550–800** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Thirdfort, Amiqus, Credas, SmartSearch | ID verification, source-of-funds checks, screening | Strong at checks; the written firm-wide and matter risk reasoning still lives in Word |
| Legl, case-management add-ons | Client onboarding and payments for law firms | Broader platforms with higher prices and sales-led onboarding |
| AML consultants and templates | Write the documents by hand | £100+/hour and a one-off document that is never updated |
| **Us** | AI-drafted firm assessment, 3-minute matter checks, audit pack | Cheap, self-serve and focused on what the SRA fines for |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Jonatan contacts 60 small firms from the SRA register and recent fine notices. Offer a free "AML file health check" done by hand with templates and Claude. Goal: 3 firms agree to pay £79.
- **Week 3–5:** Firm questionnaire, AI draft of firm-wide assessment, DOCX export.
- **Week 6–8:** Matter risk form, evidence storage, reminders, audit pack, payments.
- **Week 9–12:** Ask a compliance consultant to review templates; first 5–10 paying firms.

### Risks

- **Lawyers are conservative buyers:** Lead with fear of the published fines and a free health check; get one known AML consultant as adviser or reseller.
- **Wrong AI wording in a regulated document:** AI drafts, the MLCO approves and signs; templates reviewed by a UK specialist; no legal advice to clients.
- **ID-check vendors add this feature:** Integrate with them instead of competing on checks; stay focused on the written assessments.
- **UK-only market and English nuance:** Jonatan sells in English; later expand to Irish solicitors and UK accountants with the same engine.

### Questions to ask customers

1. When did you last update your firm-wide AML risk assessment, and who wrote it?
2. How do you record a client and matter risk assessment today?
3. Have you had an SRA AML review or desk-based request? What did they ask for?
4. What do you pay for ID checks and AML help today?
5. Would £79–149 a month for an always-ready AML file be worth it? Who decides?

### Scores

pain: 8 · willingnessToPay: 7 · lowCompetition: 5 · easyToBuild: 7 · easyToSell: 5 · skillFit: 7 · globalPotential: 5 · **total 44/70**
