## 29. RetryWoo: failed-payment recovery for WooCommerce subscription stores

**E-commerce** · $29–149 / month · score **43/70** · competition: medium

> "Every month about 60 coffee subscriptions just stop because a card failed. Most of those people never meant to cancel."

Subscription stores lose customers when cards expire or payments fail. RetryWoo is a WooCommerce plugin plus cloud service that retries payments at smart times, warns customers before cards expire, and sends one-click card-update links by email and SMS.

**Why now:** Involuntary churn from failed payments is a well-documented, measurable loss, and the known recovery tools focus on Stripe-billed SaaS. WooCommerce subscription stores (coffee, pet food, beauty, courses, memberships) are a big, under-served group that buys through the WordPress plugin directory.

### The problem

**Lena runs a coffee subscription shop on WooCommerce** with about **1,400 active subscribers** paying €19 a month. Every month around **5% of renewals fail**: expired cards, bank declines, insufficient funds. WooCommerce sends one generic email and retries a few times on a fixed schedule.

She sees the subscription count drop but doesn't know that **most of those customers never chose to leave**. Tools like Churnkey or Baremetrics Recover are built for software companies on Stripe Billing and cost more than she wants to pay. She wants a plugin that installs in ten minutes and shows: "this month we saved 41 subscriptions, worth €779 a month".

### Evidence

- About 22% of SaaS churn is involuntary (study of 5.4M failed payments); Recurly puts it at 20–26% of B2B SaaS churn [Churnkey]
- Failed payments cost about 9% of recurring revenue a year (attributed to Stripe, secondary source) [Lago]
- Baremetrics Recover's add-on price rose from $158 to $208 a month in 2025 [PricingSaaS]
- Churn Buster keeps pricing behind a sales call; a third-party tracker lists $149/month, unverified [Churn Buster / Toolradar]
- Many indie dunning tools exist for Stripe SaaS (e.g. WorkAid Dunning), which shows demand but also crowding [Indie Hackers]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** WooCommerce stores with 200–10,000 active subscriptions: coffee, food boxes, pet supplies, beauty, memberships and courses.
- **Buyer:** The store owner or e-commerce manager.
- **Start market:** Global in English through the WordPress plugin directory, WooCommerce agencies and subscription-commerce communities.

### How it works

1. **Install the plugin:** connects to WooCommerce Subscriptions and the store's payment gateway (Stripe, PayPal, Mollie) and reads failed renewals.
2. **Smart retries:** retries at better times (after payday, different time of day, by decline reason) instead of a fixed schedule.
3. **Pre-expiry warnings:** emails and SMS customers whose card expires next month, with a one-click update link (no login needed).
4. **Friendly recovery messages:** AI writes short messages in the store's tone and language, sent by email and SMS over 2–3 weeks.
5. **Recovery dashboard:** shows failed, recovered and lost subscriptions and the monthly revenue saved.

### MVP (first version)

- WooCommerce plugin reading failed renewals
- Retry rules by decline reason
- Card-update magic link page
- Email sequences with AI-written templates
- Pre-expiry card warnings
- Recovered-revenue dashboard

*Later:* SMS and WhatsApp messages · Pause or downgrade offers instead of cancelling · Mollie and other EU gateways · Agency plan for many stores · Shopify subscription apps

### Pricing

- **Starter: $29/mo:** Up to 300 active subscriptions, email recovery
- **Growth: $79/mo:** Up to 2,000 subscriptions, SMS, pre-expiry warnings
- **Pro: $149/mo:** Up to 10,000 subscriptions, several stores, pause offers

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | message templates only | €5–15 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Test store and plugin licences | WooCommerce Subscriptions licence for testing | €0–250 / year |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–750** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| WooCommerce Subscriptions built-in retries | Basic fixed retry schedule and emails | No smart timing, no pre-expiry warnings, little reporting |
| Churnkey, Baremetrics Recover, Churn Buster | Payment recovery and cancel flows | Built mainly for Stripe-billed SaaS; higher or sales-led pricing |
| Indie Stripe dunning tools | Cheap email recovery for Stripe | Stripe Billing only, not WooCommerce subscriptions |
| **Us** | WooCommerce-native recovery with smart retries and card-update links | Installs in minutes, priced for small stores, shows saved revenue |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Find 30 WooCommerce subscription stores; ask for their monthly failed-payment numbers; offer a free "lost revenue report".
- **Week 3–5:** Plugin, failed-renewal sync, retry rules, card-update page.
- **Week 6–8:** Email sequences, pre-expiry warnings, dashboard, billing; 5 beta stores.
- **Week 9–12:** WordPress.org free version listing, SMS add-on, case study with real recovered revenue.

### Risks

- **WooCommerce or payment gateways improve built-in retries:** Focus on pre-expiry warnings, messaging and reporting, which gateways do not do well.
- **Small stores, low prices:** Price by active subscriptions so larger stores pay more; aim for stores with 500+ subscribers.
- **Jonatan cannot easily sell it:** This is a plugin-directory and content play; Jonatan can help by contacting WooCommerce agencies as resellers.
- **Payment-data security:** Never store card data; use the gateway's hosted card-update pages.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many subscription renewals fail each month, and what happens next?
2. Which subscription plugin and payment gateway do you use?
3. Do you know how many failed customers come back on their own?
4. Have you tried any recovery tool? Why did you stop or not start?
5. If a plugin showed it saved you €500+ a month, what would you pay for it?

pain: 7 · willingnessToPay: 6 · lowCompetition: 4 · easyToBuild: 7 · easyToSell: 4 · skillFit: 6 · globalPotential: 9 · **total 43/70**

## 30. FaultDesk: AI fault-report desk for facility-service firms that look after housing co-ops (Sweden first)

**Facility services** · €99–349 / month · score **42/70** · competition: medium

> "Half the calls are about things the resident must fix themselves. But we only find out after we have sent a technician."

Facility-service firms that maintain Swedish housing co-ops (BRF) get fault reports from residents by phone, email and apps. FaultDesk sorts each report by urgency, asks for photos, checks if the repair is the resident's or the co-op's responsibility, and creates the work order.

**Why now:** About one in five Swedish households lives in a co-op flat, and co-op boards push their facility firms for faster answers and monthly reporting. AI can now read a resident's message and the co-op's own responsibility list in Swedish, which was not practical two years ago.

### The problem

**Johan owns a 25-person property-care firm (fastighetsskötsel) in Stockholm** that looks after **80 housing co-ops with about 3,500 flats**. Fault reports arrive everywhere: phone calls, emails, the co-op board's app, notes in the stairwell. Two office staff type them into the work-order system.

Many reports are about things the **resident must pay for under the co-op's responsibility list** (a dripping tap inside the flat, a broken dishwasher). Technicians drive out, then the co-op refuses to pay. Real emergencies, like a water leak at night, get mixed with "the light in the laundry room is broken". Johan wants every report sorted, completed with a photo and checked against the co-op's rules **before** a technician is sent.

### Evidence

- 21% of Swedish households live in co-op (bostadsrätt) flats, about 1 million households, at the end of 2025 [SCB, Hushållens boende 2025]
- German startup Lette claims more than 60% of service and maintenance requests can be handled automatically (vendor claim) [Startbase]
- Capital in property-maintenance AI is concentrated in big US players such as EliseAI (reported $4B valuation), which do not serve small Nordic facility firms [CRE Daily via Top 25 report]
- Swedish co-op apps such as Boappa and Ourliving let residents send fault reports to the board, but do not triage them or check responsibility [Boappa guide / App Store]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Facility-service and property-care firms with 5–100 staff that maintain housing co-ops and small rental buildings.
- **Buyer:** The owner or operations manager of the facility firm.
- **Start market:** Stockholm, Gothenburg and Malmö, then Norway and Finland (similar co-op models).

### How it works

1. **One inbox for all reports:** a web form and QR code for each building, an email address and an SMS number feed one queue.
2. **AI triage:** each report is sorted into trade (plumbing, electrical, locks, laundry), urgency (emergency, this week, planned) and building.
3. **Asks for what is missing:** the resident gets an automatic reply asking for a photo, flat number or access time.
4. **Responsibility check:** FaultDesk compares the fault with the co-op's own responsibility list (ansvarsfördelning) and marks it "co-op pays", "resident pays" or "check".
5. **Work order and updates:** a ready work order goes to the firm's system or technician app; residents get status messages.
6. **Monthly board report:** each co-op board gets a short report: faults, response times, costs, recurring problems.

### MVP (first version)

- Building QR codes and web fault form
- Email and SMS intake
- AI triage by trade, urgency and building
- Upload of each co-op's responsibility list and automatic check
- Work-order board with status updates to residents
- Monthly PDF report per co-op

*Later:* Integration with work-order systems used by Swedish facility firms · Night-time emergency forwarding · Boappa / co-op app connection · Norwegian and Finnish · Link to CleanProof inspections

### Pricing

- **Small: €99/mo:** Up to 1,000 flats, web/email intake, triage, reports
- **Medium: €199/mo:** Up to 4,000 flats, SMS, responsibility checks
- **Large: €349/mo:** Unlimited flats, integrations, night emergency routing

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Boappa, Ourliving and other co-op apps | Board communication and fault reporting to the board | Built for boards, not for the facility firm doing the work; no triage or responsibility check |
| Swedish property systems (e.g. Vitec, Momentum) | Full property management for landlords | Large systems sold to property owners; too big for a 25-person care firm |
| EliseAI, Lette and other funded startups | AI for property managers | Focus on US and German landlords, not Swedish co-ops and their facility firms |
| **Us** | AI triage + co-op responsibility check + work order for facility firms | Swedish, small-firm pricing, sold by someone from the industry |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Jonatan calls 20 property-care firms. For 2 firms, run one shared inbox by hand with ChatGPT for a week and count wasted trips.
- **Week 3–5:** Intake form, email/SMS, AI triage, responsibility check.
- **Week 6–8:** Work-order board, resident updates, board report, payments.
- **Week 9–12:** First 3 paying firms; measure wasted trips saved.

### Risks

- **Emergencies handled wrongly:** Anything that might be urgent (water, gas, smoke, locked out) always goes to a human phone number at once; AI never closes emergencies.
- **Small, local market:** Accept a Nordic-first market, then expand with the same engine to cleaning and facility firms elsewhere.
- **Firms already use a work-order system:** Act as the intake layer and push orders into their system instead of replacing it.
- **Overlap with AfterHours (phone answering):** FaultDesk handles written reports and rules; phone answering can be a later add-on, not the core.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many fault reports do you get per week, and through which channels?
2. How often does a technician go out for something the resident should pay for?
3. How do you know each co-op's responsibility rules today?
4. What system do you use for work orders, and who types reports into it?
5. Would €99–199 a month be worth it if it cut wasted trips and typing? Who decides?

## 31. VSME Pack: answer big customers' sustainability questions for small construction and facility suppliers

**Construction** · €39–99 / month · score **42/70** · competition: medium

> "Three customers sent three different climate questionnaires this spring. I'm a painter with 18 staff, not a sustainability department."

Big construction companies and property owners keep sending their small suppliers long sustainability questionnaires. Since the EU now says these requests should stay within the simple VSME standard, VSME Pack helps a small supplier fill in one VSME report from bills and records, and share it with every customer.

**Why now:** The European Commission recommended the VSME standard for SMEs in July 2025 and asked large companies to limit supplier requests to it. The Omnibus I package lets suppliers under 1,000 employees refuse requests beyond the VSME level, and a delegated act turning VSME into the official value-chain cap was drafted in July 2026. Small suppliers now have one standard format they can prepare once.

### The problem

**Sara runs an 18-person painting and surface-treatment firm near Malmö.** Her biggest customers, a national construction group and two property companies, each sent a **sustainability questionnaire**: energy use, fuel, waste, accidents, gender balance, policies. One was **90 questions** in a supplier portal. Without answers she risks falling down the supplier list.

She has the raw data, spread over electricity bills, fuel cards, waste invoices and payroll reports, but no time to turn it into a report. A consultant quoted **€3,000**. She has heard that the EU now says customers should only ask small firms for the simple VSME report, but she does not know how to make one.

### Evidence

- The Commission adopted Recommendation (EU) 2025/1710 on voluntary sustainability reporting for SMEs (VSME) on 30 July 2025 and asked companies to limit value-chain requests to VSME information [Linklaters / Regulation Tomorrow]
- Under the Omnibus I deal, suppliers with fewer than 1,000 employees can refuse sustainability-data requests beyond the VSME standard [A&O Shearman]
- A draft delegated act dated 3 July 2026 (C(2026) 5011) sets out the voluntary standard that will act as the value-chain cap [draft act published by Diritto Bancario]
- Platforms such as osapiens, Coolset and others already offer VSME modules, mostly as part of larger ESG suites (prices not checked) [osapiens]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Construction subcontractors, facility-service and cleaning firms, transport firms and small manufacturers with 10–250 staff that supply large EU companies.
- **Buyer:** The owner, CFO or quality/environment coordinator.
- **Start market:** Sweden and the Nordics, then any EU supplier asked for sustainability data (report in English plus local languages).

### How it works

1. **Short interview:** the firm answers basic questions (sites, vehicles, staff, policies) in 15 minutes.
2. **Upload bills:** electricity, heating, fuel-card and waste invoices and a payroll summary are uploaded or forwarded by email.
3. **AI fills the VSME Basic module:** energy use, estimated emissions, waste, workforce and safety numbers are calculated from the documents, each linked to its source.
4. **Review and sign:** the owner checks the numbers and fills the gaps the AI marks.
5. **Share once:** export the VSME report as PDF/Excel and a share link for customers; map it into common customer questionnaires.
6. **Yearly update:** next year only new bills are needed, and the report shows progress.

### MVP (first version)

- Company interview for the VSME Basic module
- Bill upload with AI extraction (energy, fuel, waste)
- Emission estimates with standard factors
- Review screen with source for every number
- VSME PDF/Excel export and share link
- Reminders for missing data

*Later:* VSME Comprehensive module · Mapping answers into customer supplier portals and questionnaires · Fortnox / Visma data import · Swedish, Finnish and German versions · Partner plan for trade associations

### Pricing

- **Basic: €39/mo (or €390/yr):** VSME Basic report, unlimited sharing, yearly update
- **Plus: €99/mo:** Comprehensive module, questionnaire mapping, 3 users
- **Done-with-you: €400 one-off:** We prepare the first report together with the firm

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | ~€0.01 per bill | €10–25 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €400–650** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| EcoVadis and customer supplier portals | Supplier sustainability ratings and questionnaires | The supplier still has to gather and type the data; ratings can cost the supplier money |
| ESG platforms (osapiens, Coolset, Plan A, Greenly) | Carbon accounting and reporting, some with VSME modules | Broad suites aimed at bigger companies; prices not checked |
| Consultants | Write the report | Thousands of euros and repeated every year |
| **Us** | Bills in, VSME report out, shared with every customer | Cheap, made for 10–250 person subcontractors, sold through Jonatan's network |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Jonatan asks 20 subcontractors if they received sustainability questionnaires; collect 5 real ones. Prepare 2 VSME reports by hand with Claude.
- **Week 3–5:** Interview flow, bill upload, extraction, emission calculation.
- **Week 6–8:** Review screen, VSME export, share link, payments.
- **Week 9–12:** First paying suppliers; approach a trade association as a channel.

### Risks

- **Big customers keep sending their own questionnaires:** Map VSME answers into the most common questionnaires; the share link plus the legal cap gives the supplier a reason to say no to extra questions.
- **Rules change again:** VSME is now the reference; keep the product simple so changes are small.
- **Low willingness to pay:** Sell on avoided consultant fees and keeping big customers; offer yearly plans.
- **ESG suites add cheap VSME tiers:** Win on bill extraction, local language and the subcontractor channel.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Have customers asked you for sustainability or climate data in the last 12 months? How many?
2. Who answered, and how many hours did it take?
3. Did any customer say it affects whether you stay on their supplier list?
4. Where do your energy, fuel and waste numbers live today?
5. Would €39 a month (or €390 a year) for one report you can send everyone be worth it?

### Scores

## 32. ClientStatus: "is it down?" status pages and staff alerts that MSPs give their clients

**IT services / MSP** · $49–199 / month · score **42/70** · competition: high

> "Outlook went down for two hours and we got 140 tickets that all said the same thing."

When Microsoft 365, the internet line or an accounting app goes down, every employee at an MSP's client opens a ticket. ClientStatus gives each client a branded status page that pulls Microsoft 365 health, vendor status feeds and the MSP's own alerts, and pushes one message to all staff.

**Why now:** Small businesses depend on more cloud services than ever, and MSPs are under pressure to show value and cut ticket volume. Microsoft exposes service health through its Graph API, and many vendors publish status feeds, so an automatic per-client page is now easy to build.

### The problem

**Erik runs a 9-person MSP in Uppsala** with **38 small business clients**. When Microsoft has an Outlook outage or a client's internet provider fails, phones ring nonstop. Every employee at every client asks "is it just me?". His technicians spend the outage answering calls instead of fixing anything.

He tried a generic status page tool, but it is built for software companies announcing their own outages. Erik needs one page **per client**, showing the services *that client* uses (Microsoft 365, their Fortnox or Visma, their line-of-business app, their office internet), filled in automatically, and a button to text and Teams-message all staff: "Known issue, we are on it, next update 14:00." Later the incident list goes into his quarterly client review.

### Evidence

- Atlassian Statuspage has no free tier and starts at about $29/month; Instatus has a free plan and Pro at $20/month; Better Stack white-label pages cost $208–250/month (vendor-authored comparisons) [openstatus / WizStatus / Better Stack]
- StatusGator, which aggregates vendor status pages, has a free plan with 3 monitors and paid tiers reported at $79, $149 and $299 per month (competitor comparison) [Pulsetic]
- MSPs report manual, fragmented client reporting [Sophos via Top 25 notes]
- Status pages and dunning were judged "red oceans" for newcomers unless aimed at a sharp sub-niche such as white-label pages for MSPs [Top 25 research notes]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** MSPs with 10–200 small business clients.
- **Buyer:** The MSP owner or service desk manager.
- **Start market:** Global in English: UK, Nordics, Australia and US MSP communities; same buyer as InsureReady and QBR Autopilot.

### How it works

1. **Add a client:** pick the services that client uses from a list (Microsoft 365, Google Workspace, Fortnox, Xero, Zoom, their ISP) and connect their Microsoft tenant read-only.
2. **Automatic page:** a branded page per client shows green/yellow/red for each service, filled from vendor status feeds and Microsoft service health.
3. **MSP's own alerts:** the MSP's monitoring tool can post incidents ("office internet down") through a webhook or email.
4. **One-click broadcast:** send a message to all client staff by email, SMS or Teams, with scheduled updates.
5. **Incident history:** a log per client, exported into quarterly reviews.

### MVP (first version)

- Multi-client MSP workspace with branding
- Service catalogue with vendor status feeds
- Microsoft 365 service health connection
- Per-client public status page
- Email broadcast to client staff lists
- Incident history export

*Later:* SMS and Teams broadcasts · RMM / PSA webhooks (NinjaOne, HaloPSA, ConnectWise) · Ticket deflection banner in the client portal · QBR Autopilot integration · Uptime checks for client websites

### Pricing

- **Starter: $49/mo:** Up to 15 client pages, email broadcasts
- **Pro: $99/mo:** Up to 50 clients, Microsoft 365 health, SMS/Teams
- **Unlimited: $199/mo:** Unlimited clients, custom domain, PSA integrations

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | not needed in MVP (optional incident summaries) | €0–10 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| SMS (later) | Only if MSPs want SMS broadcasts | €0–30 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €400–600** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Atlassian Statuspage, Instatus, Better Stack | Status pages for software companies | One page about your own service; white-label is expensive or manual |
| StatusGator | Aggregates vendor status pages, has a public page | Strongest rival; built for IT teams, not one branded page and broadcast list per MSP client |
| PSA/RMM tools | Ticketing and monitoring | Internal; clients do not see a simple public status |
| **Us** | Per-client branded status pages filled from vendor feeds, plus staff broadcasts | Built for MSPs, priced per MSP not per page |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Jonatan interviews 15 MSPs about the last big outage and ticket counts. Build a static demo page for one MSP.
- **Week 3–4:** Service catalogue, feed pollers, per-client pages, branding.
- **Week 5–6:** Microsoft service health, broadcasts, incident log, Stripe.
- **Week 7–10:** 5 pilot MSPs; measure tickets during the next outage.

### Risks

- **Weak pain: "nice to have":** Validate with ticket counts during real outages; sell only if MSPs can show saved hours.
- **StatusGator adds MSP features:** Stay focused on per-client branding, broadcasts and QBR export; bundle with other MSP modules.
- **Low price ceiling:** Treat it as a cheap entry product into the MSP bundle (InsureReady, QBR Autopilot), not a standalone big business.

### Questions to ask customers

1. During the last Microsoft or internet outage, how many tickets and calls did you get?
2. How do you tell clients' staff about known issues today?
3. Do you use a status page now? Why or why not?
4. Which services do most of your clients depend on?
5. Would $49–99 a month for branded client status pages be worth it?

## 33. NIS2 Nudge: local-language phishing drills and training records that MSPs sell to small clients

**Cybersecurity** · $2–3 / user / month (via MSP) · score **39/70** · competition: high

> "Our customer's NIS2 supplier form asks for proof of staff security training. We have 14 people and no training at all."

Small firms with fewer than 25 staff are priced out of big security-awareness tools, and many staff prefer training in Swedish or Romanian. NIS2 Nudge lets an MSP run monthly fake-phishing tests and 2-minute lessons in the local language and keeps the training records customers and regulators ask for.

**Why now:** NIS2 requires covered companies to train staff in basic cyber hygiene, and Sweden's Cybersecurity Act (cybersäkerhetslagen) came into force on 15 January 2026. These companies push the same expectation onto suppliers. At the same time AI lets criminals write convincing phishing in every language.

### The problem

**Ioana runs a 14-person logistics firm in Cluj** that supplies a large German retailer. The retailer's new **supplier security questionnaire** asks: "Do all staff receive regular security awareness training? Provide evidence." Her MSP looks at KnowBe4, but most offers require **25 seats** and the content is in English.

Meanwhile her accountant almost paid a fake invoice that came in perfect Romanian, written by AI. Ioana wants something simple: a fake phishing email once a month, a 2-minute lesson for whoever clicks, in Romanian, and a PDF that proves it was done. Her MSP wants to resell it to all **30 of its clients** under its own name.

### Evidence

- Sweden's Cybersecurity Act, implementing NIS2, came into force on 15 January 2026 and covers more organisations than the old NIS law [Transportstyrelsen]
- The Swedish civil-defence agency (MCF) has prepared regulations on security measures and training under the new law [MCF impact assessment]
- KnowBe4 costs about $1.63–3.75 per user per month, and most sources cite a 25-seat minimum [CostBench]
- Business email compromise claims rose about 30% in the second half of 2025 [Risk & Insurance]
- Coalition warns that AI phishing kits make micro businesses worth attacking [Insurance Business UK]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Firms with 5–50 staff, especially suppliers to NIS2-covered companies.
- **Buyer:** The MSP (reseller) or the owner of the firm.
- **Start market:** Sweden and Romania/Moldova (founders' languages), then other non-English EU markets through MSPs.

### How it works

1. **MSP adds a client:** imports staff from Microsoft 365 or a CSV, picks language and branding.
2. **Monthly drill:** AI writes a realistic, local phishing email based on common local scams (fake invoices, parcel notices, bank messages) from a safe library.
3. **2-minute lesson:** whoever clicks sees a short lesson explaining the warning signs they missed.
4. **Short monthly course:** everyone gets one micro-lesson per month with a 3-question quiz.
5. **Evidence report:** a PDF per client: who was trained, click rates over time, ready for NIS2 supplier questionnaires and insurers.

### MVP (first version)

- MSP workspace with client branding
- Staff import from Microsoft 365 or CSV
- Phishing template library in Swedish, Romanian and English
- Campaign scheduler and click tracking
- 2-minute lessons and quizzes
- Training evidence PDF

*Later:* Report-phish button for Outlook · German, Finnish, Polish content · Management training module for NIS2 boards · Link to Questionnaire Autopilot answers · Direct sales to small firms

### Pricing

- **MSP: $2/user/mo:** No minimum per client, white-label, all languages
- **Direct: $3/user/mo (min $29):** For firms without an MSP
- **Setup pack: $199 one-off:** We run the first drill and training for a client

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | template variations only | €5–15 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Sending domains and email service | Separate domains for simulations | €10–30 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–700** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| KnowBe4 | Market-leading training and phishing simulation | Seat minimums and longer contracts for tiny firms |
| Hoxhunt, Usecure, Riot, local players | Training platforms, some MSP-friendly and with Nordic languages | Strong competition; Romanian content and micro-firm pricing are thinner |
| Microsoft Attack Simulation Training | Built into Microsoft 365 E5 | Most small firms are on Business plans without it |
| **Us** | Local-language drills + micro-lessons + evidence PDF, resold by MSPs | No minimum, Romanian/Swedish first, built for NIS2 supplier evidence |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Run 3 consented manual tests with GoPhish for small firms in Sweden and Romania; ask 10 MSPs if they would resell.
- **Week 3–5:** Campaigns, templates, tracking, lessons.
- **Week 6–8:** MSP workspace, branding, evidence PDF, billing.
- **Week 9–12:** 3 MSP resellers live with 10+ clients each.

### Risks

- **Very crowded market:** Do not compete head-on; sell only through MSPs in markets where local content and no minimums matter.
- **Emails blocked by spam filters:** Provide clear allow-listing guides for Microsoft 365 and Google; test early.
- **Abuse for real phishing:** Verify domain ownership before campaigns, only send to imported staff of verified domains, log everything.
- **Low price per user:** Make the MSP the customer so one sale brings many clients.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Do any of your clients' customers or insurers ask for staff training evidence?
2. Which training tool do you offer today, and why don't small clients buy it?
3. In which language do your clients' staff prefer training?
4. Has a client fallen for a phishing or fake-invoice email in the last year?
5. Would you resell a $2/user tool with no minimum under your own brand?

### Scores

## 34. ShopAccess: accessibility checks and warning-letter defence for German-speaking online shops

**E-commerce** · €29–99 / month · score **39/70** · competition: high

> "We got a lawyer's warning letter about accessibility, a deadline of 10 days and a bill for almost €2,000. We didn't even know where to start."

Since June 2025 German online shops must be accessible under the BFSG law, and warning letters and market checks have started. ShopAccess tests the real checkout path every week, suggests code fixes, writes the accessibility statement and keeps a record that the shop is working on it.

**Why now:** The German Accessibility Strengthening Act (BFSG), which implements the European Accessibility Act, applies to online shops since 28 June 2025. Warning letters started in summer 2025, and the national market-surveillance body began active checks in 2026.

### The problem

**Thomas sells outdoor gear through a Shopware shop near Leipzig** with 11 staff and about **€3M in yearly sales**. In spring a lawyer's **warning letter** (Abmahnung) arrives: the shop's checkout cannot be used with a keyboard, product images have no text descriptions and there is no accessibility statement. He has **two weeks** to sign a cease-and-desist and pay the lawyer's fees.

His web agency offers a full audit for several thousand euros. An overlay widget salesperson promises "compliance in one line of code", but Thomas has read that overlays do not fix the real problems. He wants a weekly check of his actual checkout, clear fixes his agency can apply, a proper statement, and proof that he is improving.

### Evidence

- The BFSG accessibility duties for online retail started on 28 June 2025 [FashionUnited]
- Fines of up to €100,000 are possible, and warning letters give short deadlines of about 5–14 days [Händlerbund]
- Reported warning-letter costs range from about €1,784 plus a €490 analysis to €3,500–20,000 (vendor blogs, figures vary) [Xictron / Pragma-Code]
- The national market-surveillance body (MLBF) reportedly moved into active checks with its own spot tests in 2026 [Xictron]
- Almost all German online shops were found not to be accessible [Computerwoche]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Online shops in Germany and Austria above the micro-enterprise threshold (10+ staff or over €2M turnover) on Shopware, WooCommerce, JTL or Shopify.
- **Buyer:** The shop owner or e-commerce manager; agencies as resellers.
- **Start market:** Germany first (biggest warning-letter risk), then Austria and other EU countries with active enforcement such as Sweden.

### How it works

1. **Scripted checkout test:** a headless browser walks the real path: home, search, product, cart, checkout form, with keyboard only, and records every barrier.
2. **Plain-language results:** issues are grouped by page and severity, with screenshots and what a disabled customer experiences.
3. **AI fix suggestions:** for each issue, a concrete code or setting fix for Shopware/WooCommerce themes, plus bulk AI alt text for product images.
4. **Accessibility statement:** generates and updates the required statement (Barrierefreiheitserklärung) in German and English.
5. **Progress record and letter kit:** a dated log of tests and fixes, plus a checklist and partner-lawyer contact for answering a warning letter.

### MVP (first version)

- Playwright + axe-core crawler with scripted checkout path
- Keyboard-only and contrast checks
- Issue list with screenshots and severity
- AI fix suggestions for Shopware and WooCommerce
- Accessibility statement generator (German/English)
- Weekly re-test and progress log

*Later:* Bulk AI alt-text writer as a plugin · Agency white-label plan · Manual expert review add-on · Austria and Sweden versions · Product-data (PDF manuals) accessibility checks

### Pricing

| Agencies and audit consultants | Manual audits | Thousands of euros, one-off, no ongoing record |
| **Us** | Weekly checkout testing + shop-system fixes + statement + legal record | Honest (no overlay), cheap, built for German shop owners |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Scan 30 German shops' checkouts with a script; email each a free 1-page report in German; count replies. Find one German-speaking partner (agency or lawyer).
- **Week 3–5:** Crawler with checkout script, issue list, screenshots.
- **Week 6–8:** AI fixes, statement generator, weekly re-test, billing.
- **Week 9–12:** Launch through Shopware/WooCommerce agencies and merchant forums.

### Risks

- **Automated tests catch only part of the problems:** Never promise "compliance"; promise testing, fixes and a record of effort; offer a manual review add-on through partners.
- **German market, English-speaking founders:** German interface and templates from day 1; sell through German agencies and merchant associations; Jonatan handles English-speaking partners.
- **Crowded, VC-funded competitors:** Stay narrow: checkout path, Shopware/WooCommerce fixes, warning-letter kit.
- **B2B-only shops may not be covered:** Target consumer-facing shops; screen out B2B-only shops in onboarding.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Have you received an accessibility warning letter, or heard of one from a peer?
2. Who is responsible for accessibility in your shop today?
3. Have you tried an overlay or an audit? What happened?
4. What would make you trust an automated report?
5. Would €29–79 a month for weekly checks, fixes and a statement be worth it?

## 35. RenewKit: renewals, payments and a public member register for small trade associations

**Associations / trade bodies** · €49–149 / month · score **39/70** · competition: high

> "Renewal season is three months of me sending the same email to 600 members and checking bank statements by hand."

Small trade associations and professional bodies spend weeks every year chasing renewals by email and spreadsheet. RenewKit sends renewal reminders with payment links, keeps the public "find a member" register up to date automatically, and wins back lapsed members, without moving the association to a big membership system.

**Why now:** Membership software prices are rising and ownership is consolidating (Wild Apricot is now part of Momentive Software), which pushes small bodies to look for lighter options. Payment links and AI-written reminders make a "renewals only" tool simple to build.

### The problem

**Karin is the only staff member of a national association of 640 certified installers.** Members pay **€350 a year** and, in return, appear in the association's public "find a certified installer" list, which brings them customers. Renewals run from a spreadsheet, a bank account and a mail-merge.

Every spring Karin spends about **three months** chasing late payers, updating the website list by hand, and answering "did you get my payment?" emails. Some members who never paid still appear on the website; some who paid are missing. A full membership system like Wild Apricot would mean moving the website and everything else, and its prices keep rising. She wants renewals and the register to run themselves.

### Evidence

- Wild Apricot's entry plan is reported at $66/month for 100 contacts, up from $60 in April 2026; users report earlier price rises of 20% (2021) and 25% (2023) [CostBench / Hackceleration]
- Momentive Software acquired Personify, Wild Apricot's owner, on 6 January 2026 (reported by a competitor) [Raklet]
- In the Top 25 research, association renewals scored 44/70 at best, with crowded incumbents and volunteer buyers who churn when officers change [Top 25 research notes]
- Many small software categories for clubs and schools start around $15/month, which shows low willingness to pay at the bottom of the market [TutorBird via Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Trade associations, professional bodies and chambers with 100–3,000 business or professional members and 0–3 staff.
- **Buyer:** The executive director, secretary or treasurer.
- **Start market:** UK, Ireland, Nordics and other English-speaking associations found through public directories.

### How it works

1. **Import members:** upload the member spreadsheet; set membership types, prices and renewal dates.
2. **Automatic renewals:** reminders with a personal payment link (card, SEPA, invoice) go out before and after the due date; AI writes friendly reminder texts.
3. **Live member register:** an embeddable "find a member" list for the website that shows only paid-up members, with their certificates and regions.
4. **Lapsed-member win-back:** members who do not renew get a short sequence and the association sees revenue at risk.
5. **Self-service:** members update their details and download receipts and membership certificates.

### MVP (first version)

- Member import from CSV / Google Sheets
- Membership types and renewal dates
- Stripe payment links and invoice option
- Reminder sequences with AI-drafted text
- Embeddable public member register
- Revenue-at-risk dashboard

*Later:* Membership certificates with QR verification · Member CPD / training hours log · Newsletter digest writer · SEPA direct debit · Accounting exports (Xero, Fortnox)

### Pricing

- **Small: €49/mo:** Up to 300 members, reminders, register
- **Medium: €89/mo:** Up to 1,500 members, win-back, certificates
- **Large: €149/mo:** Up to 3,000 members, SEPA, accounting exports

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | reminder texts only | €0–10 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €400–600** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Wild Apricot, Glue Up, Raklet | Full membership management systems | Require moving website and data; rising prices |
| MemberPress, Memberful, Join It | Paid memberships for websites and creators | Built for content memberships, not trade-body registers |
| Spreadsheets + mail merge | What most small bodies use | Weeks of manual chasing; register out of date |
| **Us** | Renewals + payment links + live member register | Plugs into the existing website, no migration |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Jonatan emails 300 small associations from public directories offering a free "renewal audit" (lapsed members, revenue at risk). Goal: 10 calls, 3 pilots.
- **Week 3–5:** Import, membership types, payment links, reminders.
- **Week 6–8:** Public register widget, dashboard, self-service, billing.
- **Week 9–12:** Run one real renewal season with pilots; measure on-time payment rate.

### Risks

- **Low willingness to pay:** Target trade bodies with paid certifications and public registers, where members pay €200+ a year.
- **Volunteer officers change and churn:** Annual plans, and make RenewKit hold the member history so a new treasurer inherits it.
- **Crowded category:** Stay narrow: renewals and register only; integrate with what they have.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How do members renew today, and who chases late payers?
2. What share of members did not renew last year?
3. Do members get listed publicly? How do you keep that list up to date?
4. What tools do you use and what do they cost?
5. Would you pay €49–89 a month if renewals and the register ran themselves?

## 36. AskLocal: AI-search visibility reports in Swedish, Romanian and German for small SEO agencies

**Marketing agencies** · €79–199 / month per agency · score **38/70** · competition: high

> "My clients keep asking if they appear in ChatGPT. I have no way to show them, in Swedish, for the questions their customers actually ask."

Clients now ask their SEO agency "do we show up in ChatGPT?". Most AI-visibility tools track English prompts for big brands. AskLocal tracks real local-language questions for small local and B2B businesses and turns the results into a white-label monthly report the agency can sell.

**Why now:** Clicks from Google fall when AI answers appear, and agencies need a new service to sell. AI referrals are still small, so the honest product is a monitoring-and-advice add-on, not a traffic promise.

### The problem

**Linnea runs a 3-person SEO agency in Örebro** with **25 local clients**: dentists, plumbers, accountants, a furniture shop. Several clients saw Google clicks drop and ask whether ChatGPT and Google's AI answers mention them when someone asks **"bästa rörmokaren i Örebro"**.

The AI-visibility tools she tried are built for big brands, price per prompt, and work best in English. Linnea wants to track **20 local questions per client in Swedish**, see which competitors the AI names instead, get a short to-do list (reviews, structured data, local pages), and send a branded report every month as a paid add-on.

### Evidence

- Ahrefs found a 58% drop in top-position click-through rate for keywords that trigger AI Overviews (Dec 2025) [PPC Land]
- A study of 16,661 sites found Google organic clicks down 21.8% year on year in August 2026 [SEOcrawl]
- AI assistants still send only about 1% of traffic across industries, and ChatGPT sends roughly 75–83% of AI referrals [Digitaleer citing Conductor / SEOcrawl]
- 83 AI-visibility (GEO) tools were counted in 2026, and Shopify GEO apps start from $9/month [eCommerce Fastlane / Shopify App Store]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Small SEO and local-marketing agencies (1–20 staff) with local or small B2B clients.
- **Buyer:** The agency owner.
- **Start market:** Sweden and Romania (founders' languages), then Germany, Poland and other non-English markets.

### How it works

1. **Client setup:** the agency adds a client, its city, services and main competitors; AskLocal suggests 20 typical local questions in the local language.
2. **Weekly checks:** AskLocal asks those questions to ChatGPT, Gemini and Perplexity APIs and records which businesses are mentioned and how.
3. **Visibility score:** a simple share-of-mentions score per client and competitor, tracked over time.
4. **Fix list:** checks the client's website structured data, Google Business Profile basics and review counts, and lists concrete actions.
5. **White-label report:** a branded monthly PDF or link in the local language for the agency to send.

### MVP (first version)

- Agency workspace with client list
- Local-language question generator
- Weekly prompt runs on 2–3 AI APIs
- Mention extraction and visibility score
- Basic website structured-data check
- White-label monthly report

*Later:* Google AI Overviews tracking via a search API · Competitor comparison pages · Review-request add-on · German and Polish · Agency client portal

### Pricing

- **Starter: €79/mo:** 5 clients, 20 questions each, weekly checks
- **Agency: €199/mo:** 20 clients, white-label, competitor tracking
- **One-off audit: €199:** Single client snapshot to test demand

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | prompt runs across several AI APIs; grows with clients | €30–80 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–800** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Semrush AI toolkit, Ahrefs, Goodie, Profound | AI-visibility tracking for brands | English-first, priced for larger brands or per prompt |
| Many cheap new GEO apps (Citelix, Semly and dozens more) | AI-search checks from $9/month | Mostly e-commerce and English; market is crowded |
| Agencies doing manual checks | Typing questions into ChatGPT | Slow, not tracked over time, no report |
| **Us** | Local-language questions for small local clients, white-label | Cheap per agency, in Swedish/Romanian first |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Sell 5 one-off €199 AI-visibility audits to Swedish and Romanian agencies, done by hand with scripts.
- **Week 3–5:** Question generator, prompt runner, mention extraction.
- **Week 6–8:** Score, fix list, white-label report, billing.
- **Week 9–12:** Convert audit buyers to monthly plans; measure cost per client.

### Risks

- **Extremely crowded and fast-moving:** Validate with paid one-off audits before building; stay local-language and agency-only.
- **AI answers are noisy and change daily:** Show trends over several runs, not single answers; be honest in reports.
- **API costs grow with every client:** Price per client tier; limit questions; cache runs.
- **Weak proven willingness to pay:** Treat as a cheap add-on agencies resell at a markup, not a core service.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Do your clients ask about ChatGPT or Google AI answers? How often?
2. What do you deliver to them today on this topic?
3. Which tools have you tried, and why did or didn't you keep them?
4. Would you resell a monthly AI-visibility report under your brand? At what price?
5. Which local questions would matter most for your typical client?

## 37. FuneralDesk: family portal, approvals and AI-drafted notices for independent UK funeral directors

**Funeral services** · £99–249 / month · score **38/70** · competition: medium

> "Every family needs the same 40 details from us, at the worst moment of their life, and we take them over five phone calls."

Independent funeral directors spend hours on the phone collecting family details, choices and approvals, then writing notices and service sheets. FuneralDesk gives families a gentle online form and approval page and drafts the obituary, notices and order of service, without replacing the firm's main system.

**Why now:** Scotland's Funeral Director Code of Practice, approved by the Scottish Parliament in January 2024, is statutory, and a licensing scheme and inspection regime are planned. Expectations on written estimates and records are rising across the UK.

### The problem

**Margaret runs a family-owned funeral home in Fife** with 7 staff and about **180 funerals a year**. For each one, her team collects the same details over several calls: names, dates, music, readings, flowers, newspaper notice wording, and written approval of the price estimate. Notices and service sheets are typed by hand, and errors in a name or date cause real hurt.

Scotland's statutory **Code of Practice for funeral directors** also expects clear prices, written estimates and good records. Margaret's case-management software handles bookings and invoices but not the back-and-forth with families. She wants a calm, simple page for families to fill in at their own pace, one-click approvals, and drafts she only needs to check.

### Evidence

- Scotland's Funeral Director Code of Practice was approved by the Scottish Parliament on 31 January 2024 under the Burial and Cremation (Scotland) Act 2016, with a grace period to comply [Scottish Government]
- The code applies to all funeral directors working in Scotland, wherever the business is based, ahead of a planned licensing scheme [Scottish Government impact assessment]
- Funeral software prices range widely: Osiris about $100/month, Halcyon about 295/month, Parting Pro $469/month, and per-funeral pricing from about £5 (aggregator listings) [Software Advice / Capterra]
- The only revenue evidence for a funeral add-on is one unverified anecdote of $29k MRR at $650 per location, sold via association newsletters [Medium via Top 25 report]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Independent funeral directors with 1–5 branches and 80–800 funerals a year.
- **Buyer:** The owner or senior funeral director.
- **Start market:** Scotland and the rest of the UK, then Ireland and Australia.

### How it works

1. **Gentle family page:** the funeral director sends one link; family members fill in details, choices and memories at their own pace, from any phone.
2. **Estimate and approvals:** the written price estimate and key choices are shown clearly and approved with one tap, with a dated record.
3. **AI drafts:** obituary, newspaper and online notices, and the order of service are drafted from the family's answers, for staff to edit.
4. **Checklist per funeral:** a simple task list (registrar, crematorium, flowers, music) with who does what.
5. **Records:** everything is stored per funeral, ready if a regulator or family asks.

### MVP (first version)

- Family intake page with save-and-continue
- Written estimate display and approval with timestamp
- AI-drafted notice and obituary
- Order-of-service template export (PDF)
- Per-funeral checklist
- Simple staff dashboard

*Later:* Online payments and deposits · Integration with UK funeral case-management systems · Memorial page for sharing · Irish and Australian versions · Price-list page that follows UK rules

### Pricing

- **Single branch: £99/mo:** Up to 250 funerals a year, family page, drafts
- **Multi-branch: £249/mo:** Up to 5 branches, payments, integrations

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | a few drafts per funeral | €5–15 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Association event or newsletter ad | Optional, if pilots go well | €0–200 one-off |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €400–750** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Funeral case-management systems (UK and US vendors) | Bookings, cases, invoices | Family communication and drafting are weak or missing; replacing them is a big decision |
| Website and memorial providers | Websites, online notices, tribute pages | Public-facing, not the private intake and approval flow |
| Phone, paper and Word | What most independents use | Slow and error-prone |
| **Us** | Family intake + approvals + AI drafts as an add-on | No migration, built for small independents |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** 15 phone/video interviews with funeral directors from association directories; test if family page + drafts are worth £99+.
- **Week 3–5:** Family page, approvals, checklist.
- **Week 6–8:** AI drafts, PDF order of service, billing.
- **Week 9–12:** 2–3 pilot firms; ask for introductions within their association.

### Risks

2. How many calls and emails does it take to collect all the details for one funeral?
3. Who writes notices and orders of service today, and how long does it take?
4. What software do you pay for, and what is missing?
5. Would you pay £99 a month to save 2–3 hours per funeral?

## 38. EUDR Lite: deforestation due-diligence statements for small Nordic importers of wood products, coffee and cocoa

**Import / trade compliance** · €49–149 / month · score **37/70** · competition: medium

> "We import teak furniture from three small workshops. Now the EU wants GPS coordinates of the forest. Where do I even start?"

From 30 June 2027 small EU companies that first import wood products, coffee, cocoa, rubber and similar goods must file due-diligence statements under the EU Deforestation Regulation. EUDR Lite collects supplier location data, checks the risk and prepares the statement for the EU system at a flat, small-firm price.

**Why now:** The Commission's simplification review of May 2026 did not reopen the law. Large and medium operators must comply from 30 December 2026, and micro and small operators from 30 June 2027. Small importers who first place goods on the market are now planning for that deadline.

### The problem

**Mats owns a 9-person company in Gothenburg** that imports garden furniture made of tropical wood from **three workshops in Indonesia** and specialty coffee from **two co-operatives in Colombia**. Under the EU Deforestation Regulation he is the "operator" who first places these goods on the EU market, so from **30 June 2027** he must file a due-diligence statement with supplier location data and a risk assessment.

He has read about the rule being delayed and changed twice, and does not know what is still required. Software he found charges **per supplier per month plus a setup fee of thousands of euros**, built for large importers. He needs a simple tool: send each supplier a link, collect locations and documents, see the risk, and file correctly.

### Evidence

- The Commission published its EUDR simplification review on 4 May 2026 without reopening the legal text; changes go through a delegated act [JD Supra / Arendt]
- Large and medium operators must comply from 30 December 2026, micro and small operators from 30 June 2027 [PwC Belgium]
- The Commission estimates its simplification measures cut annual compliance costs by about 75% [COM(2026) 191]
- Only the operator that first places a product on the market files a statement; micro and small primary operators make a one-off simplified declaration [EY / Linklaters]
- Existing tools: TraceX about €40–50 per supplier per month plus about €5k setup, LiveEO €15 per extra supplier per month, FlyPix €25–62.50/month (Top 25 notes) [Top 25 research notes]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** Micro and small EU importers that first place wood products, furniture, coffee, cocoa, chocolate, rubber goods or leather on the EU market, with 3–30 suppliers.
- **Buyer:** The owner or purchasing/quality manager.
- **Start market:** Sweden and the Nordics (Jonatan), then the Baltics, Netherlands and Germany.

### How it works

1. **Am I in scope?:** a short check tells the importer whether they are an operator, which products are covered, and their deadline.
2. **Supplier link:** each supplier gets a simple link (in their language) to give plot GPS points or a map pin, or a postal address where the simplified rules allow, plus documents.
3. **AI document check:** certificates, harvest permits and invoices are read and matched to products and quantities.
4. **Risk checklist:** country risk level, deforestation-date check and missing items are shown in plain language.
5. **Statement ready to file:** a draft due-diligence statement with a step-by-step guide for the EU information system, and records kept for five years.

### MVP (first version)

- Scope and deadline checker
- Supplier invitation link with map-pin and file upload
- AI extraction from supplier documents
- Country risk and checklist view
- Draft statement and filing guide
- Five-year record archive

*Later:* Direct submission to the EU information system via its interface · Satellite deforestation check from a data partner · Customs-broker partner plan · German and Dutch versions · Combine with SafeListing product-data checks

### Pricing

- **Small: €49/mo:** Up to 10 suppliers, unlimited statements
- **Plus: €149/mo:** Up to 40 suppliers, several product groups, broker access
- **Setup help: €300 one-off:** We onboard your suppliers with you

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | Hetzner or Supabase/Vercel free tiers, EU hosting | €10–25 / month |
| AI API (OpenAI / Claude) | ~€0.01 per supplier document | €5–20 / month |
| Email sending | Resend / Postmark | €0–15 / month |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €400–650** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| TraceX, LiveEO, osapiens, IntegrityNext | EUDR traceability and due-diligence platforms | Per-supplier fees, setup charges, built for larger operators |
| Coolset, FlyPix and other mid-priced tools | EUDR modules and satellite checks | Closer on price; still broader than a 5-supplier importer needs |
| Consultants and customs brokers | Do the paperwork by hand | Charged per shipment or per hour, no reusable supplier data |
| **Us** | Supplier link + AI document check + statement draft | Flat price for importers with 3–30 suppliers |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** Jonatan calls 30 Nordic importers of furniture, timber and coffee; ask if they know they are operators and what they have done. Goal: 3 paid pre-orders.
- **Week 3–5:** Scope checker, supplier link, map picker, document upload.
- **Week 6–8:** AI extraction, risk checklist, draft statement, billing.
- **Week 9–12:** Pilots file test statements; adjust to the latest EU guidance.

### Risks

- **The rule changes again:** Keep the product small and data-focused (supplier locations and documents) so it survives changes; follow EU guidance closely.
- **One-time rush, then churn:** Annual plans; recurring value from new shipments, new suppliers and the five-year record archive.
- **Small, specialist market:** Partner with customs brokers and importer associations who already serve these firms.
- **Suppliers do not respond:** Make the supplier page work on a phone, in their language, with a map pin instead of GPS skills.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Which EUDR-covered goods do you import, and from how many suppliers?
2. Do you know whether you are the "operator" who must file?
3. What have you done so far to prepare for June 2027?
4. Who helps you today: a consultant, a broker, software? What does it cost?
5. Would €49–149 a month for supplier data collection and ready statements be worth it?

## 39. LogWatch: patient-record access log reviews for small Swedish clinics

**Healthcare** · €79–249 / month · score **44/70** · competition: low

> "We know we should check the logs every month. Honestly, nobody has time, and the export is 40,000 rows."

Swedish care providers must check regularly who opened which patient record. Small private clinics (physio, dental, psychology, rehab) often do it by hand or not at all. LogWatch imports the access log from the journal system, flags suspicious look-ups and keeps the proof the regulator asks for.

**Why now:** Socialstyrelsen's rules on access control and log follow-up (HSLF-FS 2016:40) were amended by HSLF-FS 2025:57, in force from 1 February 2026. IMY has already fined care providers millions of kronor for weak access control and log follow-up, and Sweden's new cybersecurity law (NIS2) has raised security attention in healthcare.

### The problem

**Karin runs a private physiotherapy and rehab clinic in Gothenburg with 14 staff.** The Patient Data Act and Socialstyrelsen's rules say she must do **systematic log follow-up**: check that staff only open the records of patients they actually treat. Her journal system can export an access log, but it is a **huge Excel file** with user IDs, patient numbers and timestamps. Twice a year her quality manager picks a few rows at random, writes "no problems found" and saves a Word file.

Then a patient complains that a receptionist read her record after a dispute between them. Karin has to show the regulator (IMY) her **written routine, her checks and her results**, and she realises the checks never looked for this kind of access at all. She would happily pay a small monthly fee for a tool that runs the checks every month, shows only the few cases that need a human look and writes the report for her.

### Evidence

- IMY's review of journal systems led to a 30 million SEK fine against Capio S:t Göran (2020) for inadequate access control [Legiscope / IMY]
- MedHelp was fined 12 million SEK in 2021, Region Dalarna 200,000 SEK in 2023, and a small private specialist clinic 20,000 SEK for exposed patient data [IMY / Läkartidningen]
- HSLF-FS 2016:40 (systematic log checks) was amended by HSLF-FS 2025:57, with the latest version from 1 February 2026 [Legiscope]
- IMY publishes a checklist for systematic log follow-up based on the Patient Data Act (2008:355) and Socialstyrelsen's rules [IMY]
- Big regions use LogPoint or Inera's extended log service; the City of Gothenburg uses an Excel-based random sampling tool, which shows how manual this still is [Region Jämtland Härjedalen / Inera / Göteborgs stad]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** private care providers in Sweden with 5–100 staff: physiotherapy, dental, psychology, rehab, occupational health, private specialist clinics.
- **Buyer:** clinic owner or the person responsible for quality and data protection (often the operations manager).
- **Start market:** Sweden (Jonatan sells in Swedish, the product UI starts in Swedish and English). Later Norway and Finland, which have similar rules on patient-record logging (to be checked).

### How it works

1. **Upload or connect:** the clinic uploads the monthly access-log export from its journal system (CSV/Excel), or we pull it automatically where the system has an API.
2. **Pseudonymise first:** personal identity numbers are hashed in the browser before upload, so we never store real patient IDs.
3. **Run risk rules:** flag look-ups of colleagues' records, patients with the same surname or address as the user, protected identities, access outside working hours and records with no booked visit.
4. **Human review:** the quality manager sees only the 5–20 flagged cases, marks each as OK or "investigate" and writes a short note.
5. **Report and routine:** LogWatch writes the monthly log-review report and keeps a ready routine document that matches IMY's checklist, ready to show in an inspection.

### MVP (first version)

- Clinic workspace and login with 2FA
- CSV/Excel log import with column mapping
- In-browser pseudonymisation of personal numbers
- 6–8 risk rules with clear explanations
- Review screen (OK / investigate + note)
- Monthly PDF report and routine template

*Later:* Direct API connection to the most common journal systems · Staff roster import to match working hours · Email reminders when the monthly check is due · Norway and Finland rule packs · Access-rights review (who should have access to what)

### Pricing

- **Small: €79/mo:** Up to 15 staff, monthly review, report and routine template
- **Clinic: €149/mo:** Up to 50 staff, 2 sites, roster matching
- **Group: €249/mo:** Several clinics, one dashboard, API connection

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| AI API | Only for report wording; analysis is rule-based | €5–20 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–750** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Inera extended log service | Log follow-up service for organisations connected to Inera | Built for regions and municipalities, not a 10-person private clinic |
| LogPoint and other SIEM tools | Enterprise log analysis | Expensive, needs IT staff, no ready routine for patient-record law |
| Journal system log views | Raw lists of who opened which record | No risk flags and no report; someone still has to read thousands of rows |
| Excel random sampling | What many organisations do today | Misses the risky cases and takes hours |
| **Us** | Upload log → risk flags → review → report | Built for small private clinics, €79–249/mo, 30 minutes a month |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Jonatan calls 20 private clinics. Victor reviews 3 real (pseudonymised) log exports by hand with a script and shows the findings.
- **Week 3–5:** Import, column mapping, pseudonymisation and the first risk rules for the 2 journal systems the pilot clinics use.
- **Week 6–7:** Review screen, PDF report, routine template, 2FA and data processing agreement.
- **Week 8:** 3 pilot clinics run a real monthly review; ask them to pay from month 2.

### Risks

- **Every journal system exports logs differently:** Start with the 2–3 systems the first 10 customers use; column mapping handles small differences.
- **Patient data is very sensitive:** Hash personal numbers in the browser, host in Sweden/EU, sign a data processing agreement and keep logs only as long as the clinic needs.
- **Small market:** Sweden has many private care providers (count to be checked); expand to Norway and Finland once the rule engine works.
- **Clinics only act after a complaint:** Sell with IMY fine cases and a free one-off log check that usually finds something.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How do you do log follow-up today, and how often?
2. Who does it, and how many hours does it take each time?
3. Can your journal system export an access log? Can you show us one (pseudonymised)?
4. Have you ever had a complaint or incident about someone reading a record they should not?
5. If a tool did the monthly check and wrote the report for €79–149, would you buy it?

### Scores

pain: 6 · willingnessToPay: 6 · lowCompetition: 7 · easyToBuild: 6 · easyToSell: 6 · skillFit: 9 · globalPotential: 4 · **total 44/70**

## 40. AllergenSync: allergen records that follow supplier changes, for restaurants

**Hospitality** · €29–99 / month per site · score **43/70** · competition: medium

> "The wholesaler sent a different brand of pesto. Nobody checked the label. It had cashews."

Restaurants must give correct information on the 14 major allergens, and the UK food regulator now recommends written allergen information for all loose food. The weak point is supplier substitutions. AllergenSync reads supplier spec sheets and invoices, keeps each dish's allergen matrix up to date and warns the kitchen when a substitute changes an allergen.

**Why now:** The FSA published best-practice guidance in March 2025 saying written allergen information should always be available for non-prepacked food. FSA incidents rose 14% in 2025/26, with allergen incidents rising fastest. AI can now read supplier spec sheets and invoice lines cheaply, which makes automatic change detection possible.

### The problem

**Tom runs two neighbourhood restaurants in Bristol with a menu that changes every season.** His allergen matrix is a laminated A4 sheet made last spring. Since then the wholesaler has **swapped five products** when items were out of stock, and the kitchen has changed three recipes. Nobody updated the sheet, because finding the spec sheet for each product takes an evening.

One Friday a server tells a guest with a nut allergy that the pesto pasta is safe. The substitute pesto contains **cashew nuts**. The guest notices a reaction early and nobody is badly hurt, but Tom gets a visit from the council's food officer and realises his records would not survive a serious case. He needs something that notices product changes for him, not another spreadsheet.

### Evidence

- FSA guidance (March 2025): written allergen information should always be available for non-prepacked food, plus a conversation with the customer [FSA]
- FSA incidents rose from 1,825 to 2,073 in 2025/26 (+14%); allergen incidents rose faster than any other category [Food Management Today]
- The FSA handled 242 allergen-related incidents and issued 58 allergen alerts in the 2025 period [Food Manufacture]
- A consultancy analysis says 597 of 1,036 UK food alerts in 2016–2021 (57.6%) were about allergens; primary source not confirmed [Human Focus]
- Kafoodle starts around £20/month per feature; its restaurant bundle is listed from £60/month plus setup [Software Advice / FitGap]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** independent restaurants, cafés, pubs with food, small hotel kitchens and caterers with 1–10 sites.
- **Buyer:** owner, head chef or operations manager of a small group.
- **Start market:** UK and Ireland in English (same 14 allergens as EU law), then the Nordics and the rest of the EU.

### How it works

1. **Import the menu:** upload the current menu and recipes (photo, PDF or spreadsheet); AI drafts the ingredient list for each dish.
2. **Match the products:** upload supplier spec sheets or product photos; AI reads the allergens and "may contain" lines and links each product to dishes.
3. **Watch for changes:** forward supplier invoices and delivery notes; when a product code or brand changes, AllergenSync asks for the new spec sheet and recalculates.
4. **Warn the team:** if a dish's allergens change, the chef gets an alert and must approve before the matrix updates.
5. **Publish:** printable allergen matrix, staff tablet view and a QR menu page for guests, all with a change history for inspections.

### MVP (first version)

- Menu and recipe import with AI ingredient draft
- Supplier spec sheet reading (14 allergens + may contain)
- Allergen matrix per dish
- Invoice forwarding and product change detection
- Chef approval of changes
- Printable matrix and QR guest page

*Later:* Integrations with wholesalers' product data · POS menu sync · Staff allergen training quiz and records · Multi-site templates · Nordic and EU languages

### Pricing

- **Single site: €29/mo:** 1 menu, matrix, QR page
- **Watch: €59/mo:** Invoice change detection and alerts
- **Group: €99/mo:** Up to 5 sites, shared recipes

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| AI + OCR API | Spec sheets and invoices | €15–40 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–750** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Kafoodle | Recipe, allergen and nutrition management | Charged per feature; chef must keep data current by hand |
| Nutritics | Nutrition and allergen analysis for food businesses | Strong for labelling and larger caterers; heavier than a small restaurant needs |
| Paper / Excel matrix | The default in most independents | Goes out of date at the first supplier substitution |
| **Us** | Spec sheets + invoices → live allergen matrix | Notices supplier changes automatically; €29–99/mo |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Visit 15 restaurants. Build 3 allergen matrices by hand with Claude from their real spec sheets and invoices.
- **Week 3–5:** Menu import, spec sheet reading, matrix and approval flow.
- **Week 6–7:** Invoice forwarding, change detection, printable matrix and QR page.
- **Week 8:** 5 pilot sites live; first invoices.

### Risks

- **Liability if AI reads a label wrong:** AI drafts, the chef approves; every allergen links to the source document; terms make the business responsible for final checks.
- **Restaurants hate admin and churn fast:** Make invoice forwarding the only habit; price low; sell to small groups where one manager covers many sites.
- **Crowded recipe-software market:** Do not compete on costing or nutrition; own the "supplier change" problem and integrate later.
- **Spec sheets are hard to get:** Accept product photos of the label as a fallback; build a shared product library across customers over time.

### Questions to ask customers

1. When did you last update your allergen matrix, and who did it?
2. How often does your supplier send a substitute product?
3. How do you find out what is in a substitute today?
4. Has an allergen question or incident ever worried you, or come up in an inspection?
5. Would €29–59 a month for automatic change alerts be worth it?

## 41. SpecReply: AI fills retailer spec sheets and supplier questionnaires for small food producers

**Food production** · €49–149 / month · score **43/70** · competition: low

> "Every new stockist sends their own 12-page spec form. It's the same questions in a different order, and it takes me two days."

Small food and drink producers who sell to retailers, wholesalers and caterers must fill in long product specification sheets and supplier approval questionnaires, each in a different format. SpecReply stores the producer's product and site data once and fills each new form with AI.

**Why now:** Retail and foodservice buyers keep tightening supplier checks after allergen and labelling problems; the FSA's 2025–26 retail survey again found undeclared allergens. AI can now read old forms and labels and fill new templates in Excel, Word or PDF. The same engine as security questionnaires works here, for a buyer that big spec platforms ignore.

### The problem

**Anna makes artisan sauces and chutneys with 9 staff.** A regional supermarket chain wants to list four of her products. Before the first order, their buyer sends a **supplier approval questionnaire**, a spec sheet template for each product (ingredients, allergens, nutrition, shelf life, packaging, origin) and a request for her SALSA certificate and HACCP summary.

Anna has all the answers somewhere: in her labels, recipe files and last audit. But each form uses its own layout, so she spends **two full days** copying and checking. A month later a farm-shop wholesaler and a café chain send their own versions. She would pay a monthly fee to answer each one in an hour and keep every answer the same.

### Evidence

- A comparative review counted 103 clauses in SALSA and 158 in BRCGS START!, the two UK schemes built for small food businesses [IJMAR]
- In that review, supplier approval was among the "clauses of concern" small manufacturers found difficult [IJMAR]
- IFST/SALSA guidance gives an example questionnaire for approving raw material and packaging suppliers, so every link in the chain sends one [IFST]
- The FSA's 2025–26 Retail Surveillance Survey tested 845 products and found labelling problems including undeclared allergens [Food Safety Magazine]
- Specification platforms such as Specright publish no prices and offer no free trial, a sign they target larger firms [Software Advice]
- No survey of hours spent on spec forms was found; this must be validated in interviews [own research]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** small food and drink producers (5–50 staff) selling to retailers, wholesalers or foodservice: sauces, bakery, dairy, drinks, snacks, meat.
- **Buyer:** founder or technical/quality manager.
- **Start market:** UK and Ireland (SALSA, BRCGS), then the Nordics and other EU countries in English; later the US and Australia.

### How it works

1. **Build the product library:** upload labels, recipes, old spec sheets, certificates and the HACCP summary; AI creates one structured record per product and one for the site.
2. **Drop in a new form:** upload the customer's spec sheet or questionnaire (Excel, Word or PDF).
3. **AI fills it:** each field is filled from the library, with the source and a confidence colour; missing data is listed.
4. **Review and export:** the quality manager checks yellow and red fields and exports the form in its original format.
5. **Stay consistent:** when a recipe or certificate changes, SpecReply shows which customers got the old answer and need an update.

### MVP (first version)

- Product and site library from uploaded documents
- Form import (Excel, Word, PDF)
- AI field filling with sources
- Review screen
- Export in original format
- Certificate expiry reminders

*Later:* Change alerts to customers who received old data · Shared portal for buyers · Nutrition calculation · EU languages · Integration with labelling tools

- **Growth: €99/mo:** Up to 75 products, unlimited forms
