## 42. VetClear: CMA price-list, estimate and prescription compliance for independent vets

**Veterinary** · £49–149 / month · score **42/70** · competition: medium

> "The corporate groups have whole teams on the CMA rules. It's me and my practice manager, after evening surgery."

After its market investigation, the UK competition authority (CMA) requires vet practices to publish price lists, give written estimates for bigger treatments and tell owners they can get a written prescription. Independent practices have no compliance team. VetClear builds the price page, estimate templates and client notices, and checks that the practice stays compliant.

**Why now:** The CMA published its final report on 24 March 2026, with remedies phased in through an Order and an RCVS undertaking; final orders and guidance were due in autumn 2026, with smaller businesses given extra time. Independents must be ready in 2026–2027 without a compliance team.

### The problem

**Priya owns an independent small-animal practice with 3 vets and 6 nurses.** The CMA's final report means she must publish a **standard price list** on her website, give a **written estimate for treatment of £500 or more** and update the client in writing if the cost will go over by more than 20%. Her team must also tell clients they can get a **written prescription** and buy medicines elsewhere.

Her practice software can print an invoice, but not a CMA-style price list or an estimate that tracks the 20% rule. She downloads guidance, makes a Word template and hopes the nurses use it. She would pay a monthly fee for one place that keeps her published prices, estimates and notices right, and shows her what is missing before a complaint does.

### Evidence

- CMA final report on vet services for household pets published in March 2026; remedies implemented through an Order on vet businesses and an RCVS undertaking [GOV.UK]
- Practices must publish a price list covering consultations, vaccinations, written prescription fees and common procedures [Whito / Pharmacy2U]
- Written estimate and itemised bill for treatment expected to cost £500 or more incl. VAT; written update if likely to exceed the estimate by more than 20% [CMA remedies coverage: Improve International / Price Bailey]
- Prescription fee cap: the provisional decision proposed £16; later coverage cites £21 for the first item; check the final Order [Bird & Bird / Money to the Masses]
- Over 60% of practices are owned by six large groups, so independents compete with companies that have compliance teams (industry commentary) [Agilio]
- Smaller businesses get longer to comply than large groups [BVA]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** independent vet practices in the UK (1–5 sites), small-animal and mixed.
- **Buyer:** practice owner or practice manager.
- **Start market:** UK only (the rules are UK-specific); later, pricing transparency tools for vets in other countries if regulators follow.

### How it works

1. **Price list builder:** the practice enters or imports prices; VetClear produces a public price page in the required categories and a version for the RCVS comparison listing.
2. **Estimate templates:** itemised written estimates for treatments of £500+, with aftercare lines and a 20% overrun alert that prompts a written update.
3. **Client notices:** ready text for prescription rights, ownership disclosure and fees, for the website, reception and emails.
4. **Compliance checklist:** a live checklist of each CMA duty with deadlines for small businesses, and a monthly reminder to review prices.
5. **Evidence pack:** a dated history of published prices and estimates, ready if a client complains.

### MVP (first version)

- Practice profile and price import (CSV)
- Public price page (embeddable)
- Written estimate builder with 20% overrun alert
- Notice templates
- CMA duty checklist with deadlines
- PDF evidence pack

*Later:* Integration with common practice management systems · AI price-list import from old PDFs · Client-facing estimate e-sign · Benchmarks vs local practices · Multi-site groups

- **UK-only, smaller market:** Price for a quick, compact win; the document engine reuses elsewhere.
- **Vets are hard to reach by phone:** Use practice-manager groups, independent vet associations and VDS/SPVS events.

## 43. AgentAML: anti-money-laundering files for small estate agencies before the EU rules of July 2027

**Real estate** · €59–199 / month · score **42/70** · competition: medium

> "We are three brokers. We know we must do KYC, but our money-laundering routine is a Word file from 2019."

From 10 July 2027 the EU Anti-Money Laundering Regulation applies directly to estate and letting agents in every member state. Small agencies must keep a written risk assessment, policies, customer checks and records. AgentAML gives them a simple file per deal, ID and ownership checks, and a ready policy pack.

**Why now:** The EU AML Regulation (AMLR) applies from 10 July 2027 with one rulebook for all member states, and AMLA is consulting on detailed technical standards in 2026. Supervisors are already tightening: in the UK, HMRC fined 170 estate agency businesses in six months of 2025, and Sweden's FMI raised the sector's risk rating to high.

### The problem

**Erik owns a 5-person estate agency in Uppsala.** Swedish brokers already fall under money-laundering rules, and the supervisor (FMI) has just raised the sector's risk level to **high** after finding large knowledge gaps in agencies. Erik's routine is an old Word document, and buyer checks are a photo of an ID card in the deal folder.

When the EU regulation applies in **July 2027**, he must show a documented risk assessment, a named AML officer, customer due diligence on **both sides of each deal** (in many countries), checks of beneficial owners when a company buys, and refresh dates for each customer. He wants a tool that makes each deal's AML file as easy as the contract.

### Evidence

- The AMLR starts to apply to estate agents on 10 July 2027 [Schoenherr / Mondaq]
- Letting intermediaries are covered for rents of at least €10,000 a month; sales agents with no minimum [AML Watcher]
- Obliged estate agents will need to appoint AML officers and put risk assessments and CDD policies in writing [KPMG Germany]
- Sweden's FMI thematic review found large knowledge gaps in broker firms; the sector risk was raised from "significant" to "high" [FMI / Fastighetsmäklarförbundet]
- HMRC fined 170 UK estate agency businesses £835,842 between April and September 2025 [Property Industry Eye]
- HMRC proposed raising the annual AML supervision fee from £300 to £400 per branch [The Negotiator]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** estate and letting agencies with 1–30 agents.
- **Buyer:** agency owner or the named AML officer.
- **Start market:** Sweden (Jonatan's network, sector already under pressure), then Baltics, Romania and other EU markets in English; UK later (crowded with ID-check vendors).

### How it works

1. **Policy pack:** a guided interview creates the agency-wide risk assessment, AML policy and officer appointment in plain language, ready for the supervisor.
2. **File per deal:** each sale or letting gets an AML file: parties, roles, source of funds questions and risk level.
3. **Customer checks:** send the buyer or seller a link for ID verification (via an EU eID/ID provider), PEP and sanctions screening, and company owner lookup.
4. **Risk and refresh:** rules set the risk level, ask for extra checks when needed and schedule refresh dates.
5. **Report and audit:** red-flag notes, a suspicious-activity checklist and a full audit trail exportable for inspections.

### MVP (first version)

- Policy and risk assessment generator
- Deal files with parties and roles
- Customer link for ID verification (one provider)
- PEP / sanctions screening via API
- Risk rules and refresh reminders
- Audit export (PDF)

*Later:* Beneficial-owner registry lookups per country · Integration with agency CRMs · Staff AML training records · More countries and languages · Source-of-funds document reading with AI

### Pricing

- **Solo: €59/mo:** 1–3 agents, policy pack, 10 deal files a month
- **Agency: €129/mo:** Up to 15 agents, unlimited files
- **Group: €199/mo:** Several offices; ID checks billed per use

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| ID + screening APIs | Pay per check, passed on to customers | €20–60 / month |
| AI API | Policy drafting | €5–15 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €500–800** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Thirdfort, Credas, SmartSearch | UK ID and AML checks for property | UK-focused, per-check pricing; not an EU-wide policy and file tool |
| Bank-grade AML suites (e.g. Trapets) | Transaction monitoring and KYC | Built for financial firms, too heavy for a 5-person agency |
| Lawyers and consultants | Write policies | One-off document; no deal files or reminders |
| **Us** | Policy pack + deal files + checks | Small-agency price, built for the July 2027 EU rulebook |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Jonatan interviews 15 Swedish agencies; Victor reads FMI guidance and the AMLR text and drafts the policy interview.
- **Week 3–5:** Policy generator, deal files, risk rules.
- **Week 6–8:** ID verification and screening integrations, audit export, security review.

### Risks

- **National details still differ:** Start in one country, keep country rules in config, follow AMLA standards as they are finalised.
- **Trust: AML is serious:** Partner with a compliance lawyer for templates; clear disclaimers; strong security.
- **Agency CRMs add AML modules:** Integrate rather than compete; focus on agencies whose CRM lacks it.
- **Language barrier outside Sweden:** Launch in English and Swedish; add languages via AI translation reviewed by local partners.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How do you document AML checks for each deal today?
2. When did you last update your risk assessment and policy?
3. Has the supervisor ever asked to see your AML routine?
4. How do you check buyers who are companies or foreign nationals?
5. Would €59–129 a month for a ready policy and deal files be worth it?

## 44. RedWork: follow-up on declined repair work for independent garages

**Automotive** · €49–149 / month · score **42/70** · competition: medium

> "We told him the brake pads were down to 2 mm. He said next month. We never heard from him again."

Garages find urgent ("red") and advisory ("amber") faults during inspections, but most drivers leave without approving all of it. RedWork sends timed, personal follow-ups with the photos and video from the inspection, offers a booking slot and tracks how much declined work comes back.

**Why now:** Drivers are under cost pressure: a 2026 analysis found that in 70% of health checks with red items, drivers did not approve all urgent work, mostly because of cost. Garages already take photos and videos in digital health checks, and WhatsApp/SMS messaging plus AI-written messages make personal follow-up cheap.

### The problem

**Mehmet runs an independent garage with 4 bays and 6 technicians.** Every day his team does health checks and finds red and amber items: worn tyres, brake pads, leaking shock absorbers. Most customers say "not today". The work is written in the job card and then forgotten. His garage software can send a yearly MOT reminder, but nothing for **declined work**.

He tried calling customers back, but nobody has time on a busy day. A simple system that sends the right message at the right time (pay-day, two weeks later, before winter) with the **photo of the worn part** and a link to book could bring back a few jobs a week. At an average of **£250 per job**, that pays for the tool many times over.

### Evidence

- In 70% of vehicle health checks with red items, drivers did not approve all urgent work; 46% said they could not afford it (vendor data) [Bumper / AutoBI]
- 47% of affected drivers did not approve advisory (amber) items (vendor data) [Bumper / AutoBI]
- UK franchised dealers left an estimated £480 million of red work unsold in 2016, about £99,200 per dealer [AM Online]
- A dealer case study reported an 86% rise in amber work approved after automated follow-up (vendor case study) [CitNOW]
- UK garage management tools with digital inspections cost about £25–149 a month, e.g. £149/month flat for EZ Garage and Garage Desk [GetApp / GarageWire]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** independent garages and small chains (2–15 technicians), tyre and service centres.
- **Buyer:** garage owner or service manager.
- **Start market:** UK (red/amber health-check culture), then Ireland, Nordics, Australia and the rest of the EU.

### How it works

1. **Capture declined items:** import declined lines from the garage software (CSV, email report or API) or let the technician tap them in the app with a photo.
2. **Plan the follow-up:** rules choose the timing: red items after 3–7 days, amber items before the season or the next service.
3. **Personal messages:** AI writes a short, friendly SMS/WhatsApp message in the garage's voice, with the photo or video and a booking link.
4. **Easy booking:** the driver picks a slot; the garage gets the job with the original inspection notes.
5. **Recovery dashboard:** shows declined value, recovered jobs and money earned each month.

### MVP (first version)

- Declined-item import (CSV / email report)
- Quick mobile entry with photo
- Follow-up rules and schedule
- AI-written SMS/WhatsApp messages
- Booking link with slots
- Recovery dashboard

*Later:* API integrations with popular garage software · Pay-later / split payment partner · Seasonal campaigns (winter tyres) · Google review requests after the job · Multi-site reports

### Pricing

- **Basic: €49/mo:** Up to 150 follow-ups a month
- **Busy: €99/mo:** Up to 500 follow-ups, booking link, dashboard
- **Group: €149/mo:** Several sites, integrations

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| SMS / WhatsApp + AI | Messages billed per use, passed on | €20–60 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–800** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Garage management systems (Garage Desk, EZ Garage and others) | Jobs, invoices, digital inspections, MOT reminders | Declined work is stored but rarely chased in a smart way |
| Bumper | Pay-later finance for repairs, with workshop tools | Finance-led; garages may want follow-up without finance |
| CitNOW, autoVHC | Video and health checks for dealers | Dealer-focused and priced for franchised networks |
| **Us** | Declined item → timed personal follow-up → booking | Simple add-on for independents, works next to any garage software |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Jonatan visits 15 garages and asks for last month's declined-work list; we send follow-ups by hand for 2 garages.
- **Week 3–5:** Import, rules, message drafting and sending.
- **Week 6–7:** Booking link, dashboard, mobile entry.
- **Week 8:** Show recovered revenue to the pilots; convert to paid.

### Risks

- **Garage software adds the same feature:** Be the specialist that works with any system; partner with GMS vendors as an add-on.
- **Messages feel like spam:** Limit to 2–3 messages per item, use the garage's name and real photos, easy opt-out (GDPR/PECR).
- **Getting data out of old systems:** Start with CSV/email reports; mobile entry as fallback.
- **Most evidence is from dealers and vendors:** Measure recovery in the 2 hand-run pilots before building.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How much red and amber work did customers decline last month?
2. What happens to declined work today?
3. Does your garage software send any follow-up for it?
4. If we brought back 3–5 jobs a week, what would that be worth to you?
5. Would you pay €49–99 a month for that, next to your current software?

## 45. ValueLog: social value tracking and reports for small public-sector suppliers

**Public sector** · £49–199 / month · score **41/70** · competition: medium

> "We promised the council 2 apprentices and 200 volunteering hours. Now they want quarterly evidence in their portal and I have no idea where it is."

UK public contracts now weigh "social value" (local jobs, apprentices, volunteering, carbon) and ask suppliers to report against promised KPIs. Small facility, cleaning and construction suppliers track this in spreadsheets. ValueLog collects the evidence from staff and records, and produces the quarterly report each council wants.

**Why now:** The Procurement Act 2023 went live in February 2025, with more transparency on contract KPIs. In 2026 the government consulted on further reforms that put more weight on skills and jobs. SMEs say social value is an admin burden, and the number of buyer reporting platforms keeps growing.

### The problem

**David runs a cleaning and facilities company with 60 staff** that holds contracts with two councils and an NHS trust. To win them, his bid writer promised social value: **local hires, an apprentice, school visits and volunteering hours**, plus carbon reduction. Each buyer now asks for quarterly evidence, but in **different formats and portals**.

His office manager spends two days each quarter chasing supervisors for photos, timesheets and names, then filling in three different templates. If the numbers are low, the KPI may be published under the new procurement rules, and it could hurt the next bid. He needs one place where staff log activities as they happen and reports come out ready.

### Evidence

- 76% of respondents to techUK's 2023 SME survey said addressing social value is an administrative burden [techUK]
- Under the Procurement Act, social value KPIs can be published, exposing shortfalls; some organisations hold back from publishing while they test what they can deliver [Social Value Portal]
- Local authorities and suppliers say a growing number of reporting platforms makes consistent social value data harder [Socitm]
- Social Value Portal does not publish supplier pricing; its Social Value Academy training costs £375 one-off [G2 / FitGap]
- A buyer-side platform on G-Cloud charges public buyers £8,000 a year for up to 75 contracts, with free supplier access [GOV.UK Digital Marketplace]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** UK SMEs (10–250 staff) holding public contracts: facility services, cleaning, security, construction, maintenance, catering, IT services.
- **Buyer:** managing director, bid manager or office manager.
- **Start market:** UK; later Ireland and EU countries where green and social criteria in public procurement are growing.

### How it works

1. **Load the promises:** upload the bid answers and contract KPIs; AI turns them into a list of measurable targets per contract.
2. **Log as it happens:** supervisors log activities from their phone (apprentice started, school visit, volunteering hours) with photos and names.
3. **Pull in data:** import payroll postcodes for local-hire rates, training records and fuel/energy data for carbon.
4. **Track progress:** a dashboard shows each KPI against target, with warnings before the quarter ends.
5. **Report in their format:** export each buyer's template or TOMs-style report, plus case studies written by AI from logged activities, ready for the next bid.

### MVP (first version)

- Contracts and KPI targets (AI from bid text)
- Mobile activity logging with photos
- CSV import for staff postcodes and training
- KPI dashboard and quarter-end warnings
- Excel/PDF report export
- AI case-study writer

*Later:* Carbon reduction plan module · Templates for the most common buyer portals · Bid library: reuse evidence in new tenders · Subcontractor data collection · Ireland and EU frameworks

### Pricing

- **One contract: £49/mo:** 1 contract, mobile logging, reports
- **Supplier: £119/mo:** Up to 5 contracts, imports, case studies
- **Bid-ready: £199/mo:** Unlimited contracts, bid evidence library

### Costs

|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| AI API | KPI extraction and case studies | €10–30 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–750** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Social Value Portal (TOMs) | Measurement framework and platform | Often buyer-led; supplier membership pricing not published |
| whatimpact, Thrive, Loop | Buyer-side social value platforms | Councils pay; suppliers still collect evidence by hand |
| Bid writers and consultants | Write social value answers | Help win, not deliver and report |
| Spreadsheets | Most SMEs today | Two days per quarter, lost evidence |
| **Us** | Supplier-side evidence logging and reports | Built for SMEs with several contracts, £49–199/mo |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Jonatan interviews 15 facility and construction suppliers with public contracts; collect 5 real reporting templates.
- **Week 3–5:** Contracts, KPI extraction, mobile logging.
- **Week 6–7:** Dashboard, imports, report export and case-study writer.
- **Week 8:** 3 suppliers produce a real quarterly report in ValueLog.

### Risks

## 46. ProtectReady: Martyn's Law procedures and training records for standard-tier venues

**Events & venues** · £19–99 / month · score **40/70** · competition: medium

> "We run a 350-capacity music venue with casual staff who change every month. How do I prove they all know what to do if something happens?"

The UK's Martyn's Law (Terrorism (Protection of Premises) Act 2025) is expected to apply from spring 2027 to venues and events for 200+ people. Standard-tier venues must notify the regulator and have public protection procedures that staff know. ProtectReady writes the procedures from a short interview, trains staff with short lessons and keeps the records.

**Why now:** The Act received Royal Assent in April 2025 with at least a 24-month implementation period, so duties are expected to apply from spring 2027. The Home Office published statutory guidance on 15 April 2026 and the SIA consulted on its own guidance until June 2026, so venues are preparing now.

### The problem

**Sam manages a 350-capacity music venue and bar in Leeds.** Under Martyn's Law, his venue falls into the **standard tier**. Before the law switches on, he must notify the regulator (the SIA) and have **public protection procedures**: how to evacuate, invacuate, lock down and tell people what is happening, and staff must know them.

His team is 25 people, mostly part-time students who change every term. A one-off training session will be out of date by the summer. He wants a simple plan for his building, a 10-minute phone lesson for each new starter, and a record that shows who has done it, in case the SIA asks.

### Evidence

- The 2024 Home Office impact assessment counted about 178,900 sites in scope [GOV.UK impact assessment]
- Standard tier covers premises for 200–799 people; enhanced tier 800+ [Home Office myth-buster]
- Estimated cost of about £330 a year for a standard-tier premises (mostly staff time) and £5,210 for enhanced tier [Home Office myth-buster]
- Statutory guidance published 15 April 2026; SIA consultation on its guidance open until 12 June 2026 [Bevan Brittan / Keoghs]
- Duties expected to come into force from early April 2027 [Travers Smith]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** standard-tier venues (200–799 capacity) and multi-site operators: pub groups, music venues, leisure centres, places of worship, visitor attractions; later small event organisers.
- **Buyer:** venue manager, operations manager or owner of a small group.
- **Start market:** England, Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland (the Act covers the whole UK).

### How it works

1. **Venue interview:** a 15-minute guided interview about exits, capacity, staff roles and layout.
2. **Procedures drafted:** AI drafts evacuation, invacuation, lockdown and communication procedures in plain language, following the statutory guidance.
3. **Staff micro-lessons:** each staff member gets a 10-minute phone lesson and quiz, sent automatically to new starters.
4. **Records and reminders:** who is trained, refresh dates, drills done and procedure reviews, all with timestamps.
5. **SIA-ready pack:** one-click PDF with notification details, procedures and training records.

### MVP (first version)

- Venue profile interview
- AI-drafted procedures with review
- Staff invite link and micro-lessons
- Quiz and training records
- Drill log and refresh reminders
- PDF compliance pack

*Later:* Enhanced-tier plan module · Event (temporary site) version · Multi-site dashboard for groups · Rota integration for new starters · Welsh and other languages

### Pricing

- **Venue: £19/mo:** 1 site, procedures, up to 30 staff
- **Busy venue: £39/mo:** Up to 100 staff, drill log
- **Group: £99/mo:** Up to 10 sites, dashboard

### Costs

| D4H, Noggin, Vatix | Crisis and risk management platforms | Enterprise tools; heavy for a 300-capacity pub |
| Free ProtectUK / SIA guidance and checklists | Official templates | Free, but no staff training records or reminders |
| Security consultants and training firms | One-off plans and courses | Out of date when staff change |
| **Us** | Interview → procedures → staff lessons → records | Cheap, built for casual staff turnover |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Interview 20 venue managers; write 3 procedure packs by hand with Claude from the statutory guidance.
- **Week 3–5:** Interview flow, procedure drafting, staff lessons.
- **Week 6–7:** Records, reminders, PDF pack.
- **Week 8:** Pilot with 5 venues and one small pub group.

### Risks

- **Low willingness to pay for the standard tier:** Government estimates only £330/year of cost; price low and sell to groups with many sites.
- **Free official templates:** Our value is training records and new-starter automation, not the template.
- **Date slips:** Guidance is final; the date is set by regulations, expected spring 2027.
- **UK-only:** Fast, focused win; training-record engine reuses for other safety duties.

### Questions to ask customers

1. What capacity is your venue, and do you know which tier you are in?
2. What have you done so far to prepare for Martyn's Law?
3. How do you train new staff on emergencies today, and how do you record it?
4. How many sites and staff do you manage?
5. Would £19–39 a month per site for procedures plus training records be worth it?

## 47. PayFair: EU pay-transparency kit for companies with 20–250 staff

**HR / Recruiting** · €49–199 / month · score **40/70** · competition: medium

> "An employee asked for the average pay of her role by gender. We have 40 staff and no HR system. What are we supposed to send?"

The EU Pay Transparency Directive gives every worker the right to ask about pay levels, bans questions about salary history and requires pay ranges for candidates. Big compensation tools cost thousands a year. PayFair helps small employers set job levels and pay ranges, answer pay-information requests on time and keep job ads compliant.

**Why now:** The transposition deadline was 7 June 2026. Only a few member states had fully transposed by then, but Poland, Belgium, Czechia and Malta have partial rules in force and others are drafting. Rules on candidates and the right to information apply to employers of all sizes, while big tools are priced for companies with 250+ staff.

### The problem

**Marta is the office manager of a 45-person engineering firm in Warsaw.** Poland's first pay-transparency rules took effect in December 2025: candidates must be told the pay or pay range, job adverts must be gender-neutral, and the firm can no longer ask about past salaries. When the full directive is transposed, any employee can ask for **average pay levels by gender for their category of work**, with a deadline to answer.

The firm has never defined job levels. Salaries were set one by one. A compensation platform quoted **several thousand euros a year**, far too much. Marta needs a simple way to group roles, set ranges, check job ads and produce an answer to a pay request in an afternoon.

### Evidence

- Only four member states (Italy, Slovakia, Lithuania, Malta) had fully transposed the directive by late June 2026; Sweden sought a postponement [WageIndicator / King & Spalding]
- Poland's recruitment rules took effect on 24 December 2025: pay information for candidates, gender-neutral adverts and no questions on past earnings [Lewis Silkin / RSM Poland]
- Employers must answer pay-information requests within two months under the directive; Poland gives 30 days [Trusaic]
- Ravio's pricing page gives £5,400 a year as an example for a 250-person company [Ravio]
- Lattice charges $6 per user for compensation with a $4,000 annual minimum [FirstHR]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** employers with 20–250 staff in EU countries that have transposed (Poland, Belgium, Czechia, Malta, Italy, Slovakia, Lithuania first), plus small recruiting agencies.
- **Buyer:** CEO, CFO, office manager or the single HR person.
- **Start market:** Poland and other early countries in English, with local-language document outputs; more countries as laws pass.

### How it works

1. **Import salaries:** upload a payroll export (CSV); names can be replaced by IDs.
2. **Group roles:** AI suggests categories of "work of equal value" from job titles and short descriptions, using skills, effort, responsibility and working conditions; the owner adjusts.
3. **Set ranges:** PayFair suggests a pay range per level and flags people outside it and gender gaps per category.
4. **Answer requests:** when an employee asks, PayFair produces the answer letter with the right averages and tracks the deadline.
5. **Check job ads:** paste a job advert to check pay range, neutral wording and banned questions for that country.

### MVP (first version)

- Payroll CSV import with pseudonymisation
- AI-assisted job grouping
- Pay ranges and outlier/gap view
- Pay-information request letters with deadline tracking
- Job-ad checker for 2–3 countries
- Annual employee notice template

*Later:* Gender pay-gap report for 100+ employers · Integrations with HR/payroll systems · Market salary benchmarks · Recruiting agency multi-client mode · More countries

### Pricing

- **Small: €49/mo:** Up to 50 staff, ranges, requests, ad checker
- **Medium: €119/mo:** Up to 150 staff, gap view, 3 users
- **Agency: €199/mo:** Recruiting agencies, unlimited job-ad checks for clients

### Costs

| Item | Note | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Domain | .com or .io | €15–40 / year |
| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| AI API | Job grouping and ad checks | €10–30 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |
| Stripe | Only a % of payments | ~3% |
| Claude Code / AI coding tools | Victor builds faster with AI (subscription) | €100–150 / month |
| **First 3 months** | | **≈ €450–750** |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Ravio | Compensation benchmarking and pay equity | About £5,400/year for 250 staff; built for scale-ups |
| Figures, PayAnalytics, Syndio, Trusaic | Pay transparency and pay equity software | Mid-size and enterprise focus, quote-based |
| HR systems (Personio, HiBob, Lattice) | HR suites adding comp modules | Need the full HR suite; minimum contracts |
| Employment lawyers | Advice per case | Expensive for every request |
| **Us** | Job levels + ranges + request letters + ad checks | €49–199/mo, built for 20–250 staff |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Interview 15 SMEs and 5 recruiters in Poland and one more early country; build one pay structure by hand.
- **Week 3–5:** Import, job grouping, ranges and gap view.
- **Week 6–8:** Request letters, job-ad checker, country rule packs.

### Risks

- **Countries keep delaying:** Start only where rules are in force (Poland first); the earlier Top 25 report dropped this idea for this reason, so validate demand before building.
- **Legal accuracy per country:** Partner with one employment law firm per country for rule packs; clear disclaimers.
- **HR suites add the same features:** Target firms without an HR suite and recruiting agencies.
- **Salary data is sensitive:** Pseudonymise on upload, EU hosting, strict access roles.

### Questions to ask customers

1. Do you have defined job levels and pay ranges today?
2. Have employees or candidates asked about pay ranges since the new rules?
3. Who would answer a pay-information request, and how?
4. Have you looked at any pay-transparency tool? What did it cost?
5. Would €49–119 a month for a simple kit be worth it?

## 48. RegisterReady: landlord database registration and certificate tracking for small letting agents

**Real estate** · £29–149 / month · score **39/70** · competition: high

> "We manage 180 properties for 90 landlords. Half the gas certificates are in someone's email. Now every one has to go on a government register."

England's new landlord database ("Register your rental property") opens region by region from 15 December 2026, and every rented home must be registered by November 2027 with safety information. Small letting agents and landlords with a few properties must collect and keep this data current. RegisterReady reads certificates, finds gaps and reminds before anything expires.

**Why now:** The PRS database launches on 15 December 2026 in the West Midlands and rolls out across England over 12 months; all private rented properties must be registered by 14 November 2027, with an annual fee. Tenancy reforms under the Renters' Rights Act already started on 1 May 2026, and councils have stronger enforcement powers.

### The problem

**Lucy runs a small letting agency in Birmingham** that manages **180 homes for 90 landlords**. The West Midlands is the first region where the new database opens, on 15 December 2026, and existing landlords there have **three months** to register. Each entry needs landlord and property details plus safety information: the **gas safety certificate, electrical report (EICR) and EPC**.

Her CRM holds the tenancy data, but certificates sit in emails and shared folders, and some landlords handle their own gas checks. Giving false information or letting entries lapse can lead to Rent Repayment Orders. She needs a tool that reads every certificate, shows what is missing or expiring and prepares the data for each registration.

### Evidence

- The database ("Register your rental property") starts on 15 December 2026 in the West Midlands, then other regions over 12 months [HCR Law / Bevan Brittan]
- Landlords generally get three months after their region opens; all properties must be registered by 14 November 2027 [Bevan Brittan]
- Registration will be mandatory with an annual fee still to be confirmed, and will include gas, electrical and EPC information [JLL]
- False or misleading information, or failing to keep entries active, can lead to a Rent Repayment Order [Womble Bond Dickinson]
- Gas safety checks are needed every 12 months and EICRs every five years [council guidance, e.g. Derby City Council]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** small letting agents (50–1,000 managed homes) and portfolio landlords with 5–50 properties in England.
- **Buyer:** agency owner, lettings manager or the landlord.
- **Start market:** West Midlands first (opens first), then each region as it opens; Wales and Scotland have their own registers (later).

### How it works

1. **Bulk upload:** drop in folders or forward emails with certificates; AI reads property address, type, engineer, date and expiry.
2. **Match to properties:** certificates are matched to the property list from the CRM export.
3. **Gap report:** shows which homes miss a gas certificate, EICR or EPC, or have one expiring soon.
4. **Chase:** sends landlords and contractors a link to upload what is missing.
5. **Registration pack:** prepares the data for each property's registration and reminds when entries need updating.

### MVP (first version)

- Property list import (CSV)
- Bulk certificate upload and email forwarding
- AI extraction of certificate data
- Gap and expiry dashboard
- Chase links for landlords and contractors
- Registration data export per property

*Later:* Integrations with letting CRMs · Direct submission if the government offers an API · Deposit and right-to-rent documents · Decent Homes / Awaab's Law evidence · Wales and Scotland registers

### Pricing

- **Landlord: £29/mo:** Up to 20 properties
- **Agency: £79/mo:** Up to 300 properties, chase links
- **Agency Plus: £149/mo:** Up to 1,000 properties, multiple users

### Costs

|---|---|---|

| Server + database | EU hosting (Hetzner / Supabase) | €10–25 / month |
| AI + OCR API | Certificate reading | €15–40 / month |
| File storage | Certificates | €5–10 / month |
| Email / SMS sending | Resend / Postmark low tiers | €0–15 / month |
| Landing page | Built by us | €0 |

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |

| Spreadsheets and admin staff | Today's default | Slow for hundreds of homes in a 3-month window |
| **Us** | Read certificates → gaps → chase → registration pack | Narrow focus on the register deadline, works next to any CRM |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Call 20 West Midlands agents; process one agent's certificate folder by hand with Claude.
- **Week 3–5:** Import, certificate reading, matching, dashboard.
- **Week 6–7:** Chase links, registration export, reminders.
- **Week 8:** 3 pilot agencies prepare real registrations.

### Risks

- **CRMs add the feature:** Target agents on older CRMs and landlords without one; integrate later.
- **UK-only, deadline then slower:** After registration, keep customers with expiry tracking and new duties (Decent Homes).

2. How will you prepare for the landlord database in your region?

## 49. SCR Desk: single central record and safer-recruitment checks for small independent schools

**Education** · £39–149 / month · score **39/70** · competition: medium

> "The inspector opened our SCR in the first hour. Two references were missing dates and a governor's check wasn't recorded. That set the tone for two days."

Every school in England must keep a Single Central Record (SCR) of safeguarding checks on staff, volunteers and, in independent schools, the proprietor body. Inspectors check it early, and errors are a common avoidable finding. SCR Desk collects the evidence, checks each entry and chases missing references and DBS checks.

**Why now:** Keeping Children Safe in Education is updated every September and inspectors (Ofsted and ISI) look at the SCR early in every inspection. Councils now sell "SCR health checks" to schools, which shows the need. AI can read DBS certificates, references and ID documents to fill entries and spot missing fields.

### The problem

**Helen is the bursar of a 180-pupil independent prep school.** She keeps the Single Central Record in a large spreadsheet: identity, right to work, DBS, barred list, prohibition checks, overseas checks, qualifications and **section 128 checks for governors and managers**. Supply staff, coaches and peripatetic music teachers come and go every term.

Before the last inspection she spent **three evenings** checking every row against the paper files and found gaps: a reference with no date, a DBS update check not recorded. She wants a tool that stores the evidence with each check, shows red flags instantly and chases referees and staff automatically.

### Evidence

- The SCR is described as one of the first things inspectors review and one of the most common sources of avoidable findings [MHR]
- Independent schools must also record section 128 checks for management and checks on all members of the proprietor body [High Speed Training]
- There is no set format; the SCR can be paper or electronic, so many schools use spreadsheets [High Speed Training]
- Councils such as Worcestershire sell SCR health checks to schools [Worcestershire County Council]
- SCR modules exist inside larger HR/MIS products (MHR, Access, iSAMS, Veracross) without public prices [vendor pages]
- No published count of SCR failures was found; this must be validated with bursars [own research]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** small independent schools, nurseries, special schools and tutoring/education providers in England (later Wales, Scotland and British international schools abroad).
- **Buyer:** bursar, head or designated safeguarding lead.
- **Start market:** independent schools in England, then British international schools worldwide (they follow similar standards).

### How it works

1. **Import the current SCR:** upload the spreadsheet; SCR Desk maps columns and flags empty or invalid fields.
2. **Attach evidence:** upload DBS certificates, IDs and references; AI fills dates and numbers and links the document.
3. **Check rules:** required checks by role (teacher, volunteer, governor, contractor) with red/amber/green status.
4. **Chase:** automatic emails to referees and new staff for missing documents.
5. **Inspection view:** a clean SCR export and an audit trail of who checked what and when.

### MVP (first version)

- SCR spreadsheet import and mapping
- Role-based check rules
- Evidence upload with AI field extraction
- RAG status dashboard
- Referee and staff chase emails
- Inspection-ready export

*Later:* DBS update service checks · Safer recruitment workflow (online searches, interview notes) · Training records (safeguarding, Prevent) · MIS integrations · International schools version

### Pricing

- **Small school: £39/mo:** Up to 60 people on the SCR
- **School: £79/mo:** Up to 200 people, chase emails
- **Group: £149/mo:** Several schools or nurseries

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |

## 50. GrantPack: grant, grid-application and handover paperwork for one- to five-person heat-pump and solar installers

**Energy / installers** · £39–129 / month · score **35/70** · competition: high

> "I'm an installer, not an admin. Every heat pump means a grant claim, an MCS pack and sometimes a G99 that bounces back for one missing drawing."

Small UK heat-pump and solar installers carry the paperwork for the Boiler Upgrade Scheme grant, grid (DNO) applications and MCS certification for every job. Full installer CRMs cost hundreds of pounds a month. GrantPack is a light tool that turns survey notes and photos into the grant claim, the G98/G99 application and the customer handover pack.

**Why now:** Ofgem reworked the Boiler Upgrade Scheme on 28 April 2026 and updated installer guidance again in October 2026 (V5.2). MCS is moving installers to its redeveloped scheme through 2026–2027. Heat-pump installs fell 22% in H1 2026, so small installers are watching every cost and hour.

### The problem

**Gareth is a heat-pump installer with one apprentice and a part-time admin.** For each job he must show the **£7,500 grant deducted on the quote**, raise the Boiler Upgrade Scheme voucher, collect the customer's confirmation, complete MCS commissioning records and give a handover pack. If the job includes a battery or bigger inverter, he may also need a **G99 grid application**, which only starts the 45-working-day clock once it is complete.

He looked at installer CRMs, but the full platforms cost **£199 a month and more** and want to run his whole business. He only wants the paperwork done right first time from his survey notes and photos.

### Evidence

- The BUS is installer-led: the installer handles the grant paperwork and carries the cash-flow gap until redemption [Gas Engineer Software / Reonic]
- The scheme was reworked on 28 April 2026; installers must show the grant deducted upfront on the quote [autarc]
- Ofgem published a V5.2 summary of updates to BUS installer guidance in October 2026 [Ofgem]
- G99: the 45-working-day DNO clock only starts once the application is complete; missing data is a common cause of delay [Payaca / Reonic]
- 20,895 retrofit heat-pump installs in H1 2026, down 22% on H1 2025 [Payaca]
- Payaca Growth costs £1,199/month; autarc starts at £199/month for 2 users (per Payaca's comparison) [Payaca]

### Who pays

- **Customer:** MCS-certified heat-pump, solar and battery installers with 1–5 staff, and plumbers adding heat pumps.
- **Buyer:** owner-installer or office admin.
- **Start market:** UK; later Ireland (SEAI grants) and other EU grant schemes.

### How it works

1. **Survey to job:** upload survey notes, photos and product choices; AI fills the job record.
2. **Grant claim:** checks eligibility rules and drafts the BUS quote wording and voucher data.
3. **Grid check:** decides G98 vs G99 from export capacity and drafts the application with the required data and drawings list.
4. **Handover pack:** commissioning records, warranties and customer documents in one PDF.
5. **Rule updates:** we track Ofgem, MCS and DNO changes and update checklists.

### MVP (first version)

- Job record from survey notes and photos
- BUS eligibility checklist and quote wording
- G98/G99 decision and application draft
- Handover pack PDF
- Rule-change checklist updates
- Simple job list

*Later:* Direct DNO portal submission · Heat-loss calculation import · Ireland SEAI grants · Payments and deposits · Accounting integrations

### Pricing

- **Solo: £39/mo:** Up to 8 jobs a month
- **Team: £79/mo:** Up to 25 jobs, grid applications
- **Busy: £129/mo:** Unlimited jobs, 5 users

### Competitors and the gap

| Who | What they do | Gap |
|---|---|---|
| Payaca | Full installer platform | Growth plan £1,199/month; built for established businesses |
| autarc | Heat-pump and solar installer software | From £199/month for 2 users (per Payaca); still a full CRM |
| Reonic, Zigaflow, Gas Engineer Software | Installer CRMs with grant and MCS features | Feature-rich; less focused on the 1–5 person firm |
| **Us** | Paperwork only: grant, grid, handover | Cheaper and lighter, but must beat well-funded tools |

### Plan

- **Week 0–2:** **Validate, no code.** Interview 15 small installers; do 3 jobs' paperwork by hand with Claude.
- **Week 3–5:** Job record, BUS checklist, handover pack.
- **Week 6–8:** G98/G99 drafting, rule updates, pilot with 3 installers.

### Risks

- **Crowded, funded competition:** Only pursue if interviews show small installers refuse full CRMs; otherwise drop the idea.
- **Fewer installs in 2026:** Fewer jobs means price sensitivity; keep the price per job low.
- **Rules change often:** Rules in config; a monthly rule-update routine.
- **UK-only at first:** Ireland and EU grant schemes are later options.

### Questions to ask customers

1. How many BUS jobs did you do last quarter, and how long does the paperwork take per job?
2. Have you had a grant claim or G99 application rejected or delayed? Why?
3. What software do you use today, and what does it cost?
4. Would you use a paperwork-only tool next to your current tools?
5. Would £39–79 a month be worth it?
